# 2 · The wireframe — every screen in both applications

**This absorbs `Caprebate — Wireframe & Build Spec.html` v5 and keeps its
discipline**: route, fields, what it writes, what it calls, **what it must never
do**, and **an acceptance test each**. The old page covered S0–S13 and X1–X4 for
screens that did not exist. These exist, so a route here is a route you can open,
and an acceptance test is one you can run.

**Three rules apply to every handler and are not repeated per screen:**

1. **Authorise on the object, on every request.** Handlers do not branch on a role — they fetch the row and hand it to `authorise`, which is why an id swapped in a URL returns a refusal rather than a page.
2. **No static file route.** Nothing maps a request path onto the filesystem; the only asset is a string constant, and `assertNoStaticFileRoute` reads the router's own source to keep that true. That is the first half of requirement **DB-W1**.
3. **Escape on output.** Every response is built with the `h` tagged template, which escapes anything not already `Safe`. There is no string concatenation of markup anywhere.

**The layout every screen below sits in — Phase 133a, 17 September 2026.** Lakhbir asked for a CRM that "looks and works
like Salesforce — easy to use, intuitive, an easy layout for each one of the logins". What was taken is the layout
pattern, never a brand: no other product's name, logo, icons, fonts or stylesheet appear. The specification the build
followed is `04-phase-work/phase133a/layout-spec.md`, and the before-and-after audit is `04-phase-work/phase133a/ui-audit.md`.

- **The shell** (`page()` in `src/web/html.ts`) draws a header — the name, the global search (`B41`), Help and a user
  menu (**Your account**, **Sign out**) — and then **the tabs of the signed-in login**. Since Phase 135 the count of my
  open tasks from `myWork` is drawn on the **Tasks** tab (`.tabcount`, `.late` when one is overdue), not as a second
  link in the header. It is still the only place a page
  is assembled, and the Content-Security-Policy string is unchanged.
- **Phase 135 narrowed this further: a menu is a login's daily work, named in `MENUS` in `src/web/navigation.ts`** — see
  *The menus* below. What follows is still true of every section a menu lists.
- **The menu shows only what a login can open** (`src/web/navigation.ts`). This REVERSES the earlier decision in
  `src/web/staff.ts` to draw every section for every role, on Lakhbir's instruction. It asks no rule of its own: each
  section names its **gate** — the function its route's store calls first, exported beside the store as
  `searchGate`, `pipelineBoardGate`, `claimListGate`, `documentCentreGate`, `documentTypesGate`, `rewardModelGate`,
  `automationGate`, `templatesGate`, `auditLogGate`, `usersGate` and `nextActionsGate`, or the module's existing
  `assert…Section`. `test/phase133a.test.ts` opens every header link for every login (200) and every section not in it
  (403). **A screen a login may not open is still refused, not hidden**: the address answers 403.
- **The header needs the database, the clock and the address**, which `nav(actor)` was never given. The router runs
  every `/staff` and `/portal` request inside `navContext` (an `AsyncLocalStorage`) rather than threading three more
  arguments through sixty page functions.
- **The Cap Rebate brand, and a CRM a new user can pick up cold — Phase 133c, the same day.** From the brand book v1.0
  (Lakhbir's file; its text and logos are in `04-phase-work/phase133c/brand-assets/`):
  - **Colour tokens** in `src/web/assets.ts` (`BRAND_TOKENS`): Cap Mint `#ABEDD8`, Cap Ink `#111111`, white, Mist
    `#F2F8F6`, Body Grey `#333333`, Muted `#6B7280` (captions on white only). **`--accent` is one token, set to Cap Ink**
    until the design team supplies the accent — a one-line change. **White text on mint is refused when the module
    loads** (`assertNoWhiteOnMint`), and `TEXT_PAIRINGS` lists every text/ground pairing, each checked at 4.5:1.
  - **Type**: headings `"Bookman Old Style","Roboto Slab",Georgia,"Times New Roman",serif`, no italics; body
    `Calibri,"Segoe UI",Inter,Arial,Helvetica,sans-serif` at 16 px/1.6; the slide-36 scale (H1 44, H2 32, H3 24 for card
    titles, H4 20, H5 18 for form labels, H6 16 for table headings, caption 12); `tabular-nums` on the body. System
    fonts only — the CSP is unchanged.
  - **The name a person reads is "Cap Rebate"** — page titles, the header, the footer, the portal, emails. Code
    identifiers, file names, the database and the project's documents keep "Caprebate". Words quoted verbatim from the
    operating procedure (`src/web/stages.ts`) keep it too, and are shown through `brandName()`.
  - **The logo** is the supplied PNG (`GET /assets/cap-rebate-logo.png`), at 120 px on the sign-in page, the public pages
    and the portal. The back office shows the name in the heading font: no horizontal lockup or icon-only mark exists.
  - **UK dates, one helper** (`src/web/localtime.ts`): `ukDate` "24 Sep 2026", `localStamp` "24 Sep 2026, 12:32" (with a
    clock, "Today, 12:32" / "Yesterday, 12:32"), `localWhen`, `dueReading`/`dueWords` "Due in 3 days (24 Sep 2026)" / "3
    days overdue (21 Sep 2026)". **`page()` passes the body's text through `ukDatesInMarkup`**: text between tags only,
    never an attribute, an input's value, `<code>`, `<pre>` or `<textarea>`. Stored values stay ISO.
  - **The next step first** (`src/web/next-step.ts`): a claim's right-hand panel opens with "Move to <stage>" as a button,
    or "Not ready yet", the first need in plain words (one table, `plainNeed`) and the action that fixes it. The gate is
    still read on the server; the ten stages are drawn once, in the stage bar; the gate table is behind "See everything
    this stage needs" (`#stage-needs`). A lead's panel does the same for the enquiry checks.
  - **Empty rows hidden** (`fields()` in `src/web/components.ts`): a field shows only with a value, with one "Add missing
    details" link; a "not known" answer that decides something (the enquiry checks, the claim's facts, jurisdiction,
    claimant type, prior claims) is kept. An explanation sits behind "Why?" (`why()`, a `<details>`).
  - **A refused form keeps what was typed** (`src/web/refill.ts`): `problem()` redraws the page the form was on under the
    same login (a captured GET), puts the posted values back into the form that posts to that address, puts the refusal
    at the top of the form (beside the field since Phase 133d, below), and opens its `<details>` and tab. Still 400. A form it cannot find gets the plain refusal
    with the typed values listed. Passwords are never written back.
  - **First visit** (`src/web/welcome.ts`): a welcome card on Home and the portal with the login's three most frequent
    jobs, until dismissed. **Help** in every header opens that screen's training section.
  - **Built, switched off, and asked**: `NAME_SWITCHES` in `src/web/navigation.ts` — `portalSteps`
    (`src/web/portal-steps.ts`: 1 Your estimate … 6 Aftercare). It ships **off**; `ServerOptions.names` sets it for a
    test. *The `menu` switch and its `MENU_NAMES` table were removed by Phase 135: "Introducer fees" is now the section's
    name, and the other two proposals no longer name a menu entry.*
  - **"The data this page was built from"** is drawn for Admin only (`dataLink()`); the JSON addresses answer as before.
  - `test/phase133c.test.ts` crawls every page each login reaches and fails on an exclamation mark, an avoid-list word,
    "Caprebate", an ISO date, a field reading "not recorded"/"not on hold"/"not lost", or a `<details>` in a `<p>`.
- **What Phase 133c left, and the estate outside the app — Phase 133d, the same day.** The record is
  `04-phase-work/phase133d/proof-record.md`.
  - **"(required)" in words on every required field** (`src/web/required.ts`): `page()` passes the body through
    `requiredWordsInMarkup` after `ukDatesInMarkup`. A control's label is found by `for`/`id`, then by the `<label>` it
    sits in, then by a `<label>` with no `for` ending right before it; the words go inside that label once.
    `test/phase133d.test.ts` crawls every page each login reaches and fails on a required control whose label does not
    say "(required)" (`requiredWithoutWords`).
  - **A refusal beside its field** (`src/web/refill.ts`, `besideField`). A refusal may carry `field`, the posted name it
    is about. `take()` in `src/web/tasks.ts`, `src/web/crm.ts` and `src/web/leads.ts` sets it from its own key, and the
    other checks of the ten most-used forms set it by name: a task, the next action, a lead, an enquiry check, a claim
    move (`refuse(…, field)` in `src/web/pipeline.ts`; the move form's next-action fields are `next_action_title`,
    `next_action_owner` and `next_action_due`), the claim, the organisation, a contact, a property and a document
    request (`createDocumentRequest`). `problem()` takes it as a sixth argument. The message (`p.fielderr`,
    `role="alert"`) is drawn straight after the field, or after the `<label>` holding it; the field gains
    `aria-invalid="true"` and `aria-describedby`. A refusal with no field, or one naming a field the form does not hold,
    stays at the top (`div.formerr`). The refusal's first letter is shown as a capital.
    **Phase 133e** carried `field` to every other back-office store that refuses about one posted field: finance
    (`src/web/invoices.ts`, `src/web/finance.ts`), introducers (`take()` in `src/web/introducers.ts`), the claim's facts,
    grants and past owners (`src/web/claim-facts.ts`, through `finish()` in `src/web/claim-record-pages.ts`), document
    types (`readTypeFields`, `createType` in `src/web/documents.ts`), automation rules (`ruleValues` in
    `src/web/automation.ts`), templates (`src/web/templates.ts`), admin settings (`src/web/configuration.ts`), the survey
    job (`src/web/surveyor.ts`), and account settings and Create a user (`SettingsError` in `src/web/settings.ts` now
    carries `field`, passed at the call sites in `src/web/staff.ts`). Every `*-pages.ts` module's `problem` signature
    takes the sixth argument. A refusal about the record's own state carries no field and stays at the top.
  - **Every required control is JOINED to its label** (Phase 133e). `requiredLabelledByPositionOnly` in
    `src/web/required.ts` reports a required control labelled only by a `<label>` that sits before it; `test/phase133d.test.ts`
    D5 crawls every page each login reaches and fails on one. The account settings, Create a user, the assessment's
    close form and the upload page now give each field an `id` and its label the matching `for`.
  - **The stage bar wraps**: a step's `min-width` is `max(5.2rem, min-content)`, so no step is narrower than its longest
    word and the arrow never cuts a label; the bar scrolls sideways only if the ten cannot fit.
  - **The avoid list is one module** (`src/web/voice.ts`, `AVOID` and `avoidHits`), read by `test/phase133c.test.ts`
    and by `03-app/caprebate-brand-check.cjs`. "capital gains" is allowed only where the text distinguishes it ("not
    capital gains tax"); "guaranteed-use" (a grant's term) is allowed; "tax scheme", "workaround" and "tax avoidance"
    are listed.
  - **The engagement letter names the adviser "Cap Rebate"**, twelve times, and nothing else in it changed (Lakhbir,
    17 September 2026: no "Ltd" and no company number until the legal entity is settled; settled 18 September 2026, and Phase 133g put CAP REBATE LTD and 17307313 into its parties and signature blocks). `test/phase133c.test.ts` no
    longer exempts the letter's clauses.
  - **Outside the application**: `Caprebate site.html`, `03-app/caprebate-notices-page.html` and
    `03-app/caprebate-complaint-form.html` use the same tokens and font stacks (read from `src/web/assets.ts`), no web
    font, and the name "Cap Rebate". The site shows the supplied logo top-left at 120 px as a `data:` URI (the page
    has no CSP of its own; the host checklist mentions no font), and converts ISO dates to UK dates in the assessment
    frame's drawn text (`CB_UKDATES`, a separate script the harness and the extractor never read).
    `node 03-app/caprebate-brand-check.cjs` checks all three, and `--self-test` plants thirteen faults.
- **The five long screens followed — Phase 133b, the same day.** The business page (`B6`), the survey job (`B8`) and
  finance (`B9`) draw `highlights`, `recordTabs` and `sidePanel` from `src/web/components.ts` with tab ids of their own;
  admin settings (`B43`) and document types (`B29`) draw `sectionList`, the same anchor-and-`:target` mechanism as a
  list down the left. A tab's id is any id unique on its page, so the stylesheet marks the current tab **by position**
  (`CURRENT_BY_POSITION` in `src/web/assets.ts`, up to `MAX_TABS`, which both components refuse to exceed). Every
  panel each screen drew is still drawn, from the same view, in a tab or a section; after a save the redirect names the
  tab or section as its fragment. `test/phase133b.test.ts` proves each screen's tabs for a login that opens it, that
  every tab lands on a panel on the page, and that no id is used twice. The record is `04-phase-work/phase133b/`.
- **A refused screen is a page.** `sendRefusalPage` in `src/web/router.ts`, called from `src/server.ts`, answers 403 with
  the login's own header and "You cannot open this page", naming no object. A data address (`.json`, `.csv`) and
  `/api/` keep `{"error":"not authorised"}`.
- **One set of components** (`src/web/components.ts`): badge, card, empty state, highlights panel, the claim's stage
  bar (it draws `pipeline.stage` and `pipeline.steps` from the claim view and decides nothing), the client's stage bar
  (the six client-facing steps only), record tabs, the right-hand panel and the record layout.
- **Record pages** (claim, organisation, property, lead) are highlights, the stage bar on a claim, three tabs —
  Details, Related, Activity — and a right-hand panel of the next action and quick actions. **Every panel the page drew
  before is still drawn**, in one of the tabs, from the same view.
- **Tabs, menus and the board need no script.** A tab is a link to its panel's anchor and the stylesheet shows the
  targeted panel (`:target`, `:has()`); without CSS, or without `:has()`, every panel shows in turn. The More menu and
  the user menu are `<details>`. The pipeline is an `<ol>` of stage columns, a plain list without CSS.
- **The home page per login — rows of tiles since Phase 136** (`homeRows` in `src/web/home.ts`) reads each row through
  the store its screen reads, and only for a login whose gate admits it. Sales, Admin and Read only: New leads, Existing
  leads — next actions, Today and overdue, Pipeline by stage; Admin then Users, Switches, Audit log and Refused sign-ins
  (which reads the audit log, so the reading is itself recorded). Accounting: its three money tiles, then Today and
  overdue. Surveying: its two job tiles, then Today and overdue. See `B0`.
- **The stylesheet** (`SITE_CSS` in `src/web/assets.ts`) is still the one asset, still a string constant, with no
  `url()`, `@import` or `@font-face`. Every class a screen used before is still defined. Text colours meet WCAG 2.2 AA
  and the test computes the ratios from the stylesheet's own tokens.

### The menus — which section each login works in, and the verdict — Phase 135, 21 September 2026

Lakhbir, 18 September 2026: *"Some of the tabs along the top seem to be replicating information - this needs to be
streamlined and not bloated"*, and *"some of the users have so much information under More - think if this is actually
needed for each user or not."* Measured before this phase (a signed-in crawl of `/staff` for each login): Sales had eight
tabs and **twelve** entries under More, Read only five and **thirteen**, Admin eight and sixteen.

**The rule from this phase on.** A login's menu lists the sections it does its daily job in — being allowed to open a
section is not a reason to list it. **More holds at most four entries, or it is not drawn** (until UI tidy phase 2, 2 October 2026, which removed More: every section now sits in a named group). The menu never grants or
removes a permission: every section keeps its own gate, `src/authz.ts` is untouched, and a section left off a menu still
opens by its address for whoever could open it before. `test/phase135.test.ts` asserts each login's exact menu.

| Section (address) | Who does their daily job there | Verdict | Why |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Home (`/staff`) | everyone | **KEEP** — first tab for every login | Where each login starts. Phase 136 turns it into tiles. |
| Dashboard (`/staff/dashboard`) | management, now and then | **MERGE INTO Home** | The same job as Home. Off every menu. **Phase 136:** its figures are Home's **Reports** tile (`/staff/reports`); the old address answers 303 to Home. |
| Leads (`/staff/leads`) | Sales, Admin; Read only reads it | **KEEP** — Sales, Admin, Read only | A new enquiry starts here. |
| Assessments (`/staff/assessments`) | Sales, Admin | **MERGE INTO Leads**, as a filter | A filled assessment is a lead. Leads shows "All leads · Filled assessments"; the assessments list keeps its address and its own gate, and the Leads tab stays marked on it. |
| Engaged clients (`/staff/clients`) | Sales, Admin | **MERGE** with Organisations into one **Clients** list | Two lists of the same companies, both opening the one client page (Phase 134). |
| Organisations (`/staff/organisations`) | Sales, Admin; Read only reads it | **MERGE INTO Clients** | The list address sends the reader to `/staff/clients`, after asking the same gate it always asked. |
| Clients (`/staff/clients`, the one list) | Sales, Admin, Accounting (Phase 151b); Read only reads it | **KEEP** — Sales, Admin, Read only; **and Accounting since Phase 151b**, as its billing view | Company name first; who it is waiting on, status, owner, claims. Gated as Organisations was (`organisation.read`), so Read only can open it; the "waiting on" column is drawn only for a login allowed the research (`research.read`), exactly as before. **Phase 151b (Word answer 40):** the tab's gate also admits `client_billing.read` (Admin and Accounting), and a login holding that and not `organisation.read` — Accounting — gets `src/web/billing.ts`'s view instead of the record: each client's name, who to chase and how to reach them, the claims waiting for an invoice, and the invoices with every chase. The client record stays refused to Accounting. |
| Pipeline (`/staff/pipeline`) | Sales, Admin; Read only reads it | **KEEP** — Sales, Admin, Read only | The second Pipeline row (`pipeline-sales`, the same address) is **removed**. |
| Properties (`/staff/properties`) | nobody daily | **OFF THE MENU** | Reached from the client page and from search. The address still opens. |
| Claims (`/staff/claims`) | Accounting | **KEEP for Accounting only; OFF THE MENU for the rest** | Sales, Admin and Read only reach a claim from the client page, the pipeline and search. **Accounting keeps Claims where the brief put Clients**: Accounting may not read the client record (`organisation.read` is Admin and Sales, settled 6 September 2026), and this phase does not change a permission. The question is in CAPREBATE QUESTIONS, "Should Accounting see the Clients list?". **Answered (Word answer 40) and built in Phase 151b:** Accounting now has Clients as well, as its billing view; Claims stays, because that is where the money on a claim is. |
| Tasks (`/staff/tasks`) | Sales, Accounting, Surveying, Admin | **KEEP** | The header's "N open tasks" link opened the same page, so the count now sits on the Tasks tab itself and the header link is gone. |
| Next actions (`/staff/next-actions`) | Sales, Admin | **MERGE INTO Tasks**, as a filter | Tasks shows "My tasks · Every open task · Claims with no next step"; the list keeps its address and gate, and the Tasks tab stays marked on it. |
| Introducers (`/staff/introducers`) | Sales | **KEEP** — Sales; under More for Admin | The firms that refer clients. |
| Reward model → **Introducer fees** (`/staff/introducer-rewards`) | Accounting settles fees; Admin sets the model | **MOVE TO SETTINGS** (Admin); under More for Accounting | Renamed "Introducer fees" (the switched-off rename, now on). Sales reaches it from the Introducers page. |
| Finance (`/staff/finance`) | Accounting, Admin | **KEEP** — Accounting, Admin | Invoices, fees and VAT. |
| Survey jobs (`/staff/survey`) | Surveying | **KEEP** — Surveying; under More for Admin | The review queue. |
| Calendar (`/staff/calendar`) | Sales, Admin | **KEEP under More** — Sales, Admin | Booked calls. Read only never books one. |
| Documents (`/staff/documents`) | nobody daily | **OFF THE MENU** | Documents belong to a client, and Phase 140 built the client's Files. Asked for one company (`?organisation={id}` alone) the address sends the reader to that company's files; otherwise it is the list across every client, reached from Search. |
| Users (`/staff/users`) | Admin | **MOVE TO SETTINGS** | |
| Admin settings → **Switches** (`/staff/admin-settings`) | Admin | **MOVE TO SETTINGS** | Sales and Read only may read it, but do not work there. |
| Document types (`/staff/document-types`) | Admin | **MOVE TO SETTINGS** | "Documents" and "Document types" side by side read as one thing twice. |
| Automation (`/staff/automation`) | Admin | **MOVE TO SETTINGS** | |
| Email templates (`/staff/templates`) | Admin | **MOVE TO SETTINGS** | |
| Compliance (`/staff/compliance`) | Admin | **MOVE TO SETTINGS** | |
| Audit log (`/staff/audit`) | Admin | **MOVE TO SETTINGS** | |
| Settings (`/staff/admin`, new) | Admin | **NEW** — the last tab for Admin | One page of eight tiles: Users, Switches, Document types, Automation, Email templates, Compliance, Audit log, Introducer fees. Each tile is drawn only where its section's gate admits the login. |
| Search (`/staff/search`) | everyone | **KEEP in the header** | Not a tab. Its box now reads "Search clients, claims, invoices…". |
| Your account (`/staff/settings`) | everyone | **KEEP in the user menu**, renamed from "Settings" | Two things called Settings would be the same kind of duplicate this phase removes. |

**The menus this gives — since UI tidy phase 2 (2 October 2026), a menu down the left in named groups, with no More; since Phase 160a (19 October 2026) a navy one, with the search, Help, the user menu and Admin's views in a top bar over the page; since Phase 160b the client portal's too.**
Lakhbir chose "design 2" (00-START-HERE/hub/UI-TIDY-PLAN.md). What was under More moved into its group: Admin's Calendar,
Introducers and Survey jobs, Sales's Calendar, Accounting's Introducer fees. No section joined or left any login's menu,
and every gate is as it was. `MENUS` in `src/web/navigation.ts` names each login's groups; the module refuses to load if
a menu names a section that does not exist or names one twice. `test/phase135.test.ts` and `test/ui-tidy-menu.test.ts`
assert each menu exactly.

| Login | (no name) | Winning clients | Clients and claims | Money | Admin |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Sales | Home, Tasks | Leads, Pipeline, Introducers | Clients, Calendar | — | — |
| Accounting | Home, Tasks | — | Clients, Claims | Finance, Introducer fees | — |
| Surveying | Home, Survey jobs, Tasks | — | — | — | — |
| Read only | Home | Leads, Pipeline | Clients | — | — |
| Admin | Home, Tasks | Leads, Pipeline, Introducers | Clients, Survey jobs, Calendar | Finance | Settings |

(Until 2 October 2026, tabs then More: Sales Home, Leads, Clients, Pipeline, Tasks, Introducers / Calendar; Accounting
Home, Clients, Claims, Finance, Tasks / Introducer fees; Surveying Home, Survey jobs, Tasks; Read only Home, Leads,
Clients, Pipeline; Admin Home, Leads, Clients, Pipeline, Tasks, Finance, Settings / Calendar, Introducers, Survey jobs.)


**How it is built — Phase 135.**
- `SECTIONS` lists each menu section once (the second Pipeline row is gone), each with its gate and `under` — the
  addresses that also mark its tab as current (`/staff/assessments` under Leads, `/staff/next-actions` under Tasks,
  `/staff/organisations` under Clients, the eight tile addresses under Settings). `MENUS` names each login's tabs and More
  by key; the module refuses to load if a More list exceeds `MORE_LIMIT` (4) or names a section that does not exist.
  `tabsFor` still draws a listed section only where its gate admits the login.
- **One tab, two views** (`filterBar`, `LEAD_FILTERS`, `TASK_FILTERS` in `src/web/navigation.ts`; class `.viewbar`): a
  row of links at the top of Leads and filled assessments, and of Tasks, every open task and the claims with no next
  step. Each view keeps its own address and gate, and a filter is drawn only where its gate admits the login.
- **The one Clients list** (`src/web/client-list.ts`, `B5`) and **Settings** (`src/web/admin-tiles.ts`, `B46`) are new
  modules. `GET /staff/organisations` asks `organisation.read` and answers **303 to `/staff/clients`**.
- **On a phone** a list drawn as `table.stack` becomes one card per row with each cell labelled from `data-label` (no
  sideways scroll); the Clients list is the first.
- **The demo's view-only login** is added by `tools/demo-server.ts` only (`readonly@example.invalid`, the seed's
  password). `src/seed.ts` still seeds one user per role and gives the view-only role to nobody.
- **Accept** `test/phase135.test.ts`: each login's exact menu, More at four or fewer, every back-office address answering
  each login as it did before the phase except the two changed on purpose, the one list, the two filtered tabs, the
  Settings tiles and the dashboard button. It fails on the pre-change copy (20 of its 25 tests); the record is
  `04-phase-work/phase135/`.

## 2.0 · How the old screen ids map onto the built ones

| Old wireframe | Now | Note |
|---|---|---|
| S0 | `/` | Unchanged in role |
| S1–S6 | `/assessment/Q1 … Q3` | **The seven-screen front door is superseded.** The live one is six screens: `Q1 · Q1a · Q1b · Q1c · Q2 · Q3` |
| S7 | `/assessment/figure` | A single "up to" ceiling with a per-property breakdown |
| X2 | `/assessment/no-claim` | The reasoned negative, reachable from the gate |
| S8a + S8a-1 | `/assessment/close` | The close **and** the slot picker, one screen |
| S8c | `/assessment/close` (POST response) | The thank-you is the close's own response, not a separate route |
| **JS-S8** | **NOT BUILT** | Self-serve account creation does not exist. **A client account is created by an ADMIN.** See §5 |
| JS-S8b | `/staff/clients/{id}` | Preparing the letter is back-office work on the business page |
| JS-S9 | `/portal/letter` + `/portal/letter/sign` | **In-session, not on a tokenised link.** See §5 |
| S10–S12 | `/staff/clients/{id}` panels 2 and 3 | Research and what is still missing |
| S13 | `/portal/documents` | The upload |
| X1 | portal task list + panel 3 | An unknown routed to capture |
| X3, X4 | engine output | Out-of-time terminals and the assumptions list travel with the figure |

---

## 2.1 · The client application

### `C0` — the public front page
- **Route** `GET /` · public, no session, no cookie beyond strictly necessary.
- **Writes** nothing. **Calls** nothing.
- **Must never** Fire an analytics event carrying an address or a typed value. Set a marketing cookie before consent is answered.
- **Accept** No request to any register or enrichment endpoint is made from this page. (Trivially true today: **the whole application makes zero external calls** — `grep 'fetch(' src/` returns nothing outbound.)

### `C1` — the assessment, six screens
- **Route** `GET /assessment` (start or resume) · `GET|POST /assessment/{Q1|Q1a|Q1b|Q1c|Q2|Q3}` · public.
- **Fields** `Q1` prior claim by them · `Q1a` prior claim by a previous owner · `Q1b` UK tax status, and **on *yes* only** the profit question · `Q1c` what prompted this · `Q2` **a per-property repeater** — address, sector, entity type, and the spend pair that property's trigger selects · `Q3` works, **one row per property**.
- **Writes** `assessment_drafts` only — the state and the screen reached, keyed on an httpOnly draft cookie. **No business row, no assessment row.**
- **Calls** the engine package, in process. `src/web/engine.ts` is **the only file that imports it** — one line to grep — and every gate, band, channel, figure and assumption comes from there. **Nothing in the web half decides a tax question.**
- **Must never** Render a trigger control or an entity control on **property 1**: it inherits both silently, and rendering them turns a six-screen funnel into an eight-screen one for the client who was never the drop-off risk. Ship `Q3`'s heading unconditionally — where that property's trigger was a refurbishment or a fit-out the heading must read *any **further** work since*, or the client enters the same money twice and the figure inflates. Terminate on *Not sure* at `Q1b`.
- **Accept** `SCREEN_IDS` equals `CB_FUNNEL.SCREENS` exactly, asserted by a test, so the order cannot drift. Every string in `SCREENS`, `OPTIONS` and `COSMETIC_NOTE` is transcribed from the site's own renderer — **ported, not improved**.

### `C2` — the figure
- **Route** `GET /assessment/figure` · public, draft required.
- **Shows** a single **"up to"** ceiling, its qualifier, and the per-property breakdown.
- **Must never** Store the ceiling as the value — **the engine computes and stores both bounds**, and a stored ceiling cannot be replayed after a switch moves. Sum ceilings across properties and present the total as one number without the breakdown: adding upper bounds gives the sum of maxima, which gets *less* achievable with every property added. Render the figure without the assumptions list — with the range gone, that list is the only guard left on the number.
- **Accept** The stored run reproduces the same **range** when replayed against the same engine version, and the displayed ceiling equals its upper bound.

### `C3` — the reasoned negative
- **Route** `GET /assessment/no-claim` · public, draft required — **and only a draft the engine has stopped**. Any other draft is sent back to the screen it was on.
- **Shows** the site's own reasoned-negative screen, ported: its heading, **the gate's reason in the engine's words** (`gateQ1b(state).message`), the note that later spend is a separate claim, then CB_BOOKING's gated-exit offer and *Talk to someone →* to the close.
- **Must never** Produce a figure, or route an unknown here or past here to keep a lead alive. **While a draft is stopped, `Q1c`, `Q2`, `Q3` and the figure redirect here**, on GET and POST alike; `Q1`, `Q1a` and `Q1b` stay open so the prospect can correct an answer.
- **Accept** The screen names the gate that fired, in words. *No* at Q1b lands here; *not sure* and no answer never do.

### `C4` — the close, and the thank-you
- **Route** `GET /assessment/close` (the form, with live slots) · `POST /assessment/close` (writes, then renders the thank-you) · public.
- **Fields** `full_name` (**required**) · `business_name` · `job_role` (thirteen values plus free text, and **free text is never a validation failure**) · `channel` (**required**, one of three) · `contact_value` · `sms_variant` where the channel is text · `contact_consent` (**required, and it is not marketing consent**) · `marketing_optin` (**unticked, separate, per channel**) · `slot` (**telephone only**).
- **Writes** `businesses`, `assessments`, one `assessment_answers` row per question, `estimates`, `contacts`, `bookings`. **The lead is written here.** **At a reasoned negative (UC-C9) it writes instead** a `leads` row with no organisation, and a `contacts` and a `bookings` row belonging to that lead — no `businesses`, `assessments` or `estimates` — under the gated-exit offer rather than the close's, and the thank-you prints no range and does not say an assessment was recorded.
- **Must never** Refuse a slot posted alongside email or text — it is **dropped**, because the client has simply left a radio button selected from before they changed channel. Render a cached availability grid: read live, hold on selection, release on abandonment. Ship the placeholder telephone number that is still in the thank-you copy (§5).
- **Accept** The screen cannot be passed without a name, a channel and a per-channel consent; the assessment persists here; a time never offered is refused **at the write**.

### `C5` — sign in
- **Route** `GET|POST /login`, `POST /logout` · public.
- **Writes** `sessions`; `rate_limit_hits` on failure.
- **Must never** Distinguish *no such user* from *wrong password*, in the message **or in the timing**.
- **Accept** A failed sign-in returns the generic failure and a real argon2id verification was performed; where a sign-in lands is decided by `can()`, not by a role string.

### `C6` — the portal
- **1 October 2026 — THE PORTAL HOME AS A DASHBOARD** (`src/web/portal-dashboard.ts`, drawn at the top of `/portal`,
  under the welcome; every section below is unchanged). Lakhbir: "for clients, it should show what the progression is,
  what we are working on, any up coming calls, notifications from the portal, and what cap rebate needs from them." Five
  cards, each with its button: **Where your claim is** — the six steps as a bar (the names the back office's bar reads,
  `clientProgress`) and "Step N of 6"; **What we need from you** (`#waiting-on-you`, replacing the old card of that name)
  — each open question with **Answer it** and each document asked for with **Send it**, both to their forms below; **What
  we are working on** — this step's words for the client (`STAGES[…].toTheClient`) and the last three things Cap Rebate
  told them it has done, newest first; **Your next call** — the soonest call not cancelled: a video call's **Join the video
  call** (its meeting's link), a telephone call "We will ring you", a call asked for with no time "We will offer you a
  time", none at all **Book a call**; **New for you** — our messages they have not read (**Read them**), and what was told
  or issued in the last 30 days. No figure, fact or value: words, dates and counts only. **Accept**
  `test/dashboard-home.test.ts` (the client's portal).
- **Route** `GET /portal` · `CLIENT` on their own business (`ownBusiness`), or staff.
- **Order since Phase 133c**: Book a call and who to contact at the top; the welcome card until dismissed; the stage
  bar; the range with its basis underneath (`CLIENT_RANGE_BASIS`); what we need from you, the questions and the documents
  asked for; then the rest; the long step explanations behind "What happens at each step".
- **Shows** the range with its qualifier · the stage **in the client's words** · the engagement letter's state · the appointment **and a control to move it** (`C6a`) · open questions · account settings · documents.
- **Must never** Show internal state, or guess a stage it does not recognise — where the recorded stage is unknown it **says so**.
- **Accept** Signed in as another client, every one of these returns a refusal, not an empty page.
- **Phase 134 — one company, many claims.** The heading is the **company's name**; the tab is **Your claims**; a
  **Your claims** card holds one tile per claim — the property, its town, how far it has got in the client's six steps,
  and the reference as a small grey label — each opening `C6b`. Questions, documents and messages stay here, at company
  level. The company-wide stage bar is drawn only where the company holds no claim yet. Each claim is asked of
  `claim.progress_read` (ADMIN, SALES, and CLIENT `ownBusiness`; the view-only login NO).

- **Phase 139 — "What Cap Rebate has done".** A card under Your claims (`clientStepsCard` in `src/web/approvals-pages.ts`) lists every step staff have told this client about — the company's, then each claim's under the property's name — as "✓ Cap Rebate has checked your company details — 21 Sep 2026", and a step Admin has taken back as "↻ Your company details are being re-checked — since …", so it never silently vanishes. Read by `clientSteps` in `src/web/approvals.ts`, asked of `client_step.read` (ADMIN, SALES, CLIENT `ownBusiness`; the view-only login NO), from `client_step_notices` alone: a step's key, its claim and a date. **Must never** carry a fact, a value, a register record, a figure, an estimate, a reason or a staff name — it has no path to any of them. **Accept** `test/phase139.test.ts`, which searches the raw response by value for each.

### `C6b` — one claim's progress — Phase 134
- **Route** `GET /portal/claims/{claim}` · `CLIENT` on their own company's claim, or ADMIN and SALES.
- **Shows** "Company > Property" · the company's name as the heading · the claim's property and grey reference · its
  step in the client's six words, as a stage bar · the step's own words · a link to what we need from you, on `/portal`.
- **Must never** Show the ten staff stages, a hold reason, an exit note, a value, a next action or a person. A hold shows
  the step the claim returns to; an exit says only that the claim is closed.
- **Accept** `test/phase134.test.ts` — a client of a company with three claims sees exactly three; another company's
  claim and a claim that does not exist answer the same 404.
- **Phase 139** the claim's own told steps, in the same "What Cap Rebate has done" card, under the step's words.

### `C6a` — the client moves their own call
- **Route** `POST /portal/bookings/{id}` — one route, three verbs: a chosen `starts_at`, `release=on`, or `cancel=on` (Phase 132e).
- **Who** `booking.move_own`, **a rule of its own, CLIENT only, `ownBusiness`** — added by Phase 113a and deliberately NOT `CLIENT` added to `booking.write`. That action means *own and arrange a client call*: it builds the diary through `bookableUserIds` and gates the whole back-office calendar section, so a client on it would appear in the answer to "who could take this call" and would pass a section gate.
- **Shows** moments from `publicSlots` — **the same function the funnel-close picker uses, with the people taken out.** A client never learns whose diary it is.
- **Writes** `bookings` (the same row — nothing here creates a second one) and a `booking_changes` row with `changed_by` naming the client. The staff booking page's existing change history renders it unchanged.
- **How late — AT ANY TIME, since Phase 132e (16 September 2026).** Lakhbir: *"Give the client full control to cancel at any time, and rebook at a time that suits them"*. A call the client already holds may be moved, released or cancelled however close or past it is. `CLIENT_MOVE_NOTICE_MINUTES` = **120** now applies to one end only: a NEW time the client picks must be at least two hours away (`tooSoonForNewTime`), and the picker draws no time inside it. The staff path is not subject to it. **The funnel-close booking has no two-hour rule and never had one**: it offers and accepts any free slot from now.
- **Cancel — `cancelOwnBooking`, at any time, and the times still come first.** The panel draws new times, then *"None of these work — ring me instead"* (release), then **Cancel this call**. A cancellation keeps the row, its time, channel and entry point; sets `cancelled_at` and `cancelled_by`; frees the slot (`bookedSpans` and `isBookable` read `cancelled_at IS NULL`); writes a `booking_changes` row with `cancelled = 1`, an outbox line (not sent) and a `BOOKING_CANCELLED` audit line; and **moves nothing about the enquiry** — no lead, claim or organisation status. It is final on its row (migration 025's triggers): no un-cancel, move or reassignment, by the store or by SQL. The back office's calendar lists **Cancelled calls** with who cancelled and when, marks the day row, and the booking page says so and draws no scheduling form.
- **Accept** `isBookable` is asked again at the moment of writing, with `ignoreBooking` set to this booking. A posted time no picker drew is refused. Another client's booking id and an id that does not exist return the same answer.
- **Test** `test/portal-booking.test.ts` — 27 tests since Phase 132e, including a negative control: adding `CLIENT` to `booking.write` fails it; every cancellation case (days away, twenty minutes away, already passed, waiting for a time) and the refusals after one; and the two-hour rule for a new time. Run under the clock grid: 350 instants, 350 green.

### `C7` — the engagement letter
- **Route** `GET /portal/letter` · `POST /portal/letter/sign`.
- **Renders** the actual `.docx`, parsed at request time. **The open terms are derived from the document** by `unresolvedTerms()`, so a term that gets filled in disappears without anybody editing code — and a new blank appears without anybody remembering to add it. **Eight are open today** (§5).
- **The specimen values are substituted or marked, never silent.** The template names a specimen client, gives it a specimen company number, and quotes an illustrative range; rendering it unchanged would show a real client somebody else's name in their own contract. **The specimen number was not fictional:** 09183422 is on the Companies House register as a dissolved company that has nothing to do with Caprebate (checked 12 September 2026, Phase 129). **(Updated 28 September 2026, Phase 153: the letter's specimen is now invented — "Quenmoor Care Group Ltd", company number 98604417, neither on the Companies House register that day — in the .docx and in `src/web/letter.ts` alike.)** Since Phase 129 the number is replaced whenever the organisation's record holds one, and flagged as a specimen when the record's number is absent or blank. The name with its suffix is replaced before the bare name, so a record called "Test Care Ltd" no longer renders as "Test Care Ltd Ltd".
- **STUBBED** No provider is contacted. The state machine `NOT_SENT → SENT → VIEWED → SIGNED` is real and lives in `engagements`.
- **Accept** The screen says signing is stubbed, and the signed row is what `assertSignedEngagement` reads server-side.

### `JS-C8` — answering a question
- **Route** `POST /portal/tasks/{id}`.
- **Writes** `client_tasks` → `ANSWERED`, with the answer state and **who gave it**.
- **Must never** Store *I don't know* as a blank. It is a state, and the "what is still missing" panel is computed from it.

### `C9` — documents, and the documents we asked for
- **Route** `POST /portal/documents` — **Phase 140: multipart, the file with the form, one step** (by `type_code`, by `request`, or a new version of `document`); urlencoded, the older link page with no `PUT` address on it · `PUT /upload/put`, or from a browser a multipart `POST` to the same address, since the CRM follow-on (the bytes, **and steps 2 to 7 on the same request**, through the one handler) · `GET /portal/documents/{id}` (what we did with it) · `GET /portal/documents/{id}/download`.
- **Writes** `logical_documents`, `documents`, `document_events`; a `document_links` row where the request was on a claim; `client_tasks` when an upload receives a request; the object store, outside the web root.
- **Shows** the documents we asked for, each with why, what it holds up and its due reading, and an upload button; the received ones ticked; the documents sent, each with its version. CRM 4 of 10.
- **Must never** Trust the extension — the type is sniffed **server-side**. Give the storage directory an execute bit. Promote before the strip. Ask the client for a file name. Offer a button that marks a request received. Accept a second write through a used link.
- **Accept** The 39 handler tests are green against the adapter; a promotion attempted before a recorded strip throws `E_PROMOTE_BEFORE_STRIP`; **the download returns 410 rather than bytes**, because the original was deleted and there is nothing to serve; `test/documents.test.ts` takes a link from the portal, PUTs a file, sees the request received and the replayed PUT refused; `test/follow-on.test.ts` sends the file from the portal's own form, which carries no script.

### `C10` — the client's own settings
- **Route** `/portal/settings` and its sub-routes — **the same implementation as `/staff/settings`**, so a password change cannot rotate the session on one and forget on the other. What differs is the shell and which sections are drawn; **a client posting to the working-times route is refused by the rule, not by the absence of a form.**

## 2.2 · The back office

### `B0` — the landing page — rows of tiles, Phase 136
- **1 October 2026 — HOME AS A DASHBOARD** (`src/web/staff-dashboard.ts`; the rows below are kept and placed in it).
  Lakhbir: "for workers, it should be client driven whos a new lead (where did the lead come from) whats in the pipeline at
  which stage, any new messages received, a calendar allowing users to block off time … and below it should be
  activities for today … a CTA on each, if its a zoom call clicking the CTA launches zoom and the same goes for email.
  Phone will be manual", and "Admin should have a full overview allowing them to click tabs across the top". **One page,
  four views** (`dashView`): Sales (also the view-only login, with no button that changes anything), Accounting,
  Surveying — each login its own role's; **Admin chooses with tabs** (`?view=sales|accounting|surveying|admin`, Sales
  first). **Sales:** the figures at a glance (`.dash-strip`: new leads and how many waited too long, new messages, calls
  today, claims in the pipeline) · New leads (the row, each with where it came from) beside **New messages** (each company
  with messages from them this person has not read — `newFromClient` — and **Read and reply**) · Pipeline by stage ·
  **Your calendar** · **Today's activities** · Today and overdue · Existing leads — next actions. **Accounting:** its
  figures, Money, the calendar, today's activities, Today and overdue. **Surveying:** its figures, Survey jobs, the
  calendar, today's activities, Today and overdue. **Admin's tab:** **Who is working** (every member of staff who can sign
  in: working now = a session seen in the last 30 minutes; today's first and last moments, from `sessions`) and the Admin
  tiles. **Today's activities** (`callsBetween`, `firstEmails`): today's calls that are this person's or nobody's yet, in
  time order — a Zoom call's button **Start the Zoom call** (`/staff/calendar/{id}/video`, the host's page inside the
  site), a telephone call's number shown and its button **Open the call** (made by hand, then noted) — and every new lead
  nobody has written to yet (no free-typed or template email to the lead or its company) with **Write the first email**
  (`/staff/leads/{id}/email`). **Your calendar:** the week, Monday to Sunday (`?week=`, Last · This · Next week), your
  calls and your blocked time, today marked; **Block off time** (`POST /staff/time-blocks`: a day, from, until, an
  optional last day, an optional reason only staff see) and **Free it** (`POST /staff/time-blocks/{id}/remove`), each
  coming back only to a Home address. **A block holds the diary exactly as a call does** (migration 066, `time_blocks`;
  `bookedSpans` and `isBookable` in `src/web/schedule.ts` read both), so no slot is offered to a client or a colleague in
  it. Refused: a finish not after the start, more than 31 days, wholly past, or over a call already in that diary ("Move
  the call first"). Admin, Sales, Accounting and Surveying keep a diary; a block is freed by its owner or Admin; never
  edited or deleted; audited `TIME_BLOCKED`, `TIME_FREED` (hours only, never the reason). **Accept**
  `test/dashboard-home.test.ts`; `test/phase136.test.ts` (the rows' new order) and `test/configuration.test.ts` restated.
- **1 October 2026 — ADMIN'S TAB: PEOPLE AND API KEYS.** Lakhbir: the Admin tab "is essentially back end access this
  allowa admin to create new users, archive old users, reset passwords, update the API keys and save them, see which
  staff are working and what times". **People:** everyone who can sign in, their role and when they last signed in, with
  **Reset password** (`POST /staff/users/{id}/temporary-password`, as on the Users page: a temporary one shown once, their
  sessions ended) and **Archive** (`POST /staff/users/{id}/disable` with `back=/staff?view=admin`, the same disable —
  sessions revoked at once, the last administrator refused — and back to the tab), never for yourself; **Add a person**
  opens the Users page's form (`#create-user`); archived people listed under **Archived (n)**. **API keys**
  (`src/web/saved-keys.ts`, migration 067 `saved_keys`): every key in use except `CAPREBATE_MFA_KEY` and the retired ones,
  each with where its value comes from — **Saved here** (when, by whom), **From the key file or the website's settings**,
  **Not set**, or **Saved here, but this server cannot open it** — **never a value**. **Save it** (`POST
  /staff/keys/{NAME}`) needs Admin's own password typed again; the value is sealed with AES-256-GCM under
  `CAPREBATE_MFA_KEY`, the key's name bound in (a sealed value copied to another name does not open), and without a good
  `CAPREBATE_MFA_KEY` nothing is saved and the card says so. **Use the key file's value again** (`POST
  /staff/keys/{NAME}/unsave`, password again) adds a row with no value. The newest row per name wins over the key file or
  settings (`overlaySecrets`, `src/secrets.ts`); every request checks the newest row number and rebuilds the keys only
  when it moved (`refreshSavedKeys`, `src/server.ts`), so every copy of the website uses a saved key from its next
  request. Rows are never edited or deleted. Audited `KEY_SAVED` / `KEY_UNSAVED` with the key's name only. **Three
  colours** (Lakhbir, 1 Oct 2026: "not set = red, active = green, set but not active = amber"): **In use** (green) — set,
  and its service switched on and ready (`serviceOn`: each service's own switch or readiness check — `EMAIL_SENDING`,
  `INBOUND_EMAIL`, `IDENTITY_CHECK`, `COMPANY_SEARCH`, `ADDRESS_LOOKUP`, the register and Claude switches, `zoomBlockers`,
  `whatsappMissing`, `turnstileWhy`, a valid GA4 id); **Set, not in use** (amber) — set but its service is off, or saved
  and unreadable, with the reason in words; **Not set** (red). The source (saved here, or the key file or settings) is
  under each. **Admin's view tabs are in the top bar** (`viewTabs` in `src/web/navigation.ts`), between the name and the
  search box, on every back-office page, the one Home shows marked. **Accept** `test/dashboard-home.test.ts` (Admin's tab).
- **Route** `GET /staff` · gated once by `backoffice.enter` for the whole `/staff` prefix. Drawn by `homeRows` in `src/web/home.ts`: a heading and rows of tiles, nothing else. Lakhbir, 18 September 2026: "tiles over heavy text … new leads, existing leads' next actions, today and overdue, pipeline by stage."
- **The rows, in order.** Sales, Admin and Read only: **1 New leads** — a count tile (to `/staff/leads#leads-new` since Phase 137), then one tile per lead with status New and no claim, and — for a login that may handle them (`lead.write` on `__website_enquiries__`) — one per unanswered website enquiry (to `/staff/leads#enquiry-<id>`), exactly the cards of the Leads page's New section: company, where it came from, how long it has waited; oldest first; red once it has waited more than one working day (`isLateNewLead` / `workingDaysSince` in `src/web/working-days.ts` since Phase 137: every Monday to Friday after the day it came in, by the UK date, **not counting England and Wales bank holidays** — `BANK_HOLIDAYS_EW`, copied from gov.uk's `bank-holidays.json` for 2025 to 2028; after `BANK_HOLIDAYS_KNOWN_TO` a weekday counts again, so a lead turns red early rather than late, and `test/phase137.test.ts` fails 90 days before the list runs out. The Leads page's New section reads the same function). **2 Existing leads — next actions** — one tile per lead Contacted, Qualified or Nurture: company, its next action (its earliest open task) and when it is due; red when late or when it has none; then a tile to **Claims with no next step** (`/staff/next-actions`, for `nextActionsGate`) — linked, never repeated on Home. **3 Today and overdue** — every open task due today or late that the login may read (`tasksDueByToday`, each through `staff_task.read`; its own and its team's, from `myWork`, for Accounting and Surveying), and the questions and document requests it put to a client due today or late; late first, in red. Leads' and claims' next actions are tasks, so they are here. **4 Pipeline by stage** — one tile per stage of `pipelineBoard` with its count, opening `/staff/claims?stage=<key>`; On hold when any; then **Reports**. At phone width (≤ 720px) this row alone draws two tiles to a row (`ul.pairtiles`, Phase 137); every other row stays one to a row. Admin then: **Users**, **Switches** (`/staff/admin-settings`), **Audit log** ("Checked — nothing changed", or "Problem found — tell Lakhbir" in red when the chain does not recompute) and **Refused sign-ins** (`LOGIN_FAILED` lines among the latest audit lines). Accounting: **Invoices due**, **Fees to invoice**, **VAT position**, **Reports**, then Today and overdue. Surveying: **Waiting for your figures**, **All your survey jobs**, **Reports**, then Today and overdue.
- **Every tile** is one link (`.hometile .tilelink`) and holds at most three lines; text is cut at 48 characters, at the last whole word, with an ellipsis. A row holds at most twelve tiles; beyond that the last is **See all**. A row the login's gate refuses is not drawn; a row it may see with nothing in it says so in one line ("No new leads."), so empty is never mistaken for broken. At 720px and below the tiles stack in one column.
- **Gone from the page, Phase 136:** "Signed in as", the welcome card (now at the top of Help's landing section, `/staff/help`, until hidden; hiding it returns to Help), the two dashed notes (the outbox is on Your account; UK time is in the guide), the long My tasks block (on Tasks), the dashboard button, and the list of claims nobody owns that break the one-next-action rule (it is the Claims with no next step view).
- **Must never** Be the only gate, or carry a claim's value. Every row reads through its own screen's store, which authorises every row again; every section behind it authorises its own objects. A section a login may not open still answers 403 when its address is typed.
- **Accept** `test/phase136.test.ts`, `test/tasks.test.ts`, `test/follow-on.test.ts`, `test/documents.test.ts`.

### `JS-B1`/`JS-B2` — filled assessments
- **Route** `GET /staff/assessments` · `GET /staff/assessments/{id}`.
- **Who** ADMIN, SALES (`assessment.read`). **ACCOUNTING is absent by design.**
- **Phase 135** a view of **Leads**: the list is headed by the Leads filters ("All leads · Filled assessments"), and the Leads tab is current on it. The address and its gate are unchanged.
- **Accept** ACCOUNTING signing in and requesting an assessment id gets a refusal, not a redacted page.

### `B3`/`B4` — the calendar
- **Route** `GET /staff/calendar` (`?view=day|week&date=`) · `GET /staff/calendar/{id}` · `POST /staff/calendar/{id}/schedule` · `GET /staff/calendar/{id}/video` · `POST /staff/calendar/{id}/length` (Phase 156d: how long a video call lasted) (30 Sep 2026: the video call, joined inside the back office).
- **Who** `booking.read` / `booking.write` — ADMIN and SALES.
- **Writes** `bookings`, `booking_changes`.
- **Must never** Store, compare or transmit a local time. Every stored moment is UTC and every wire value is a `Z`-terminated ISO-8601 string; only the LABEL is local. **Never reintroduce an offset constant** — `+1` is wrong for four months of the year, `0` for eight, and both are wrong on the two mornings that matter. `test/localtime.test.ts` fails on either.
- **Accept** A slot that `isBookable` refuses is refused at the write even if it appeared on a screen.

### `B5`/`B6` — engaged clients and the business page
- **Phase 135 — the one Clients list.** `GET /staff/clients` (`?sort=waiting|name`) draws `clientList` in `src/web/client-list.ts`: every company `organisationList` admits, each row's **Waiting on** from `waitingOn` (null, and the column not drawn, for a login without `research.read`). Columns: Company (the link, with the legal name where it differs and the sector), Waiting on, Status, Owner, Claims. **Who** the list: `organisation.read` — ADMIN, SALES, READ_ONLY (it was `research.read`, ADMIN and SALES, until this phase; the organisations list it absorbed was already `organisation.read`). The business page and every action on it keep their own rules. The six states the engaged-client list carried as columns are on the company's page: the highlights and the **Waiting on** card (research, questions with the client, still missing, each pack ready or not). **Accept** `test/phase135.test.ts`, `test/engaged.test.ts`.
- **Route** `GET /staff/clients` · `GET /staff/clients/{id}` · `POST /staff/clients/{id}/research` · `.../research/retry` · `.../research/pick` · `.../research/pick/choose` · `.../research/pick/take-back` · `.../tasks/send` · `.../tasks/answer` · `.../tasks/withdraw` · `.../notes`.
- **Who** ADMIN, SALES.
- **Writes** `research_runs`, `research_sources`, `client_tasks`, `internal_notes`.
- **Must never** Maintain panel 3 by hand. Render a note unescaped. Fire anything: **no connector exists**, and every source is reported against its gate. Withdraw an ANSWERED question — that would delete a fact the client gave, and the refusal is read off the row rather than achieved by not drawing a button.
- **How panel 3 gets its cash gates** `src/web/funnel-session.ts` reads `assessment_drafts.state_json` — the CB_FUNNEL state the six screens saved — and maps it onto an engine Session. Every mapping in it is justified from the engine's own code; its NOT MAPPED block says what is deliberately left absent and why. A DONT_KNOW or a SKIPPED answer sets **nothing**, in one place (`known()`), which is what makes a `null` gate a report rather than an artefact.
- **Phase 139 — CHECK AND TELL THE CLIENT (`#checks`, after `#claims`).** Lakhbir: "When viewing the client's portfolio there needs to be where the information has come from (API) with a secondary approve box. When a user clicks approve this then pushes the details to say Cap Rebate has completed this to the client." Drawn by `checksBlock` in `src/web/approvals-pages.ts` from `checksPanel` in `src/web/approvals.ts`, for `fact.approve` (ADMIN, SALES) only — no other login gets the section. **Every fact** from a register record (`register_records`, one line per field), a client's own portal answer (`client_tasks` answered by the CLIENT), a recorded claim fact (`claim_facts`, latest per key), a surveyor's job and commitment, a tax adviser's sign-off and an issued client report is one line: a **source chip** ("Companies House", "EPC register", "HM Land Registry", "Care regulator", "You told us", "Your document", "Surveyor", "Tax adviser", "Cap Rebate"), what it is in words, its value (staff only), **the date it arrived** (the record's own time), and a **"Checked and approved"** button drawn as a tick box. Ticking posts `POST /staff/clients/{id}/checks/approve`; taking a tick off posts `…/checks/unapprove` and **requires a reason**. Both write `fact_approvals` (migration 034, no update, no delete) and audit `FACT_APPROVED` / `FACT_UNAPPROVED` with the fact's reference, the step and the claim — never a value, never the reason. **A fact's reference is built from the row it came from** (`reg.<record>.<field>`, `ans.<task>`, `cf.<claim fact row>`, `job.`, `survey.`, `signoff.`, `report.`), so a new value is a new row, a new reference, and starts unticked. A fact that is not known — a client's "I don't know", a claim fact recorded as not known — is shown and **cannot be ticked** (N4). **The facts group into seven client steps** (table below): the company's own step, then six per claim. A step is **complete only when it has at least one fact, every fact is ticked, and nothing it needs is still missing** (the company's missing company facts; the company's missing property facts hold every claim's property step, because it is not known which claim they are about; the claim's askable tax facts not known). **"Tell the client and move on"** (it was "Tell the client" until 30 September 2026; see the redesign below) is drawn only on a complete step not already told, and is never automatic: `POST /staff/clients/{id}/steps/tell` writes `client_step_notices` (migration 034) and audits `CLIENT_STEP_TOLD`. **Only Admin** can take a told step back: `POST /staff/clients/{id}/steps/take-back`, a reason required, audited `CLIENT_STEP_TAKEN_BACK`; the client then sees "being re-checked", never the reason. A property register's record belongs to the claim on the property it names (`register_records.property_id`, new in 034); with no property, to the company's one claim if it has one; otherwise it is listed under "Not matched to a claim yet" and completes no step. **A claim fact the client answered** (`claim_fact.<key>`, asked from the claim page's WE NEED, Phase 138's carry) is offered here as the fact: the client's words are put in a box of the fact's own kind (a date, an amount, a choice — a choice is chosen only on an exact match), and ticking records it through `recordFact` with source `CLIENT_ANSWER` and ticks the new fact in the same transaction; a value the fact cannot take is refused and nothing is written. From then on the recorded fact stands in for the answer. **The paid-research rule stands:** the Lookups section keeps one "Run the research" button and no tick beside a lookup — approval is on the results, never on the calls. **The facts fold** under "Check the N facts" (open where a step has three or fewer, or is the step just worked in): a tick's form carries the step's anchor, `at`, and the redirect is `?done=checked&step=<anchor>#<anchor>`, accepted only in the shape `step-(company|<claim id>)-<step>` — ids only, never a name. **Accept** `test/phase139.test.ts` (12 tests; all 12 fail against the `.bak-phase139` files) and the payload proof.
- **Layout — 30 September 2026: THE CLIENT PAGE REDESIGNED (regroups Phase 138's sections into tabs; nothing removed).** Lakhbir: "the whole interfact for a client needs to be designed - theres too much going on and its hard to find things". Agreed in the chat from a drawn layout ("Yes, build it"). Drawn by `src/web/client-layout.ts`. **The header**: the company, owner and who we wait on as before; **the client's six steps as a bar** (`progressBar`, `<ol class="cprogress">`, the portal's own step names, the current one `aria-current="step"`, from `businesses.stage`); and three actions — **Chat** (`?chat=all#chat`: every portal message, email and WhatsApp with them, and the box to write), **Log a call**, **Files** — with every other quick action under **More** (the quick action "Email the client" is now "Write an email"). **The next step** (`nextStepCard`): the step ready to tell the client (else one with facts to tick, else the first not told), up to six of its facts with their source chips and ticks, and on a ready step **"Tell the client and move on"** (the same `POST …/steps/tell`); otherwise "N to tick. M still missing." and a link to the checks. Where every step on a claim's stage is told but the claim has not moved, it says why (`whyNotMoved`: the gate's unmet needs, or the next-action rule). **The lookups** (`lookupsCard`): "Lookups X of Y done", a count per state (Failed, Done, Waiting, Not run yet, Not switched on), each lookup with its publisher, dataset and state (and when, if done) under "See each lookup", and a link to the full panel under Checks. **The tabs** (`tabbed`): Chat, Claims, Properties, Questions (questions and answers), People (details, contacts, identity), Checks (checks and lookups), Files, Activity; a section not named goes under Activity. **No script**: CSS `:target` and `:has(:target)` show a tab, so any link to a section's anchor (`#questions`, `#sources`, `#chat`) opens its tab; with no target the server's choice (`is-default`) shows — the section just changed (`?done=`), else Checks when a step is ready or being checked, else Chat. Inside a tab each section keeps Phase 138's open-or-closed rule; the chat is always open. **"Tell the client and move on"** (`tellAndMoveOn`, `src/web/tell-and-move.ts`; Lakhbir chose "Move it when allowed"): (1) `tellClient` unchanged — only a complete step, only once; (2) **one email** (kind NOTIFICATION) to the company's `contact_email`, else its first contact with an address: "Cap Rebate news: we have …", what was done with the date, where a claim moved "We are now moving on to the next phase: <the client's step name>", and the portal's address — never a fact, value, figure or register name; (3) **for each claim whose current stage the step sits on** (`StepDef.procedure`; every claim of the company for the company step), when every step on that stage (the company's and the claim's own) is told, `moveClaim` FORWARD one stage — the same gate, next-action rule and audit as the Move button; a refusal, or a login that cannot move claims, stops nothing (the client was still told) and the page says why. A step for an earlier or later stage moves nothing. The redirect reads `done=told`, `told-moved`, `told-emailed` or `told-moved-emailed`. **Accept** `test/client-redesign.test.ts` (six tests: the header, bar and actions; the tabs, their order, grouping and default, no script or style; the lookups card; the next-step card before and after ticking; a told step that leaves the claim where it is, with the email and the portal; a told step that finishes a stage and moves the claim, then the card showing why it stays at the next); `test/phase133b`, `phase138`, `phase139` restated for the tabs and the new button.
- **Layout — Phase 138: COLOURED SECTIONS THAT OPEN AND CLOSE (supersedes the tabs below).** Lakhbir: "The different sections should be coloured, in collapsible boxes, and when we are working on a particular section it should show what we are working on, what data is needed and where the data is coming from." The client page and the claim page (`B17`/`B18`) draw every section with ONE component, `section` in `src/web/components.ts`: a `<details class="sec sec-<kind>">` with a coloured band down the left edge, an icon and a word for its kind (**Company**, **Property**, **Facts and figures**, **Documents**, **Money**, **Messages** — colour is never the only signal), a heading, and a status chip in words — **Not started**, **Working on it**, **Waiting for the client**, **Done**. Every section's body opens with the same three lines, in this order: **WORKING ON** (what is being done in it now), **WE NEED** (each missing fact, with an **Ask the client** button that posts to `/staff/clients/{id}/tasks/send`, the route and rules it always had; once asked it reads "Asked — waiting"; the first five, then "and N more"; two sources asking the same question are one line) and **COMING FROM** (Companies House, the EPC register, the care regulator, HM Land Registry, the client, the surveyor…). Each missing fact is placed in its section by `section` and `from` on `MissingFact` (`src/web/engaged.ts`), read from the source or screen that already decides which pack it blocks — Companies House is the company; EPC, the valuation office, the care regulator and the land registry are the property; the client's property and works answers are the property; everything else is the claim's facts. **Colours** are brand tokens only (`src/web/assets.ts`, `.sec-company` ink, `.sec-property` mint, `.sec-facts` green, `.sec-documents` muted grey, `.sec-money` amber, `.sec-messages` striped mint and ink). **Open or closed:** a section changed by the request that sent the person back (`?done=`) is open; otherwise the person's own choice; otherwise **Working on it** and **Waiting for the client** start open and **Done** and **Not started** start closed. **The person's choice** — "keep this open for me", "keep this closed for me", "Let the page decide" at the foot of each section — posts to `POST /staff/me/sections` (`src/web/sections.ts`, `keepSectionChoice`), which refuses a key off `SECTION_KEYS`, a choice other than yes/no/auto, or a return address that is not one of the two page shapes (ids only), and keeps it per person in `section_prefs` (migration 033), audited `SECTION_PREFERENCE_SET` with the key alone. It is never in an address, and it changes nothing a login may see: a section a login may not read is never built. The client page's sections, in order: `#claims` Claims · `#checks` Check and tell the client (Phase 139, ADMIN and SALES) · `#questions` Questions for the client (with `#tasks`, and `#missing` behind "Everything still missing … and answer one from a call") · `#activity` Messages and activity (with `#company-notes`) · `#details` The company · `#contacts` Contacts (one card per contact) · `#properties` Properties · `#sources` Lookups · `#answers` What the client told us · `#documents` Documents. The view-only login gets Claims, Messages and activity (no notes), The company, Contacts, Properties and Documents, and no ask. **Gone:** the "Waiting on" card (its facts are in the highlights and the WORKING ON lines), the Claims count in the highlights, the duplicate hidden tab heading (`recordTabs` names a panel by `aria-label` now, on every screen that uses it) and the numbered headings ("2 · Sources"), which are plain words here, on the survey workspace and on finance. **Accept** `test/phase138.test.ts`.
- **Layout — Phase 134: THE ONE CLIENT PAGE** (its tabs superseded by Phase 138). One page per company at `/staff/clients/{id}` (`src/web/client-page.ts`), folding in what `B13`/`B14` drew. The heading is the **trading name**, the legal name under it in small text. A **Claims** card holds one tile per claim — property and town, stage, next action, and the reference as a small grey label — each opening the claim **inside** the company at `/staff/clients/{id}/claims/{claim}`, under a "Company > Property" breadcrumb. Tabs: `#activity` (tasks, **one timeline for the company**, and the internal notes, each note optionally tagged with the claim it is about), then — for `research.read` — `#tasks` (questions to the client), `#missing`, `#answers`, `#sources`, drawn by the unchanged `panel4`, `panel3`, `panel1`, `panel2`; then `#contacts`, `#properties`, `#documents`, `#details`. A question sent from `#missing` may also name a claim of the company (migration 032 lets a question carry one; the same-organisation trigger still refuses another company's). The right-hand panel holds the quick actions and **Waiting on**. The view-only login reads the organisation's tabs and no research or notes.
- **Layout — Phase 133b, superseded by Phase 134** Five tabs, one per numbered panel, `#answers`, `#sources`, `#missing`, `#tasks`, `#notes`.
- **Phase 138 — THE BLOAT CUT, written before any code changed (21 September 2026).** Lakhbir: "think about all the information the user sees and ask if it is relevant on the client's page or is it just bloat." Every block on the two pages, who uses it, how often, and the verdict. **The rule: if no role uses it weekly, it does not sit open on the page.** Roles: A Admin, S Sales, RO Read only, Acc Accounting, Sv Surveying (only on an assigned claim). Nothing in this table changes who may see a block — each is still drawn only where its own rule says yes.
