- **Accept** An untiered source is treated as **PAID** and the screen says the tier is unsettled; an unrecognised missing fact is reported as blocking **both** packs.

### Phase 139 — the client's steps, on top of the ten-stage procedure

The steps are what a client is told. They sit on top of the ten-stage operating procedure (`PROCEDURE_STAGES` in
`src/web/stages.ts`) and **never replace it: telling a step moves no stage, and moving a stage tells no step.** The
portal's own six-step bar (`clientStageFor`, from `raiseBusinessStage` in `src/web/pipeline.ts`) is unchanged beside it.
`STEPS` in `src/web/approvals.ts` is the list.

| Step (staff read) | Whose | Its facts | Sits on the procedure's stage(s) | The client reads, once told | …and while Admin has it back |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Company details checked | the company | Companies House records; the client's answers to company questions | 4 Data gathering | Cap Rebate has checked your company details | Your company details are being re-checked |
| Property details checked | each claim | the property's register records (EPC, HM Land Registry, care regulator, valuation office, planning, postcode, address lookup); the client's property answers | 4 Data gathering | Cap Rebate has checked your property details | Your property details are being re-checked |
| Tax details checked | each claim | the claim's recorded tax facts; the client's answers to a claim fact | 4 Data gathering | Cap Rebate has checked your tax details | Your tax details are being re-checked |
| Survey booked | each claim | a surveyor assigned the claim's job | 5 Survey | Cap Rebate has booked your survey | Your survey booking is being re-checked |
| Survey done | each claim | the surveyor's commitment (never its figures) | 5 Survey, 6 Valuation | Your survey is done | Your survey is being re-checked |
| Figures checked by a tax adviser | each claim | the tax adviser's sign-off record (never its figure) | 7 Tax adviser sign-off | A tax adviser has checked the figures | The tax adviser's check is being re-checked |
| Report ready | each claim | an issued client report | 8 Client report and accountant report | Your report is ready | Your report is being re-checked |

Stages 1 to 3 (enquiry, assessment, engagement), 9 (invoice) and 10 (defence pack) carry no client step: the portal's own
bar already says where those are, and none is a fact Cap Rebate checks.

### `B7`/`B8` — the surveyor's workspace
- **Route** `GET /staff/survey` · `GET /staff/survey/{job}` · `GET /staff/survey/{job}/payload.json` · `POST .../rooms` · `POST .../commit` · `POST .../amend` · `POST .../disclosure`.
- **Who** `surveyor_job.enter` (ADMIN, SALES, SURVEYING), then `surveyor_job.read` — and for SURVEYING that is `assignedSurveyor`, a test on **the specific job**.
- **Writes** `surveyor_room_counts`, `surveyor_rate_events`, `surveyor_commitments`, `anchor_log` — and, since Phase 132, `surveyor_control_observations` (migration 023), with `surveyor_rate_events.blank_control` marking a field served blank.
- **Must never** Assemble the surveyor's object by reading everything and deleting the estimate. **It is built forwards from what the quarantine released**, so a new field is absent by default; a build-then-strip assembler is one forgotten key away from leaking, and a developer who sees an estimate arrive and get hidden will build exactly that. Add a *reveal the client-facing estimate* control — **the old survey-pack wireframe had one and it was deliberately not copied**: the estimate is not in the surveyor's response to be revealed, and commitment releases it with no separate action and nobody to ask. De-duplicate the denial log lines.
- **Layout — Phase 133b** Highlights (sector, stage, opened, rooms counted, rates recorded, your figures, and **our estimate: withheld or released — never a figure**); six tabs, `#property`, `#rooms`, `#rates`, `#evidence`, `#figures`, `#record`, one per numbered section — and since Phase 142b a seventh, `#messages`, the thread with the client, read only, where the rule admits the surveyor; the right-hand panel holds the quick actions (**Commit your figures**, or **Amend** once released, is first) and the quarantine panel. The workspace and its payload are built before the layout draws a line; the Surveying home card links to `#figures`. **Must never** put a figure in the highlights or the side panel.
- **Accept** `cd 09-app/backend && CAPREBATE_ALLOW_SEED=1 node tools/payload-proof.ts` — see §6, N1.

### The survey pack — 1 October 2026, stage 1 of 6 (the sums and the surveyor's stamped changes)
- **Why** Lakhbir: "build the comprehensive report, as best we can and then we engage with a surveyor … each section allowing the surveyor to edit if needed and the numbers matching what they put in, change or take out"; "any edits made by the surveyor needs to essentially have their stamp to say thats their number"; "this is one thing that cannot fail". He chose (1 Oct 2026) a **pre-filled pack** with honest wording: the figures are drafted from the evidence and the surveyor reviews, changes and approves. Approving, the read-every-chapter unlock, the fingerprint, the legal check, the client's report and the photo checklist are stages 2 to 6.
- **Route** `GET /staff/survey/{job}/pack` (chapters 4, 9 and 10 built; the rest say so) · `POST /staff/survey/{job}/pack/value` · `/line` · `/revert` · `/remove` · `/restore` · `/add` · `/property` · `/extra` (Phase 153e: chapter 15's VAT position and the VAT not reclaimed, and chapter 9's shared areas from the floor plans, each with a reason). The workspace's quick actions open it (*Open the survey pack*).
- **The method** section 562 by rebuild cost (`src/web/pack-sums.ts`): the building's price (price paid less bare land) is shared out as the rebuild cost is — what qualifies = building's price × qualifying rebuild cost ÷ whole rebuild cost (floor area × whole-building rate). Each line: how much × rate = its rebuild cost; × the share that qualifies = its qualifying cost; its pool (main or special rate). **Whole pence in BigInt**, four named roundings (half a penny up); the special rate pool is what qualifies less the main pool, so **the pools add to the penny**. **No total while one value is missing** (each gap named and linked) or while a problem stands (land at or above the price; elements costing more than the building; a building costing nothing; a sum past 2^53 pence). Limits: £1bn, a million units or m², £1m a unit, 100%.
- **The lines** every row of `02-modules-and-build-prep/Caprebate-Fixture-Taxonomy-v1.csv` for the sector and `ALL` whose pool a survey decides (MP, SR, APPORTION, FOLLOWS_PLANT); never loose equipment (MP_AIA, the accountant's claim), an excluded row or the structures allowance (`src/web/pack-lines.ts`, read whole, quoted commas kept, a broken file refused). GP 37, dental 38, care homes 38, supported living 37, private hospitals 42.
- **What is drafted, and only from evidence** the price (the title register's, by property), the floor area (the energy certificate's, **of that property only**), a whole-building system's quantity (the pack's floor area, whoever gave it — the certificate, or the surveyor in chapter 9; its label says *The floor area (chapter 9)*), a room-counted row's quantity (the room counts on the job, **only when every room it needs is counted**), and from the fixture list the pool and the share with its authority (*Our draft*). **Never a rate** (BCIS's; the surveyor's until BCIS is bought) and never the land. The pack shows the price, which the workspace withholds (REGISTER_AMOUNTS_WITHHELD): section 562 shares out the price, and Lakhbir chose the pre-filled pack.
- **Writes** migration 070: `survey_packs` (one per job, and its property), `survey_pack_added_lines`, `survey_pack_entries` — every value a surveyor typed, every line taken out or put back, with **who, when and a reason (required)**; the newest entry is the value, an empty one puts our draft back. **Never edited or deleted** (triggers); the database itself refuses an entry with no reason, a share above 100% or a negative value. Drafts are not stored: they are read each time, so a later room count or certificate comes in by itself. Audited `SURVEY_PACK_CHANGED` (what, the line, the fields — never a value).
- **Who** reading: `surveyor_job.read`. Changing: `surveyor_job.commit` — **the assigned surveyor only**; Admin reads without an edit link. A company with several properties and no claim: the surveyor says which, once.
- **Out-of-date pages** every form carries the pack's counter; the check and the write are one transaction; a change from an older page is refused and the page drawn again saying so.
- **Accept** `test/pack-sums.test.ts` (12: a worked example by hand; 20,000 seeded packs against an independent oracle to the penny; order, removal, zero and pool moves; every gap; bad values; the extremes; 50,000 fuzzed strings into the parsers; typed values read back) and `test/survey-pack.test.ts` (14: the drafts; the stamp and its history; draft back, take out, put back, add; the database refusing edits; who may change it; refusals naming the box; out-of-date pages; several properties; a complete pack's page totals equal the module's; 400 random changes from two tabs with the counter, history and totals agreeing; 2,000 random forms never breaking a page). 21 single-line mutations each turned them red.

### The survey pack — 2 October 2026, stage 2 of 6 (the legal check)
- **Why** Lakhbir agreed (1 Oct 2026) the legal check comes before the survey: a building bought from an owner who could claim can leave the buyer nothing on the fixtures unless the value was fixed in time (CAA 2001 s.187A; the s.198 election and its two-year notice, s.201).
- **Route** chapter 7 of the pack, `GET /staff/survey/{job}/pack?ch=7` (`&q=` opens an answer to change it) · `POST /staff/survey/{job}/pack/legal`. The right-hand side of every chapter shows the check's state (*Clear*, *Clear, with a deadline*, *Not settled yet*, *Fails*) and whether it stops approval.
- **The law is the engine's** (`src/web/pack-legal.ts`). The questions are the sector's own (`getSector(…).Q`), **chosen by the facts their `onSet` writes** (the trigger, the deal, the dates, the previous owners, the election, the s.187A facts, the tribunal), found by setting each once on a scratch session — so all five sectors' sets are read the same way, and no list of ids is kept in step. The answers are replayed through each question's own `showIf` and `onSet` in the sector's order; then **`CB_ELECTION.assess`** (only where the flow asked about an election) and **`CB_S187A.assess`** give the position. Today is `s.__now`; the claimant type is the business's (unknown read as a company, whose earlier date never relieves).
- **The verdict** GREEN — not a purchase, shares bought, nobody could ever claim, or the rules met; URGENT — still time to fix the value (LIVE), with the deadline; AMBER — a question unanswered, an engine INDET, an election's reach unclear, or a nominal election over listed items (take them out); RED — NIL, or a nominal election over the fixtures generally. **Not known is never a pass.** A complete election over every fixture, or a disposal value statement, **caps** the claim: `legalAllows` refuses approval on AMBER, RED, or a pack figure above the cap (stage 3 calls it).
- **Writes** migration 071, `survey_pack_legal_answers`: the engine's question id, the answer as JSON (checked by the database), who gave it and **where it came from (required)**; the newest row is the answer; **never edited or deleted**. Only a question the flow asks now can be answered; a single-choice answer starts on *Choose…* and cannot be saved unchosen; a date must be real. An earlier answer changed leaves later ones kept and unused (*Answers no longer asked*).
- **Who** reading: as the pack. Answering: the assigned surveyor (`surveyor_job.commit`), Sales or Admin (`claim_facts.record`). Not another surveyor, Accounting or view-only.
- **Accept** `test/pack-legal.test.ts` (13): the questions the engine's own and the same in every sector; the verdict on each kind of purchase (a new building; shares; nobody could claim; bought within two years with nothing fixed — the deadline; bought years ago — a specialist; a complete election — capped, and refused above the cap; a late election; a nominal one; "not sure"); what may be typed; through the pages — the stamp and source, refusals, out-of-date pages, who may answer, answers kept for ever, an earlier answer changed. 22 single-line mutations each turned them red.

### The survey pack — 2 October 2026, stage 3 of 6 (read it all, then one click)
- **Why** Lakhbir: "whole pack needs to be read through then approve button ungreys like someone reading and accepting T&C"; "when the surveyor signs it should be a single CTA approve click"; the fingerprint, approved; "this is one thing that cannot fail".
- **Route** chapter 23, `GET /staff/survey/{job}/pack?ch=23` (`&details=1` opens the RICS form) · `POST /staff/survey/{job}/pack/details` · `POST /staff/survey/{job}/pack/approve` · `GET /staff/survey/{job}/pack/version/{n}`. The right-hand side of every chapter shows *Approval*: ready, how many things first, or approved and which version; and how many chapters are opened. The chapter list ticks each one opened.
- **The button is grey** (`disabled`) until every one of these holds, each shown ticked or said in words with a link to put it right (`readiness`, `src/web/pack-approve.ts`): a surveyor assigned; the property chosen; the sums complete; the legal check allows it (`legalAllows`); **every built chapter opened by the assigned surveyor** (4, 7, 9, 10 today; `chaptersToRead`, `src/web/pack-chapters.ts`) — and chapter 7 again after anyone else last answered the legal check; the RICS number on file; the job's locked estimate. A click that gets past the grey button is refused with the first thing missing.
- **One click** in one transaction: the pack and the legal check read again; both counters must match the page's; the whole report (`snapshotOf`: the surveyor, RICS number and firm, the declaration, the automation note, the property, the four values with their sources, every line with its values and stamps, the sums, the surveyor's changes, the legal verdict and answers) written as JSON with keys in a fixed order (`canonical`); its SHA-256 is the fingerprint; kept as the next version; then `commitFigures` (or `amendFigures` after the first, reason "Survey pack version N approved.") so the claim reads the figures as before — the method note carries the version and fingerprint. Anything refused or failing undoes the lot. Audit `SURVEY_PACK_APPROVED` (the version and fingerprint).
- **Writes** migration 072: `survey_pack_reads` (who opened which chapter, at which legal-check counter); `surveyor_details` (RICS number, firm; never edited or deleted); `survey_pack_approvals` (version, both counters, the JSON, the fingerprint, who and when; one version per number and per state of the pack; **never edited or deleted**).
- **The declaration** four sentences, pre-filled and honest (Lakhbir chose "Pre-filled, honest wording"): reviewed to the extent the report sets out; Cap Rebate drafted the figures from the evidence listed, each saying where it came from; checked and changed any not agreed, each change marked with the surveyor's name; adopted as their own professional opinion. The automation note goes at the end of the report.
- **Who** opening a chapter counts only for the assigned surveyor (nobody else's is written down). Approving and the RICS number: the assigned surveyor only. Admin and Sales see the Sign chapter and the versions without a button.
- **Accept** `test/pack-approve.test.ts` (12): the fingerprint the same whatever order the keys were made in and different for a penny; grey and every gap in words; the RICS number; only the surveyor's reading counts, and chapter 7 again after Sales answers; ready, and nothing approved before the click; nobody else may approve; version 1 with its fingerprint, every stamp kept, the commitment written and the estimate released; a second click and an out-of-date page (the pack's or the legal check's) refused; a change then version 2 by amendment, version 1 unchanged; versions never edited or deleted, and a copy altered underneath shown as altered; eight clicks at once give one version; a commitment the job refuses keeps nothing. 34 single-line mutations each turn it red.

### The survey pack — 2 October 2026, stage 4 of 6 (the client's report, the accountant's sign-off, then the client)
- **Why** Lakhbir: the report goes to the client "visually appealing"; asked how it travels: "Adviser, then client"; our own adviser "Staff record it, as today"; the client's accountant by "Email + private sign-off link"; the design as mocked up, "Yes, build it as shown".
- **The report** (`src/web/survey-report.ts`): one set of contents (`reportModel`, from the approved version and its fingerprint), drawn twice — a page (`reportHtml`) and a PDF (`reportPdf`). Five parts: the cover (who for, who by with the RICS number, the spend that qualifies and its two pools as a bar, the building's key facts, the approval); how the figure is worked out (price → land → building → rebuild cost → what qualifies, the sum in full, the two pools, the four values each with its source); item by item (each value marked Evidence, Our draft or Surveyor, a bar for its share, the items checked and not in the building); the legal check and what happens next; the declaration, name, RICS number, time, the full fingerprint and "How this report was made". Its colours are fixed, so it looks the same in the back office, the portal and on the accountant's (dark-themed) page. Since stage 6 (2 Oct 2026; brought across by Phase 153f) the cover also shows the **site plan** when the version has one: the client's building dark, the buildings round it grey, from Ordnance Survey's free outlines, with OS's credit line and the words "not the legal boundary of the site"; the INSPIRE boundary never appears.
- **The PDF** (`src/web/pdf-canvas.ts`, `src/web/pdf-metrics.ts`): no dependency — boxes, rounded boxes, lines, circles and text in Helvetica, Helvetica-Bold and Times-Bold, placed exactly with each character's real width (read from metric-identical fonts, checked against Adobe's figures). The same bytes every time, so it is never stored: it is made again from the kept version.
- **The order** (`src/web/report-signoff.ts`): the surveyor approves → a tax adviser signs off → the client has it. Chapter 23's **Send the report** panel shows the three steps. Our own adviser's sign-off is recorded on the claim as before. The client's accountant is emailed the PDF and a private link (`sendForSignoff`), by Admin or Sales, only to the company's own Accountant or Adviser contact with an address, with how the client gave permission in words; sending follows every email's rules (the switch, the test website only, never to an invented or do-not-contact address). On the link (`answerLink`) they sign off — name, a firm ending Limited, LLP or plc, the title they sign under, and a tick — or send it back with a reason, once, within 30 days. A signed answer is put on the claim by Admin or Sales (`recordAnswer`) through the claim's own `recordSignOff`, once. The client sees a version (`releasedVersion`) only once a sign-off **of that version's figure** is on the claim, recorded after it was approved, and only while its fingerprint holds; Admin or Sales then tell them by a portal message (`tellClient`), once a version.
- **Writes** migration 073: `pack_signoff_requests` (the version, the contact, the address, the link's SHA-256, how the client gave permission, the email, who and when, when the link runs out), `pack_signoff_answers` (once a request: signed with name, firm and title, or sent back with a reason — the database refuses a signed answer with no firm), `pack_signoff_recorded` (an answer and the claim's sign-off it became — once each way), `pack_client_notices` (once a version). None is ever edited or deleted. The outbox's never-edited rule lets a sent `REPORT_FOR_SIGNOFF` email's words be replaced, like a sign-in code's. Audit `REPORT_SENT_FOR_SIGNOFF`, `REPORT_SIGNOFF_ANSWERED` (ids, version and outcome — never the link, never a figure). (No table name says "report": none of them stores a report.)
- **Accept** `test/survey-report.test.ts` (14): exact widths and wrapping, the same bytes every time, every cross-reference landing on its object, spacing never leaking; the page and the PDF saying the same figures, sources, declaration and fingerprint; who sees the report; who may send it, to whom, with what; the email's PDF the report's own, only the hash kept, the words removed once sent; the link (no login, wrong ones open nothing, once, 30 days, the firm, the title and the tick, a reason to send back); recorded on the claim once, by Admin or Sales; the client sees nothing before, then the page, the PDF and the card, and another client nothing; told once; a later version with another figure not released by a sign-off of the first figure; a version whose fingerprint fails never shown, signed or downloaded; nothing new edited or deleted. 33 single-line mutations: 32 turned it red; the other (a byte count that equals the character count for this text) changes nothing.

### The survey pack — 2 October 2026, stage 5 of 6 (the client shows us the building: a photo checklist and a recorded call)
- **Why** Lakhbir: "a recorded phone walkthrough … and add the option for … a guided photo checklist in the client portal". Asked: the photos are **kept, for testing** ("Keep them, test only": 7 years, encrypted, opened by Admin, Sales, the DPO, the client and the assigned surveyor — `SURVEY_PHOTOS` in `src/web/survey-evidence.ts`); the walkthrough is a **recorded Zoom call** ("Recorded Zoom call"), because the test website takes at most 4.5 MB a request and a page may run no script; the design as mocked up, "Yes, build it as shown".
- **The checklist** (`src/web/survey-checklist.ts`): made from the sector each time, in the filming guide's order — outside, the plant room, the electrics, comms and data, water, each room type of the sector (`src/web/rooms.ts`), corridors and safety, the kitchen, the ceiling and floor voids. Each item says what to photograph and which fixture-list lines it is evidence for. Nothing about the list is stored; only what the client did.
- **A photo** (`src/web/survey-evidence.ts`, `src/web/evidence-pages.ts`): sent by a client of that company, one at a time, with the tick "nobody is in this photo". A JPEG or PNG known by its first bytes, at most 4.4 MB. Its hidden details are taken out before it is kept (a JPEG's EXIF with the place and phone, XMP, IPTC, other application blocks and comments, keeping the colour profile; a PNG's text, EXIF and time chunks); the picture itself is copied as it was. Then it is **encrypted** with the store's key (`CAPREBATE_ORIGINALS_KEY`, AES-256-GCM, `putSealed` under `survey-photos/`). With no key it is refused, and the page says why. Taken back by the client, or by Admin or Sales with a reason, it is shown to nobody. An item can be marked "not in our building" or "not safe or not ours"; the newest mark is the item's.
- **Who sees a photo** the client of that company, the assigned surveyor, Admin and Sales (`surveyor_job.read`); nobody else.
- **The walkthrough call** the client asks for one in the portal (a message to us). **Admin or Sales books it** at one of their free times — they own client calls in the diary; a surveyor is kept out of it on purpose (`booking.read`). Booked, it is this job's walkthrough, so its Zoom meeting is made with `auto_recording: cloud` (an ordinary call's is `none`). After the call, the assigned surveyor, Admin or Sales fetches the recording from Zoom (`GET /v2/meetings/{id}/recordings`): its link (only a zoom.us one), passcode and length are kept; the video stays with Zoom. Opening it is recorded. Zoom's plan must have cloud recording, and the Server-to-Server app the recording scope.
- **How the surveyor inspected** desktop with the client's evidence, a live video survey, or a site visit — said by the assigned surveyor in **chapter 2, Your inspection** (now built, and one of the chapters read before approval). Approval waits for it. It goes into the approved version and the report ("How the building was inspected"), with how many photos and minutes of recording the surveyor had.
- **Each line's evidence** chapter 10 shows each line's photos as a chip, linking to them.
- **Writes** migration 074: `survey_photos`, `survey_photo_withdrawals`, `survey_checklist_marks`, `survey_walkthrough_asks`, `survey_walkthroughs`, `survey_recordings`, `survey_recording_views`, `survey_inspection_basis` — none ever edited or deleted. Audit kind `SURVEY_EVIDENCE_ADDED`.
- **Accept** `test/survey-evidence.test.ts` (21): JPEG and PNG details taken out and the picture kept; only images, at most 4.4 MB, only with the tick, only from that company's client; kept encrypted and never in the clear, refused with no key; who sees it; taken back; marks; each line's photos; the call asked for once, booked only by Admin or Sales, its meeting recording to the cloud, the recording fetched once and only a Zoom link, opening it recorded; the basis only by the assigned surveyor; the portal pages, a photo sent through the page, the pack's chapter 2, the photos page and the chip; nothing edited or deleted. `test/survey-report.test.ts` now checks the inspection line in the page and the PDF. 56 single-line mutations: 50 turned it red; the other six change nothing a test can see (a length check the loop's end already catches, twice; three refusals `packJob` already makes; a filter over a group with no building items).

### The survey pack — 2 October 2026: photos sent many at once, sorted by Admin or Sales
- **Why** Lakhbir: "give users the ability to not only upload photos individually but also in one go. admin can then split and upload photos as this is more user friendly". Asked who sorts: "Admin and Sales"; the mock-up: "Yes, build it as shown".
- **The client** (`addPhotos` in `src/web/survey-evidence.ts`; the "Send lots of photos at once" box in `src/web/evidence-pages.ts`): up to 15 photos in one send (`manyFiles` in `src/web/multipart.ts`), with one "nobody is in any of these photos", up to **4.4 MB in all** — the test website's limit is for the whole send, and a page may run no script to split it, so this is often one or two full-size phone photos. Each is checked and cleaned like a single photo; if any is refused, none is kept and the words say which ("Photo 2: …"). A send our own server finds too big is refused in words; on the test website, Vercel stops one over 4.5 MB before it reaches us. They are kept, encrypted, under the item key `UNSORTED`, and the portal says how many wait.
- **Admin or Sales** (`sortPhotos`; `/staff/survey/{job}/pack/sort`): every waiting photo with a list of the checklist's items in groups, "Leave for now" and "Not useful: take it back"; one Save for all. Every choice is checked first; a wrong one saves nothing. Each move is a row in `survey_photo_sorts` (migration 075, never edited or deleted); the newest is the photo's item, so a photo can be moved again. Audit kind `SURVEY_PHOTOS_SORTED` (counts only). A sorted photo counts towards its item, the chapter 10 chips and the report like any other; an unsorted one counts towards none. Chapter 2 shows "Sent all at once, not sorted yet" with **Sort them** for Admin and Sales.
- **Accept** `test/survey-evidence.test.ts` (now 26): the reader; who sends, the tick, 15 and 4.4 MB, all or none; kept cleaned, encrypted and unsorted; only Admin or Sales sort, all or nothing, the newest move wins, the same move twice is not a move, one taken back; the portal box, sending through it, too big in words, the sorting page, chapter 2's row. 25 single-line mutations, all 25 red.

### The survey pack — 2 October 2026: full-size photos sent straight to the file store, locked on the phone
- **Why** Lakhbir asked whether the test website's 4.5 MB a send could be raised. It cannot: it is Vercel's fixed limit on a request to the website. "go with option 2" (each photo straight to the private file store); asked how it travels: "Lock on the phone"; the limits: "100 photos, 25 MB each"; the mock-up: "Yes, build it as shown".
- **The page** "Send lots of photos at once" gains one script of our own (`src/web/photo-upload-script.ts`, served at `/assets/photo-upload.js`). Only this page's security policy allows it (`photoPageCsp` in `src/web/evidence-pages.ts`: `script-src 'self'`, and `connect-src` adds `https://vercel.com` on the test website); every other page is unchanged. The script sets no style and writes no markup. It shows each photo's state and a count. Without it the box posts as before (15 photos, 4.4 MB a send).
- **The lock** For each send the server makes a one-time RSA-2048 key pair. The phone gets the public half; the private half is kept in the database, encrypted with the store's key (`sealBytes`). For each photo the phone makes a fresh AES-256-GCM key, locks the photo with it, and locks the key with the public half (RSA-OAEP, SHA-256): `CBUP1`, the locked key's length, the locked key, the nonce, the photo and its tag. Vercel never holds a photo it can read.
- **The way** (`src/web/photo-upload.ts`) start a send (a client of the company, the tick, 1 to 100 photos, the key set) → a slot per photo (one's own send only, in range, once each, at most 25 MB locked, half an hour) whose address on the test website is a **Blob presigned PUT** for exactly that file, private, size-capped (`presignPut` in `src/web/shared-objectstore.ts`, @vercel/blob 2.8 `issueSignedToken` + `presignUrl`, signed in by the project's OIDC connection; an address on any other site is refused), and on any other copy our own route → the phone sends it → done: the server reads it, opens it, checks and cleans it as any photo (JPEG or PNG, at most 25 MB, hidden details out), keeps it encrypted as not yet sorted, and removes the locked upload whatever happens. Each slot's outcome is recorded once: KEPT, REFUSED or ABANDONED.
- **The sweep** an upload not finished within a day is removed and recorded ABANDONED (`sweepUploads`): at the next send, and whenever the outside timer wakes the site (`/hooks/read-replies`).
- **Writes** migration 076: `survey_photos` rebuilt with the photo limit at 25 MB (every row copied, its index and triggers made again, the foreign-key guard at the foot); `survey_upload_sessions`, `survey_upload_slots`, `survey_upload_outcomes` — none ever edited or deleted.
- **Not yet proven against Vercel itself** the Blob presigned PUT is built to @vercel/blob 2.8's own code and tested against a store that answers as its SDK does; the first real send on the test website is its proof. It needs `CAPREBATE_ORIGINALS_KEY` set there (it is not, 2 Oct 2026: photos are refused in words until it is).
- **Accept** `test/photo-upload.test.ts` (16), the page's own script locking each photo in Node's Web Crypto: the lock opens only with its send's private half (a changed byte, another send's key or a strange file opens nothing; a fresh random key each photo); the private half kept only encrypted; who may start, slot, send and finish; done keeps the photo cleaned and encrypted, removes the upload, answers twice the same; refusals recorded once; the Blob address exactly scoped and never another site's; the sweep, by a new send and by the timer; the page's policy and script, and no other page's; a whole send over HTTP; 25 MB fits; nothing edited or deleted. 38 single-line mutations: 36 red; the other two change nothing (a key-length check the decryption already fails, a size check the slot's own cap makes unreachable). Driven in Chromium on the demo: four 6 MB photos in one send, all kept; a HEIC file skipped in words.
- **2 October 2026, after the first real send.** On the test website every photo of the first send failed with "The website did not answer". The logs could not be read from the session, so: (1) every step of a send that fails now ANSWERS IN WORDS with what went wrong (`failureWords`: the error's kind and message, any web address and token-like run taken out, at most 200 characters) and writes the same to the log (`photo upload: …`); (2) when the store gives no one-time address, the photo comes through our own route instead (its slot's key ends `.site.lock`, the only such slot our route accepts on the test website), at most 4.4 MB a photo there; a bigger one is refused in words with the reason. The end of a send that kept nothing is shown in red, and the iPhone hint only follows a photo skipped for its type. Three tests added (16); 8 single-line mutations, all red.

### The survey pack, stage 6 — 2 October 2026: the free lookups on the test website, and the report's site plan (brought across from an unmerged branch by Phase 153f, 4 October 2026)
- **Why** Stage 6 of the survey pack: "switch the lookups on in test" and the Ordnance Survey building outline on the report's cover. Lakhbir, asked which lookups: "All the free ones"; asked how the client's building is chosen: "Automatic".
- **Built — the lookups.** `TEST_WEBSITE_LOOKUPS` (`src/connectors/registers.ts`): on the test website only (`onTestWebsite`, or a copy started with `CAPREBATE_LOOKUPS_HERE=1`), the energy certificate (EPC), price paid (HMLR_PPD), planning (PLANNING) and the care regulator (CQC) are switched on and their free gate is waived, under a recorded waiver (`waiver_record`), as the postcode check's is. `REGISTER_SWITCHES` is unchanged, every one still off; the paid lookups, Companies House's register lookups, the boundary and the ONS table are not touched; a lookup that needs the signed engagement still waits for it, and a client who chose to answer the questions themselves is still not looked up. `switchesHere` and `freeGateFor` (`src/web/research.ts`) carry it into the research panel and "Run the research". On the test website the care regulator's council area comes from postcodes.io (only the postcode is sent), because the ONS postcode table is not there. Price paid is homes only, so it is asked only for supported living, as before. The test website now reads `EPC_API_TOKEN`, `CQC_SUBSCRIPTION_KEY` and `OS_DATA_HUB_API_KEY` from its settings (`api/index.mjs`, `TEST_DEPLOY_KEYS`). Before real visitors: publish the privacy notice, then take the waiver out.
- **Built — the site plan.** `src/connectors/os-buildings.ts` (a new service, `OS_FEATURES`, `https://api.os.uk`, UK, ten calls a minute) and `src/web/site-plan.ts`, migration 083 (`site_plans`; written as 078 on the unmerged branch, renumbered by Phase 153f). Chapter 9 has a "Site plan for the report" card and one button for the assigned surveyor. The postcode goes to postcodes.io for its point; a 160 m square goes to Ordnance Survey's Features API for OS Open Zoomstack's local buildings (free, the OS OpenData plan; the key in a header, never in the address). The client's building is the one the point falls in, else the nearest (said with how far). Outlines are kept in decimetres from the point. At approval the plan is copied into the version (`site_plan` in its JSON, only when there is one), so an approved report never changes; the cover draws it as an SVG on the page (presentation attributes only) and as shapes in the PDF (`pdf-canvas.ts` gained `poly`, even-odd fill). OS's credit line, "Contains OS data © Crown copyright and database right 2026", and "not the legal boundary of the site" go with it. No coordinate or postcode is written to the audit chain (`SITE_PLAN_FETCHED`: counts only). The Land Registry boundary (INSPIRE) is never drawn anywhere.
- **Accept** `test/survey-stage6.test.ts` (11): the waiver holds only on the test website (production or preview) or with the opt-in, for the four only, the shipped switches unchanged; the research run on the test website reaches EPC, planning and CQC (its council area from postcodes.io) and nothing else, and anywhere else sends nothing; the client's building (inside, nearest, a courtyard is not inside); who may fetch, only on the test website, only with a key and a postcode; what each call sends; refusals in words with nothing kept; the plan in the approved version, on the page and in the PDF, unchanged by a later fetch; chapter 9 and its route; nothing edited or deleted.

### Show us your building — 2 October 2026: redesigned, and a video
- **Why** Lakhbir: "The UI is horrible, everything seems everywhere and there no distinction around being able to send a video ... we need to add an option of upload video." The mock-up was agreed with one change: "item by item needs a 'i dont have this' which takes it from the list when clicked". Limits: "10 minutes, 1 GB each". Kept: "Same as photos".

Photos keeps "send lots at once" and the checklist; "I don't have this" takes an item off the list (an item with a photo stays), with a link to show the hidden ones. A video (`src/web/survey-video.ts`, migration 077): the phone checks the length, cuts the video into 4 MB pieces, locks each one as it locks a photo, and sends each straight to the file store (or through our own route when the store gives no address). Each piece is opened and kept encrypted under `survey-videos/<video>/<n>`. When all have come, it must be an MP4 or MOV of at most 10 minutes; the place it was filmed (ISO 6709 in the header) is blanked digit for digit; it is kept. Played back by range, never whole, so no answer is over 4.5 MB. Seen by the client, the assigned surveyor, Admin and Sales; taken back by the client, or Admin or Sales with why. A send not finished in a day is removed by the next send or the reply timer. None of the six new tables is edited or deleted.

### The left-hand menu — 2 October 2026: UI tidy phase 2 of 17
- **Why** Lakhbir: "Admin's 'Survey jobs' is hidden under 'More'. It took me a while to find." He chose design 2, a menu down the left in named groups, and asked to "think about sub menus within the main menus on the left hand side for example someone clicks on a client - what does the left menu look like on certain pages".
- **Built** The back office's and the portal's header is now a black menu down the left from 900 px: the name (the portal keeps its logo, on white), Admin's "Home shows" choice, the search, the sections in named groups (table above), then Help and the user menu at the foot; it stays in view as the page scrolls. The current section is mint with black words. More is gone. **A menu that opens out**: under the current section, the entries a page names for itself (`setSubMenu` in `src/web/navigation.ts`, for this request only, escaped, with an optional title such as a client's name); with none named, Settings opens out to every setting the login's gates admit, the open one marked. Later phases name a client's claims and sections, a survey job's parts and Finance's. **Under 900 px** the menu folds to a black bar with the name and a **Menu** button: a checkbox and its label, read by the stylesheet (`:has(.navtoggle:checked)`), so no script; the button shows keyboard focus. The header's markup keeps its order (`.util` is laid out with `display: contents`), every gate and address is as it was, and nothing outside `body.app` changed. `tools/ui-shots.mjs` gains `SCREEN=1` (one screen high) and a plan entry's `click`.
- **Accept** `test/ui-tidy-menu.test.ts` (13): each login's groups exactly, in order, no More; a switched-off login is listed nothing; the client's two entries and no groups; Settings opens out on a setting's page with that one marked, on Settings itself with none marked, and nowhere else; a page's own entries replace the default, are escaped, last one request, and an empty list draws nothing; every header ends with one Menu button and no script; the layout's rules from 900 px and under it, focus, mint with black words, every rule under `body.app`, nothing under 12 px. `test/phase135.test.ts`'s menus and its More test, and `test/phase133a.test.ts`'s More check, now read the new menu. 20 single-line mutations, all red (two survived at first: a gate dropped from the menu, and untitled entries ignored; a test for each was added).

### The Pipeline as one table — 2 October 2026: UI tidy phase 3 of 17
- **Why** Lakhbir chose design 2 for the pipeline, "one table", with "colours and darker boxes to make this easier to read especially on the pipeline". At laptop width the board of columns ran off the right edge.
- **Route** `GET /staff/pipeline` is now the table; `?q=` finds by business, site or claim number and `?show=late|mine|nobody` narrows it (anything else shows all). `GET /staff/pipeline?layout=board` is the board of columns, as it was. A move's `back` is one fixed word: `board` returns to the table, `columns` (new) to the board; the return address joins `done=moved` with `&` when it already holds a `?` (it gave `?layout=board?done=moved`, found by the test).
- **Built** `pipelineTable` in `src/web/pipeline-pages.ts`: four boxes (in progress; next step late and nobody owns it, each a link to its filter; on hold), the bar of stage groups, then one table in the five groups (`src/web/look.ts`), each opened by its coloured band naming its stages and count, then On hold, Stopped and No step set; the latest next step first in each group. A row: the business (the link), the site without the company's name (`siteOf`) and the claim number; the stage in its colour; the next step with the **Move to …** button under it; due (late in red, "No date" in grey); the owner or "Nobody yet"; the range, only where the login may read it. **Record a lead** only where `lead.write` allows. `BoardCard` gains `stage_key`, `owner_id` and `next_action_due` (the next-step task's due day), which `view.json` now carries too. On a phone each row is a card. The guide's `B21` and its Help say so. Nothing was taken away: the board, its columns' owner and needs, and every Move button are one click away.
- **Accept** `test/ui-tidy-pipeline.test.ts` (11): every claim exactly one row, under its own group's band, groups in order with their counts; each row's link, site, stage colour, owner, late / no date / no next step, Move button; a late next step red, read straight from the task (one made late whatever the date); latest first; the boxes count from the same data and two open their filters; Late, Mine, Nobody owns it and All each exactly their claims, the current one marked, an unknown filter is All; search by number and by name, keeping the filter, escaped, with a way back; `siteOf`; the board one click away, a move returns to its own view, a refused one goes back to it; Record a lead only for Sales and Admin; the range only where it may be read; Accounting and Surveying still refused. `test/phase133a.test.ts` reads the board at `?layout=board`. `test/ui-tidy-harness.ts` (not a test) starts the app and signs each login in, for phases 3 to 17. 19 single-line mutations, all red (one survived at first, the due date dropped from the data: the test now reads it from the task).

### Phase 160f — 20 October 2026: the public notices on the test website, their facts proposed, and the complaint form
- **Why** Phase 160e's stress test found /privacy and /notices saying the notices "are being prepared" while the forms took names, emails and phone numbers. Lakhbir: "Build and publish on test" (the four facts nobody had supplied filled with our recommendation, marked for the data protection officer), and "Check the database first" on where the website runs: the Vercel dashboard (20 Oct 2026) shows the Turso database in US East (Virginia) and the Blob store in IAD1, with the website's code, so nothing moves and the notice says so.
- **Built** **The page** (`03-app/caprebate-notices-page.html`): the two dates, the legal basis (legitimate interests, Article 6(1)(f)) and the retention period (seven years from the end of the claim's accounting period, or one year after an enquiry closes) filled, and Vercel, its Blob store and Turso named as the test website's host, file store and database, in the United States, each marked "Proposed, awaiting our data protection officer" (eight marks); its inline style attributes moved to classes; `#privacy` and `#complaints` anchors for the footer's links. `caprebate-notices-acceptance.cjs` lists the proposed facts, and `--live` refuses the page until the officer confirms each. **Served on the test website only** (`src/web/notices-site.ts`; createApp's `testNotices`, passed by api/index.mjs alone): `/notices/` with a draft banner, its style as `/assets/notices.css`, no long dash in its words (`plainDashes`), refused if anything inline appears; `/privacy` goes to `/notices/#privacy`. Anywhere else the placeholder stays. **The complaint form** (`POST /notices/complaint`, migration 118 `complaint_forms`): kept at once with when it arrived, emailed to data.protection@caprebate.co.uk, slowed from one network address (`COMPLAINT_SOURCE_LIMIT`, 10 in the window), and listed at the top of the staff complaints page until a member of staff logs it into the complaints log, received by FORM at the time it arrived (`logFormComplaint`, `POST /staff/compliance/complaint-forms/{id}/log`).
- **Accept** `test/phase160f.test.ts` (fourteen tests; the code was broken on purpose ten ways and nine were caught: the tenth removed the compliance-write check from `logFormComplaint`, which `logComplaint` makes again).

### Phase 160e — 19 October 2026: the stress test's fixes
- **Why** Lakhbir, 19 October 2026: "stress test the website, engine, reports, and code plus website content plus portal log ins; ensure everything is working and correct before deployment". The stress test of `main` at 85cb245 (report and scripts: OneDrive, Health Capital Allowances/Business Development/Research/Caprebate stress test 19 Oct 2026) passed every suite, about 42,000 engine walks, the assessment's walks and load, 41 sign-in checks, the cross-client check and a 1,766-page crawl, and found four things to fix.
- **Built** **Documents over 4.5 MB** (`src/web/upload-pieces.ts`, `src/web/upload-pieces-script.ts`, migration 117, `117-phase160e-upload-pieces.sql`; Phase 160d also wrote a 117, and both stay: migrations are applied by file name, in order, and the test website may already have recorded this one by its name). Vercel takes at most 4.5 MB a request, and a 6 MB document sent to the test website got Vercel's own "Request Entity Too Large" page while the portal says "The most we take is 50 MB". A form marked `data-pieces` now names `/assets/upload-pieces.js`: a chosen file over 4 MB (`PIECED_ABOVE`) is sent first in pieces of 3 MB (`POST /upload/pieces/start`, then `PUT /upload/pieces/{send}/{n}`), each kept SEALED in the store's incoming area, and the form is then sent as before with its file box empty and the send's id in `pieces`. The form's own handler takes the file (`readUpload` for the portal's documents, the staff client files, claim documents and new versions; `readPortalSend` for a message's file) and does everything it did before: the same checks, the same `receiveFile`. A send is one login's, used once, every piece the size it said, and swept with its pieces after a day (`sweepPieces`, at each new start). With no script the form sends the file whole, as before. Every page writer that drew `HTML_HEADERS` now passes the page through `piecesPage`, which adds the script and a policy allowing it to send to this site only (`PIECES_PAGE_CSP`) on a page with such a form, and leaves every other page as it was. **Sign-in from one network address** (`src/auth.ts`): `LOGIN_SOURCE_LIMIT` (30 in the 15-minute window) and `RESET_SOURCE_LIMIT` (10) as well as the per-email limits; the stress test's 100 wrong sign-ins on 100 addresses from one machine were never slowed. The address is hashed before it is a key and never written down. `api/index.mjs` sets `CAPREBATE_TRUST_PROXY` (Vercel overwrites `X-Forwarded-For`), so the address read on the test website, and the one on a signature, is the visitor's and not Vercel's. **A form from another website** (`handleWeb`, `src/web/router.ts`): a POST a browser marks `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site` is refused (403) before anything is read; the Origin header cannot be used, as our no-referrer policy makes browsers send "Origin: null" on our own forms. **The home page's four long dashes** (`09-app/site/home.html`), which `page()`'s rule (Phase 160c) does not reach.
- **Accept** `test/phase160e.test.ts` (seventeen tests; the code was broken on purpose fifteen ways and thirteen were caught: the two missed each took the script off a page writer that draws no upload form, `staff.ts`'s and `messages-pages.ts`'s, so there was nothing on a page to miss). Four older test files restated where they said no page carries a script or a policy of its own: `phase133a`, `phase138` and `ui-tidy-menu` allow exactly the one external `<script src="/assets/upload-pieces.js" defer>` tag, `phase133a` and `photo-upload` expect `PIECES_PAGE_CSP` on a page with an upload form.

### Phase 160d — 19 October 2026: no em dashes in the emails, the letter and reports going forward, the text the system writes, and the export's column names
- **Why** Carried by Phase 160c: Lakhbir's "remove the — AI uses" reaches every word a person reads, not only the screens.
- **Premise re-derived (step 8)** Every email goes through one writer (`outboxTransport`, `src/stubs/email.ts`), so the emails are done there rather than string by string, which also reaches the templates Admin edits. Only two real PDFs exist: the signed engagement letter, kept as fixed bytes at signing (`engagement_signatures.pdf`, never redrawn), and the survey report, drawn again from its approved version every time it is opened, so changing its words in code would change how an approved or signed-off version reads. No hash binds the rendered words (the report's fingerprint is of its data). No professional has signed off the letter's or the report's wording (SDP-L3, ACC-C1, SV-S4 are all awaiting review).
- **Built** **Emails**: `outboxTransport.send` passes the subject and body through `plainDashLines` (`src/web/plain-dashes.ts`, the page rule line by line) for every kind but `FREE_TYPED`, a staff member's own words; attached files are not touched. **The letter**: `loadLetter` (`src/web/letter.ts`) draws the paragraphs without long dashes, as the client already read it on screen since 160c, so the screen and the signed PDF agree; whether it is the test copy and which specimens it holds are read from the document's own words first; the Word document is not changed, and the solicitor is told (FOR THE SOLICITOR, dated note at the end, on SDP-L3); a letter already signed is never redrawn. The clause label `clause N — Title` keeps its dash: it is a key other code matches, and it reaches a person only through a screen or an email, which drop it. **The report**: a version approved from now on carries `words: 'plain'` in its snapshot (`snapshotOf`, `src/web/pack-approve.ts`); `reportModel` (`src/web/survey-report.ts`) then passes every string of the model through the rule (`plainStrings`), and `reportPdf` draws on a canvas that does the same to the words written there (`plainCanvas`); a version approved before carries no `words` and is drawn exactly as made, in the PDF and on screen, where `reportHtml` marks it `data-as-made` and the page rule leaves that element alone (nesting counted). **Text the system writes**: the generated task titles (the Disposal Note task, with `DISPOSAL_TASK_TITLE_BEFORE` still matched so an open task raised under the old title is found and no second is raised; the clause 13 fee task; a lead's call log; the seeded invoice-stage task) and the "Ask for" reasons in `engaged.ts`; `companyFirst` (`templates.ts`) puts the company first with a colon. **Migration 117**: the seeded template's subject loses its dash, only while it is still exactly as migration 032 left it. **The export**: the column "Estimated value: the figure shown" (`lists.ts`; the guide and the in-app Help). **The calendar file**: "…, or in the Zoom app".
- **Accept** `test/phase160d.test.ts` (ten tests) and two in `test/survey-report.test.ts` (a new version's model, page and PDF without a long dash; an older version's model, PDF and page exactly as made); the code was broken on purpose eight ways and each was caught. Restated: `automation` (two subjects), `email-sending` (a plain-ASCII subject is not encoded), `video-calls`, `engaged`, `lists`, `phase134` (three), `phase142a`, `phase153d` (the test copy read from the document's own words).

### Phase 160c — 19 October 2026: the home page's branding, the text tidied, no em dashes on screen
- **Why** Lakhbir, 19 October 2026, with a screenshot of the portal on the test website: "polish the back end up, the text format looks all wrong and remove the — AI uses ensure our branding from the home page matches the back end".
- **Built** **The home page's branding** (the shared block in `SITE_CSS`, under `body.crm`): the menu down the left is the home page's ink (`--side` #121614) with its ivory words (`--ivory` #EDEAE2), hover `--side-2`, quiet words `--side-mute`; the current page and every main button are Cap Mint pills with ink words, as the home page's "Am I eligible?"; headings in the brand's heading type (Bookman Old Style / Roboto Slab), figures kept in the body type with lining numerals (the heading type's old-style 0 reads as an o); the page a pale mint-grey (`--page`), green-grey rules and column headings. The navy tokens and pairings are gone. **The mark**: `page()` draws the home page's mark and wordmark (`PUBLIC_MARK`, still, not animated) at the top of the menu for the back office ("Back office" under the name) and the portal ("Client portal"); the supplied lockup is no longer drawn on these pages (it is still served). The working pages stay light on purpose, so long tables read easily. **The text**: Help level with the name (`margin:0;align-self:center`, undoing UI tidy phase 2's `margin-top:auto`); the name not underlined; the account menu's role in normal weight; Sign out spaced like the other entries (`form>button` lost its `margin-top`). **No em dashes on screen**: `plainDashes` (`src/web/plain-dashes.ts`), run by `page()` on every page it draws, changes each long dash in the words a person reads (text, and the title, alt, placeholder and aria-label attributes) by seven rules: a dash that is all an element holds reads "None"; two around an aside become commas; before a joining word or a number, a comma; before a short ending (three words or fewer), a comma; after a colon in the same sentence, a comma; at the end of a run, a colon; otherwise a colon. A run that starts with its dash pulls its mark against the word before. Never inside a script, style, text box, `pre` or `code`, and never in an attribute a form sends back (value, name, href).
- **Not changed here, on purpose** — the engagement letter, the reports, the PDFs and the emails (their versions are fixed or they are not drawn by `page()`), the text the system writes and stores (a task's title shown in its edit box), and an export's own column names shown as code in Help. They are Phase 160d.
- **Accept** `test/phase160c.test.ts` (fourteen tests; the code was broken on purpose eight ways and each break was caught). Thirty-seven older test files were restated: 56 assertions that pinned a long dash in a page's words were rewritten by the same rule, and 12 that compared a page with a value from the data now pass that value through `plainDashText` or `plainDashes`; the rule's "short ending takes a comma" case was dropped the same day because it read one name two ways. It checks: the rules on real sentences; None, the colon against the word before; script, style, text box, code and form attributes left alone; a crawl of up to sixty pages from each login's home with no long dash in what the rule changes; the public pages; the home page's ink, mint pills and the brand's type in the stylesheet; Help level, the name not underlined, the role plain, Sign out spaced; no navy left. Restated: `test/phase160a.test.ts` (the ink tokens in place of the navy; the mark and wordmark), `test/phase160b.test.ts` (the portal's mark), `test/phase133c.test.ts` (the portal and the back office draw the home page's mark).

Help and the user menu left it. **The top bar**: `Shell.bar` for the portal, `crumbRoot('/portal')`, the trail (`trail`, now taking any `{ label, href }`: Your claims or Messages, then the page's title), Help and the user menu; no search, as before. ### Phase 160a — 19 October 2026: the back office in the sidebar layout Lakhbir chose

Under 900 px the sidebar folds behind the Menu button as before, and the top bar shows the trail, Help and the user menu on one line and the search under them. **Staff only**: the client portal and the public pages keep their look (`body.staffapp` is drawn only when the shell's home is `/staff`). ### Phase 159l — 10 October 2026: what the second Lockwood run found, and two legal-check readings
- **Why** The second Lockwood Surgery run (10 October 2026; 04-phase-work/sim-2026-10-10-lockwood/, OneDrive, findings L13-L20) and two readings carried by Phase 159k (#9, #10). Each makes what the client and the accountant see match what the system knows.
- **The dormant flag before the letter (item 1, L13)** — migration 114: `assessments.company_number`, `company_status`, `company_accounts_type`, kept by `submit` (`assessment.ts`) from the funnel's own search result for the company picked (`company_results`); a type the search could not read is NULL, not known (N4). `companyAtAssessment` (`who-claims.ts`); `qualifyingActivityEvidence` and `dormantWarning` say it — "Companies House, from the company search at the assessment: …" — until the research's own CH_COMPANY register record exists, which then wins. Nothing refuses (rule 13(c)).
- **A filing per return (item 2, L15)** — `claimReturns` and `dueFromAmendBy` (`claim-route.ts`): one return per accounting period from each claimed part's route (W1 its own period, W2 the first open one); null where a claimed part names no period. Migration 115: `accountant_confirm_requests.period_end` (FILED only). `sendConfirmRequest` takes `period_end`: required where the claim goes into more than one return, set to the one return where there is one; a FILED link may go at CLOSED only for a claim with more than one return. `askFor`, the email, the link page and the claim page name the return. `returnFilings` (`accountant-confirm.ts`) and `filingRecordedNeed(db, claim, today)` (`claim-record.ts`, the defence pack's close): each return already due must be confirmed filed by its own link; a return not yet due is NOT_DUE and held in `aftercarePanel.returns_to_file` ("Returns still to be filed", with its due date); a link sent before, for the whole claim, counts for the earliest return only. One return or fewer: one answer, as before. **Our reading**, Word question 283 (the accountant). The staff route (the client has no accountant) was given the same by the Phase 160 sweep (below).
- **The research panel's postcode row (item 3, L18)** — `consultAllowed` (`research.ts`): the POSTCODES_IO row, where it waits, now says it is the postcode check at the address question (its own gate), and what the research's own country check did on that click (the country and the council area kept, or no answer).
- **The events' figures (item 4, L19)** — `approvedEventFigures` (`property-events.ts`): each event's figure from the newest approved version (the purchase's own qualifying amount; each work's by its event; a version with no work holds the purchase as the whole). `eventsBlock` (`crm-pages.ts`) shows "£X qualifies, as the survey approved it in version N." in place of the band; an event added after the version keeps the band.
- **The work's own costs on the report (item 5, L20)** — `snapshotOf`'s `events.works[].input_sources` (`pack-approve.ts`): each of the nine final-account values' source, frozen with the version. `workSources` (`survey-report.ts`): each work's nine values as rows of "Where each number came from", page and PDF, with the surveyor's name and reason; a version approved before says "Its source was not kept with this version."
- **BCIS's quarter in words (item 6, L17's remainder)** — `baseWords` (`bcis-data.ts`): "2026-01" is "January 2026", in the drafts' sources; the report reads an older version's "at 2026-01" the same way (`rateSourceWords`).
- **INSPIRE end to end (item 7)** — not run: no switch-on recorded since 9 October 2026 (the test lookup log holds three NO results that day). The Phase 160 sweep moved it to the parked land-boundary item (12830205914), whose trigger it shares: the site boundary comes from Homedata.
- **Two legal-check readings (item 8)** — `LEGAL_READINGS` gains `s187a_6b_statements_route` (#9) and `fvr_requires_past_owner_claim` (#10). `withLegalEngineReadings` (`pack-legal.ts`) sets the engine's two CB_CTA switches, runs, and puts them back in a finally, synchronously; `assessLegal` (and so `legalView` and the walk) runs inside it, `assessLegalUnder` names the readings for a test. The public estimate never runs inside it (the site harness asserts CB_CTA off). `LEGAL_ENGINE_SWITCHES` (`money-readings.ts`): `packSwitchReadings` reads both ON for the facts panel and the sign-off form. The walk enumerates G14ivne and G14st8 and was re-recorded (16,689 paths): of the paths that moved, 1,515 not known → clear, 183 → clear with a deadline, 206 → fails (a nominal or £60,000 election no longer masked by the requirement, or a lease-route failure); none moved from clear to worse. Word questions 284-285 (the tax adviser); ACC-H13 (13255820733).
- **Accept** `test/phase159l.test.ts` (thirteen tests; on the code before, every one fails); phase153b (#10 shown both ways under `assessLegalUnder`), phase159f (BCIS's quarter), phase159h (the readings) and phase159j (L14's switch list) restated; `test/fixtures/phase159h/legal-paths-gp-company.txt` re-recorded.

### Phase 159k — 10 October 2026: the remaining money items — the seller's allowance statement, and three readings
- **Why** Carried from Phases 159e and 159i: the buyer's structures allowance on a purchase ran on SBA_STATEMENT_HELD and SBA_QUALIFYING_EXPENDITURE, but the statement itself (s.270IA) had no document type; and three claim readings — the long-life asset test, land remediation relief for asbestos and s.33B — had no input in the pack.
- **The statement (item 1)** — migration 112 (056's and 099's rebuild): `SBA_ALLOWANCE_STATEMENT`, bound to its own receipt policy, carries a value, CLAIM_TEAM, RET-7. phase3: `DocType`, the segment kind `SBA_STATEMENT_FIGURES` (heading rules appended last), a `DOC_SPECS` entry (`sba_earliest_contract_date`, `sba_first_use_date`, `sba_qualifying_expenditure_pence`), `PERSON_REVIEW_BY_DOC_TYPE`, a receipt policy, the name-exclusion keep list. `recordSbaStatementHeld` (`claim-facts.ts`, called by `finishUpload` after a receipt): every claim the received statement is linked to records SBA_STATEMENT_HELD YES from the client's document, once. `DOCUMENTS_FOR.PURCHASE` lists it from 29 October 2018. **A re-derived premise:** the phase asked for it as a must-have on every purchase from 29 October 2018; most healthcare buildings pre-date the allowance and have no statement, so the new condition `SBA_ON_PURCHASE` (`mandatory-documents.ts`) makes it a must-have only where SBA_QUALIFYING_EXPENDITURE is recorded on a purchase from that date (s.270IA needs it only for the buyer's allowance); Word question 282.
- **The readings (item 2)** — `MoneyReadings` gains `long_life_test`, `asbestos_lrr`, `s33b_aggregation` (on in test; `PACK_SWITCH_OF` maps CB_CTA `s33b_aggregation`). Migration 113 widens `survey_pack_entries` (108's rebuild) with five optional values — `long_life` (the purchase's long-life main pool), and per piece of work `works_long_life`, `works_asbestos`, `works_repairs_if`, `works_if_replacement` (`WORKS_PART_FIELDS`, each a part of a value above; `WORKS_PART_TOO_BIG` where it is more) — and adds the claim fact ASBESTOS_AT_ACQUISITION. **s.33B** (`s33bDecisions`, `survey-pack.ts`): repairs on integral features within twelve months across the building's pieces of work, more than half the replacement cost, move (with their share of the on-costs) from the repairs to the special rate pool before VAT and grants. **Long-life** (`allowancesFor`): per return, the long-life parts entering it; over the s.99 limit (`long-life-and-remediation.ts`, £100,000 in proportion), they move from the main pool to the special rate pool; homes never (s.93); a period not known is not decided. **Land remediation relief** (CTA 2009 Part 14): a company, the asbestos there at acquisition, W1 — the spend and 50% more (`PartResult.lrr`, `PeriodResult.lrr_pence`), two return-entry rows, never in the allowances' total, and the asbestos kept out of the structures allowance's base. The parts are frozen with the version (`snapshot.readings_159k`, only where one is given). Word questions 279-281; ACC-H12 (13255545092).
- **The seventeen readings (item 3), re-read** — in the pack and on in test: #1, #11, #13, #25 (159i), #5 (159h, legal), #20 (s.33B, here), and the long-life test and asbestos relief (new). #9 and #10 can be built in the pack's legal check but need the switch held across the whole legal flow, the two questions' showIf included: Phase 159l item 8. #7 stays off (F-Q1). The rest act in the public estimate or the engines a claim does not re-run, each on its own Word question.
- **Accept** `test/phase159k.test.ts` (nine tests; on the code before, migrations 112 and 113 and the new modules do not exist); phase3 C1, C7, C8, P21 restated; back end documents, phase151b-documents, follow-on, phase159j (L14's switch list) restated; harness K1 (sixteen specified types).

### Phase 159j — 10 October 2026: the stress suite in the build, and a document names its event
- **Why** The stress test of 9 October 2026 was harnesses run by hand (04-phase-work/stress-2026-10-09/, OneDrive); from now on it runs on every check. And Phase 159c's claim page counted each event's documents by type across the client's records, so a purchase-then-refit building shared one count of contractor's invoices between two pieces of work (carried as 159j item 5).

The upload and request forms ask "Which piece of work is it for?" where the building has more than one event.
