### Phase 159i — 10 October 2026: the money adjustments — grants, VAT, acquisition costs and the buyer's own work
- **Why** The stress test of 9 October 2026: two equal-share grants' rows did not add up (C23, worst £287,760), irrecoverable VAT went to the building alone (C12: £1m with £300k land and £200k VAT gave £450k where £420k is right), a grant on a building with work since stopped the total (159c's GRANTS_ON_EVENTS), and the coverage matrix's section 13 listed seventeen claim-increasing readings built and left off.
- **The readings** — `src/web/money-readings.ts` (`moneyReadingsHere`: all on on the test system — the test website or `CAPREBATE_COST_MODEL_TEST=1` — off elsewhere; `PackDeps.money` for tests): `grant_untraced_full`, `transaction_tax_in_base` (#11), `legal_fees_in_base` (#13), `s185_plus_own_s25` (#1), `vat_shared_with_land` (C12). The public estimate's CB_CTA is not touched.
- **Grants** (`pack-grants.ts`) — `GrantSwitches.untraced_full` and the route `FULL`: an untraced grant comes off what qualifies in full, the pools sharing it, after the traced (`ITEMS`) ones; on in test `element_trace` is on too. `PAST_OWNER`: a grant recorded as paid to an earlier owner takes nothing off the buyer's sums; `withPastOwnerGrants` (`pack-legal.ts`) lowers the cap by it (AMBER while its amount is not known). C23: each route's rows are shared by amount, largest remainders taking the pennies, so they add up. Migration 110: `claim_grants.paid_to` (CLIENT, PAST_OWNER) and `event_id` (property_events), the update trigger made again; the grant steps ask both. `PAYER_CLASSES.nhs` names an NHS improvement grant.
- **The purchase** (`packView`) — stamp duty and fees (claim facts `TRANSACTION_TAX`, `ACQUISITION_COSTS`) added to the price; the land takes its share of them and of the VAT (`PackMoney.land_share_pence`); the buyer's own alterations (`BUYER_ALTERATIONS` YES with `BUYER_ALTERATIONS_AMOUNT`) added to the main pool after grants, and `capped(v)` checks the cap against the figure less them. `VAT_CGS_ADJUSTMENT` (a new claim fact, migration 110) is a note. Chapter 15's "Other money in the figure" lists each.
- **Each event** — a grant with an `event_id` on a piece of work comes off that work (`adjustWorks`: in full, the pools sharing it; an amount not known gives no total); `property_events.vat_pence` (migration 110, `setEventVat`, `POST /staff/claims/{id}/events/{event}/vat`) scales every part of the work's cost. `GRANTS_ON_EVENTS` now names only a client's grant with no spend recorded.
- **The seventeen readings** — on in test in the pack: #1, #11, #13 (and #5 in the legal check since 159h). Left off: #7 (a connected payer's money; Rulebook F's F-Q1 is open and the deduction is the law's default), and #2, #3, #4, #8, #9, #15, #16, #19, #23, #26, #29, which act in the public estimate or the window and legal engines a claim does not re-run in the pack — switching any on moves every visitor's figure, so each waits on its own Word question. #20 (s.33B) is built in the pack by Phase 159k.
- **Questions** — Word questions 273-277; register ACC-H10 (13254826447). Carried to the new Phase 159k (13254813789): the seller's SBA statement as a document type, the long-life asset test, Land Remediation Relief for asbestos and the s.33B aggregation.
- **Accept** `test/phase159i.test.ts` (nine tests; on the code before, `money-readings.ts` does not exist, so the file does not load); `src/web/funnel-edges.ts` regenerated for the new foreign key (`node tools/gen-funnel-edges.ts`).

### Phase 159h — 10 October 2026: the legal check — every path ends where the law does
- **Why** The stress test of 9 October 2026 walked 20,367 answer paths through the survey pack's legal check (the claim harness's h6): contradictory answers passed as clear (C6, 8 paths), a seller who could never claim with an earlier owner who could was allowed with no s.185 cap (C7, 174 paths), how the client took the property on was asked only beside an election (C8), an election was checked only in total (C11), and two kinds of seller had no right answer (C26, C28). Phase 159g carried item 7: nothing read how the client holds the building.
- **The facts** — `LEGAL_FACT` adds `earlier_disposal_value` (G12bv) and `transaction_type` (G14s). `legalQuestions` asks the transaction question on every purchase of the property (its own `showIf` replaced), and adds `LC-seller-nontax` after the seller question.
- **The verdict** (`assessLegal`) — C6: "a previous owner claimed" with a seller who could never claim and nobody earlier is AMBER. C8: a premium for a new lease from a landlord who could claim needs a joint s.183 election (none: RED, not known: AMBER); from one who could not, s.184 lets it through; on a transfer below value the engine already makes no election available, and the question now reaches it. C7: a non-entitled seller with an earlier entitled owner caps the claim at G12bv (`cap_words` "section 185"), AMBER while it is not known. C11: `LegalVerdict.pool_caps` (the integral features' figure and the rest) and `legalAllows(v, qualifying, pools)` check each pool (pack-approve.ts and the pack's pages pass the pools).
- **The readings** — `LEGAL_READINGS` (all on in test): `s35_seller_not_entitled` (C26: a s.35-barred landlord seller is read as not entitled, after its seller answer), `non_taxpayer_seller` (C28), `lease_route` (C8), `s185_earlier_cap` (C7). Word questions 268-272.
- **Who claims (item 7)** — `assessLegal`'s optional `holding` (`holdingFor`: the pack's property, else its claim's; `claimantRole`): TENANT on a purchase other than a new lease for a premium is AMBER with `tenant_purchase`, which `claimRoutes` reads as W3; LANDLORD adds "leave out the tenant's fixtures"; not known adds "ask who owns it and who uses it" and decides nothing. `stillOwned(db, c, todayIso)`: a LEASEHOLD whose `lease_end_date` has passed is NO (s.188).
- **Every path a regression (item 6)** — `test/legal-paths-walk.ts` (the harness's walk, seeded, the route-deciding questions enumerated in full, 4 walks below each); `tools/legal-paths-record.ts` writes `test/fixtures/phase159h/legal-paths-gp-company.txt` (one line per path: key, level, cap, deadline); `test/legal-paths.test.ts` checks every one and names the paths that moved.
- **Questions** — Word questions 268-272 for the tax adviser; register entry ACC-H9 (13254716714).
- **Accept** `test/phase159h.test.ts` (thirteen tests; on the code before, `LEGAL_READINGS` does not exist, so the file does not load) and `test/legal-paths.test.ts`. Restated: `pack-legal`, `phase153b`, `phase154a1` and `phase159d` answer how the property was taken on in their purchase fixtures.

### Phase 159g — 10 October 2026: who claims, and what the registers already say
- **Why** The Lockwood run of 9 October 2026 picked a dormant company as the owner (L2), never compared Companies House's year end with the client's (L8), and never asked Price Paid, which held a real sale and a lease (L11); the coverage matrix found the trading-client check turning away landlords the law allows (2.5), tenure and occupier captured and not used (3.1), and "do you still own it" unable to read a person on the register (3.6). Lakhbir ran the phase as written ("merge and run 159g").
- **The company on the register (item 1, L2, L8)** — `searchCompanies` (`src/connectors/company-search.ts`) reads each match's profile (`/company/{company_number}`, at most eight, `COMPANY_SEARCH_PROFILE_LIMIT`) for `accounts_type`; the details screen shows status and accounts type and flags a dormant one (`.f4-dormant`). `companyFields` keeps `accounts_type` and `accounts_made_up_to` (the filing history's reading wins); both are added to CH_COMPANY's permitted fields and the name exclusion register's kept fields. CH_COMPANY and CH_PSC join `TEST_WEBSITE_LOOKUPS` (the free gate waived on the test website, recorded; the PSC still waits for the signed letter). `yearEndReading` / `yearEndFrom` (`claim-facts.ts`, `src/web/who-claims.ts`): the register's accounting reference date is the claim's year end, the client's checked answer compared with it (`differs`); `periodFromYearEnd` and `yearEndKnown` read it. The facts panel shows the year end, a dormant company and whose claim it is.
- **A qualifying activity (item 2)** — `ENQUIRY_CHECKS.TRADING_CLIENT` (key unchanged) now reads "The client has a qualifying activity: it trades, works in a profession, or lets the building to a business"; SOP 1's gate and step 2 say the same (`stages.ts` transcribes it). The details screen asks "Who uses the building?" (`BUILDING_USE`: own_use, let_out, rented, unsure; not required); answer row `Q2-n-use`; at submission `properties.claimant_role` (OWNER_OCCUPIER, LANDLORD, TENANT; "Not sure" is null) with its source (`USE_TO_ROLE`). `qualifyingActivityEvidence` puts the register's company and each building's role beside the check.
- **Price Paid for every sector (item 3, L11)** — `pricePaid` no longer refuses a sector; it keeps `ppd_category` (A or B, `categoryOf`). `isPricePaidLead`: category B, type "Other", or no category is a lead, never the price; `desk-first.ts` skips a lead; chapter 9's "What the registers say" (`leadsBlock`) shows it with the attribution. The engine's own `residentialOnly` stays: derive() never offers a Price Paid figure to a client outside supported living.
- **Tenure and occupier on the property (item 4)** — migration 109: `properties.claimant_role`, `claimant_role_source`, `tenure_source`, `lease_end_date`, `landlord` (a company or a role token, never a person), `council_area`, `council_area_source`. `fillTenureFromRegisters` (after each research run and each title typed by hand; the title first, then Price Paid; never over a recorded tenure); `propertyTenure` is the one reading, for the route and the legal check (159h). The property page records how the client holds it, the day a lease ends and the landlord (`CLAIMANT_ROLES`; each 'recorded by staff'; `PropertyView.holding`).
- **The site area (item 5)** — `siteAreaFlag` (more than `SITE_AREA_FLAG_RATIO`, 10, times the floor area) on the site-area lines and in chapter 9; never used as the plot.
- **A person named as owner (item 6)** — the title typed by hand takes `owner_person_name`, compared in memory with the client's contacts (`personIsClients`, `sameName`); only `proprietor_personal_match` YES or NO is kept (a new permitted and kept field on HMLR_TITLE). `stillOwned` reads it, or a kept `proprietor_name_personal`, for a sole trader, a partnership or an individual (`registerNamesClientPerson`).
- **The BCIS location (item 7, from 159f)** — `keepCouncilArea`: the postcode check's council area at submission, or the research's postcodes.io answer, kept once. `packView` drafts the location from it where the loaded table names it (`bcisLocationNamed`; `Pricing.location_from` COUNCIL); any surveyor entry, the UK mean included, wins.
- **Questions** — Word questions 263 (the tax adviser: a landlord's claim), 264 (the accountant: which year end), 265-266 (the surveyor: the Price Paid lead, the site-area point), 267 (the data protection officer: the name check, the accounts type). Register entries ACC-H8 (13254543097), SV-S12 (13254543146), SDP-P44 (13254520473).
- **Accept** `test/phase159g.test.ts` (seventeen tests; on the code before, `who-claims.ts` does not exist, so the file does not load). Restated: `phase146c` (Price Paid for every sector, the new fields), `phase153b` (a candidate's accounts type), `survey-stage6` (the waiver list and what the test website reaches).

### Phase 159f — 10 October 2026: BCIS — the pack drafts every rate, and every rate names its source
- **Why** The Lockwood run (L12) printed every rate as "Surveyor" with no source, and the stress test found the index factor displayed but not applied (C19), the £/m² flag comparing rates at different dates (C20), and no flag on a share above the sector's ceiling (C21). Lakhbir, 9 October 2026: "With BCIS data loaded, the pack could draft each rate from the right building type — this needs to happen", and BCIS confirmed our use (relayed by Lakhbir; Word question 218, closed 10 October 2026). A re-derived premise: the brief called today's s.562 sum "residual"; it is the proportional one (price × the qualifying parts' share of the rebuild cost), so the residual one is built beside it.
- **The importer** — `src/web/bcis-data.ts`: `parseBcisRates` (average prices by building function: "Building function", "Mean", optional "Median" and "Sample", the quarter from a "Rebased to 2Q 2026" line), `parseBcisElements` (by NRM element, £/m² of floor), `parseBcisLocations` (95 or 0.95); columns found by header words; any unreadable file refuses the whole import. `importBcisData` writes `CAPREBATE_BCIS_DATA_FILE` (default `~/.caprebate-bcis-data.json`), refused inside the repository. CLI: `node src/cli.ts import-bcis-data --rates … [--elements …] [--locations …] [--base 2026-04]`; `import-bcis-index --tpi` names the All-in Tender Price Index. `bcisDataHere`: the imported data, else `BCIS_PLACEHOLDER` (invented, labelled) on the test system only, else none and why.
- **The drafts** — `bcisDrafts`: the whole-building rate from the sector's building function (`BCIS_BUILDING_TYPE`, the first name the data holds; dental priced as health centres, said so) and each floor-area line from its NRM elements (`NRM_OF_LINE`: LS-SR-001 5.8, LS-SR-002 5.4, LS-SR-003 5.6, LS-SR-004 5.7, LS-SL-001 5.8 + 5.4), × the location factor (`savePackLocation`, the new pack field `location`; none: the UK mean) × the index from BCIS's quarter to the purchase date (`indexBetween`). Each is a DRAFT `Source` with a structured `basis` (`RateBasis`: kind, ref, priced_at, location, index); TEST_PLACEHOLDER where invented.
- **Every rate sourced** — migration 108 rebuilds `survey_pack_entries` (105's shape) with `rate_basis`, `rate_ref`, `rate_priced_at` (all three or none, only on a rate with a value) and the `location` field. `saveLine`, `addLine` and `savePackValue` take the source (`RateSourceForm`, `parseRateSource`); every rate form shows the three boxes. `readiness` refuses approval while a counted rate has no source (`sources`) or the rates sit at more than one day (`priced_at`). `ratesSourcedNeed` (pipeline.ts) makes the Valuation stage's "every rate sourced" a test of the newest approved version.
- **One day for every rate (C19)** — `packView` brings each sourced rate priced at another day to the purchase date by the index (its source says so) and lists any it cannot (`Pricing.mixed`). The version keeps `pricing` (`priced_at`, `purchase`, the BCIS words and location, `residual_pence`). The report's method says "priced at <the day>, the purchase date" and names the proportional way (`purchaseLede`).
- **The report (item 4, L12)** — each rate's chip names its source (BCIS, QS schedule, Invoice, Price book, TEST RATE, or No source); "Where each rate comes from" lists them in full (page and PDF); a version with any test rate says "Test rates" at the top of the method.
- **C20, C21, item 6** — `rateFlagReading` rebases every approved pack's rate to this pack's pricing day before the middle rate (those it cannot are left out, and counted); `shareFlagReading` flags a share above the sector's purchase-band top (CB_BAND) for review, never refuses; `Pricing.residual` (the building less its own rebuild cost, bounded by the fixtures' cost and the price) beside the proportional sum in chapter 22.
- **Questions** — Word question 218 closed; 261 (which way, for the surveyor and the tax adviser) and 262 (the drafting) for the surveyor; register entry SV-S11 (board item 13254340860); tracker 13254356802 for Lakhbir: get the BCIS export.
- **Accept** `test/phase159f.test.ts` (ten tests; on the code before, `bcis-data.ts` does not exist, so the file does not load); the seven suites that approve packs give each typed rate a source; `phase157a` (pricing days on its fixture versions) and `survey-report` (the rate chip names the source) restated; `phase133c`'s plain-reading list gains the new need.

### Phase 159e — 10 October 2026: the allowances on each event, and the return entries
- **Why** The stress test of 9 October 2026 (C13, C15, C17, C18; the Lockwood run, L4): the return entries showed writing-down allowances only, every spend in one period, the special rate pool at 6% in every year, no small-pools write-off and no structures allowance, and the spreadsheet could read a different period from the window. Lakhbir's target: "the highest figure we can defend". The claim harness's `h4-returns.ts` was updated for the new rows (`h4-returns.ts.before-159e` kept) and given a Phase 159e block; the engine harness stays at its two kept classes.
- **The allowances, part by part** — `src/web/allowances.ts` (`allowancesFor`, from the approved version alone): each part (the purchase, each piece of work) goes into its own period — W1 its own (`EventRoute.period`), W2 the first open one (`first_open`), W3 not claimed — read from the routes frozen in the version at approval (`snapshotOf` now keeps each route's `event_id`, `kind`, `event_date`, `period` and `first_open`). **First-year relief on W1 only**: the annual investment allowance on recorded headroom (`AIA_HEADROOM` NONE, or `AIA_HEADROOM_AMOUNT`), special rate pool first, shared across the parts in a period, never for a partnership with a company partner or where that is not known (N4), and not spent on main pool plant a company's full expensing covers; then on new plant (own-cost works; a purchase only with `BOUGHT_NEW_FROM_DEVELOPER` YES) a company's full expensing and 50% allowance from 1 April 2023, or the 40% allowance (s.45U) from 1 January 2026, the remainder joining the pool the next period. A second-hand W1 purchase takes the annual investment allowance but not full expensing (a re-derived premise: the prompt said writing-down allowances only). The super-deduction is named and not taken (it needs a contract date nothing records, and no year it applies to is open). **The pools**: whole pence, the engine's dated rates under each claimant reading, a pool of £1,000 or less written off (s.56A). **The structures and buildings allowance**: work — final account less plant less repair, from the month after the work, work wholly from November 2018; purchase from 29 October 2018 — `SBA_QUALIFYING_EXPENDITURE` with `SBA_STATEMENT_HELD` YES (NO: none); W2 from the first open period; none on supported living (s.270CF). Also a chapter 17 line per piece of work (`pack-sheets.ts`).
- **The return entries** — `returnEntries` (`src/web/return-entries.ts`) is made from `allowancesFor`: a part with no first-year relief keeps the old row ("Spend that counts, added to the pool"); one with relief shows its spend, each relief, what is left to the pool now and what joins it next period; then each pool's allowance ("Small pool written off" where s.56A applies) and balance; the structures allowance per part; and each return's total ("All the allowances in this return"). The run is five returns, or to the return after the last part's. `returnContext` adds the allowance facts (`facts`), and its period is the window's (C15: `purchasePeriod`, recorded or from the checked year end). `return_entry_contexts.facts_json` (migration 107) keeps them with the version once sent; a row without them reads as before.
- **One period source, before approval** — `yearEndKnown` (`claim-facts.ts`): `readiness` refuses approval (`year_end`) until the client's checked year end or a whole recorded period is on the claim.
- **The cash basis** — claim fact `CASH_BASIS` (migration 107; or the client's assessment answer Q3 where not recorded); `cashBasis` in `claim-route.ts`: CASH, for a claimant that is not a company, makes every part W3.
- **The report** — `reportModel(snapshot, fingerprint, ctx)` (ctx from `returnContextFor` / `contextForApproval`): section 03 and the PDF add **The allowances, part by part** (route, year, each relief, added to the pool, structures allowance) and **Year by year** (every allowance and the pools carried on).
- **The engine** (site file; generated `shared.js`): `CB_RATES.WDA_TABLE` and `datedRate` — main 25%/20%/18%/14%, special 10%/8%/6%, by the CT and IT change dates, a straddle rounded up (CA23222); `hybridMainRate` reads it; `specialRate`, `sbaRate` (2%/3%, `SBA`), `SMALL_POOL_LIMIT`, `SUPER_DEDUCTION`. `CB_BENEFIT.schedule` writes the special rate pool down by date and writes off a small pool; `reliefsEstablished` treats a purchase with `bought_new_from_developer === true` as not second-hand.
- **Questions for the tax adviser** — Word questions 257 to 260; register entry ACC-H7 (board item 13254229838).
- **Accept** site harness Suite AQ (seven checks, seven red on `.bak-phase159e`); `test/phase159e.test.ts` (fourteen tests; on the code before, `allowances.ts` does not exist, so the file does not load); `phase153b`, `phase159a`, `survey-report` and `survey-stage6` restated (the new rows, the small pool, the frozen periods, the year end before approval).

### Phase 159d — 9 October 2026: each event's route — the deadline gate, and "do you still own it"
- **Why** The stress test of 9 October 2026 (C1, C2, C9, C10, C24, C25, C27; the Lockwood run, L9): the window gate turned away claims the law allows — a building bought in 2019, still owned and never claimed, can still be pooled in a year whose return is open. Lakhbir, 9 October 2026: "but then remember refurbishment and also tax allowances" — the route is decided per event. The engine harness went from 3 classes to 2 (both kept on purpose: questions 247 and 250); the claim harness's 2019, 2020 and 2023 purchases, still owned, now pass Reports and Invoice through the 2024 pool.
- **Each event's route** — `src/web/claim-route.ts`: `claimRoutes` gives every event on the claim's building (Phase 159c's `property_events`; one route, the purchase's, where none are recorded; repairs take none) a route: **W1** the window for its own period is open; **W2** closed, but the building is still owned — the spend goes into the pool of the **first open period** (`firstOpen`, stepping a year at a time from the event's period until a window is open); **W3** not owned, or the purchase fails the legal check; **NOT_KNOWN** passes nothing (N4). A piece of work is placed in a period by the client's checked year end (`periodFromYearEnd`, exported) only where its whole date, a month or a year, falls in one period. `windowForPeriod` in `src/web/claim-facts.ts` is the claimant's rule for any period, read by the purchase's window too.
- **"Do you still own it"** — claim fact **STILL_OWNED** (migration 106; on the facts panel): `stillOwned` reads a sale date on the property (NO), the recorded fact, or the title register naming the client's company number (YES); anything else is not known.
- **The gates** — `reportsWindowNeed` ("Each part of the claim has a route, and at least one goes in") and `invoiceWindowNeed` (the return each route names: its own, or the first open one; a filing inside a part's own window passes after it has closed) in `src/web/claim-record.ts`. `moveClaim` refuses **OUTSIDE_AMENDMENT_WINDOW** unless every part is W3. The early warning (`earlyWindowWarning`) says the relief comes through this year's pool. The client's own filing confirmation names the deadline of the return the route names.
- **C9** — `legalDeadlineNeed`: where the approved version's legal check was URGENT and its deadline has passed (UK date), the check is read again (`legalVerdictForClaim`, `legalVerdictForJob` in `src/web/pack-legal.ts`); unless now clear, the sign-off gate and Reports stop, and `recordSignOff` refuses.
- **Item 8** — the legal check is the purchase's: a RED purchase with work since is W3 on its own (`PackView.purchase_w3`); the pack totals the works alone, approval is not blocked, the version records `events.purchase_w3`, and the report names the purchase as not claimed.
- **The engine** (site file; generated `shared.js`): `twoYearsFrom` — every two-year period (s.201(1), s.187A(7), s.187A(11), FA 2012 Sch 10 para 13(2)) ends the day before the second anniversary (C10, E14); `ukDayOf`/`inTimeOn` — the fixed value window and `deriveAmendmentWindow` are open all of their last UK day (C2); `saDeadline` takes the earlier tax year's deadline for a self assessment period ending from 6 April 2024 outside 31 March to 5 April (C25, basis period reform); `CB_S187A.assess` — a direct purchase from an owner known to have claimed, the period closed, no tribunal application in time: **NIL** (C27).
- **C24** — claimant types `CHARITY_COMPANY` (the company dates) and `CHARITABLE_TRUST` (self assessment) in `CLAIMANT_TYPES` and migration 106's triggers; `CHARITY` alone is not known, and says why.
- **Item 6** — the approved version keeps `routes`; the report's section 03 and the PDF say **How each part goes in**; the portal's claim page too (`portalClaimPage`).
- **Questions for the tax adviser** — Word questions 253 to 256; register entry ACC-H6 (board item 13250825382).
- **Accept** site harness Suite AP (nine checks, seven red on `.bak-phase159d`, two controls) and A-1 and X5 restated; `test/phase159d.test.ts` (twelve tests; on the code before the route module does not exist, so the file does not load); `claim-record`, `finance-claim`, `pack-legal`, `phase132-window` and `pipeline` restated.

### Phase 159c — 9 October 2026: several events on one building — a purchase, then the works
- **Why** The stress test of 9 October 2026 (E1, E4) and the Lockwood Surgery run (L5): a GP surgery bought for £650,000 and refurbished for £90,000 reached a figure for the purchase alone; the works were an answer nothing read. Lakhbir: "that's a design gap - this is needed". The engine harness (`04-phase-work/stress-2026-10-09/engine/`, OneDrive) went from 15 classes of finding to 3, none of them this phase's.
- **Events, not one trigger** — `CB_FUNNEL.eventsOf(p)` in the site's shared layer: every event on a property, in order. The first is the property's own `trigger`, `spend` and `date`; the rest are `p.events` after its first entry (or, on an older draft, its one works answer). Each event `{type, cost, cost_unknown, date, date_unknown, vat}`; `vat` is carried for Phase 159i. `CB_FUNNEL.figure` sizes **each event on its own band** (`eventFigure`, the cuts per event — the seller only on the purchase, the date and the size on each, the client's own earlier claim on the whole building) and the building's ceiling is the sum; redecoration and repairs (`cosmetic`) and work of an unknown kind add nothing and say why. `CB_BAND.bandKey("conversion")` is the refurbishment band.
- **The engine (E1, E4)** — one amount with several events ticked reads the **cautious** band (`CB_INPUTS.cautiousTrigger`): the purchase where one is ticked, with the s.187A reading on the price, else the lowest band — never the richest band on the whole amount. A session with `events` (two or more with a cost) is run **once per event** on a copy (`CB_INPUTS.run` → `byEvent`) and the results added: QE, TS, cash and the pools; `events` on the result keeps each.
- **The funnel** — `src/web/funnel4.ts`: the works screen is "add an event" — one row per piece of work (`b{i}_w{j}_type`, `_spend`, `_date`, each with "I don't know"), **+ Add another piece of work** and **Remove** keep the screen (`MAX_WORKS` 10); an older page's one works answer is read as the first row. The spend-on help says the work is added later (F3 gone). The figure page names each event with its own "up to" (`eventLine`). `src/web/funnel-session.ts` maps `events` onto the session; `base_amount` stays the first event's money.
- **Migration 105** — `property_events` (id, property, business, `ord`, `kind` PURCHASE · NEW_BUILD · EXTENSION · REFURBISHMENT · FIT_OUT · CONVERSION · REPAIRS · OTHER, `event_date` YYYY or YYYY-MM, `cost_pence`, `vat`, `source` ASSESSMENT or STAFF): `submit()` writes them (`src/web/property-events.ts`, `writeAssessmentEvents`); the lead lists them (`leadView().events`); deletable with a lost enquiry (`funnel-deletable.ts`, `funnel-edges.ts` regenerated). The survey pack's entries take nine cost-led values per piece of work (`works_*`, line_key the event's id); the contractor's cost breakdown is labelled as the final account.
- **The claim page** — section **What happened to the building** (`claim.events`): each event with its date, its cost and its own "up to" (the band on its own cost, `eventCeiling`; `claim.read_value` only), and its own documents with whether one is held (`DOCUMENTS_FOR`). **Add an event**: `POST /staff/claims/{id}/events` (`claim.write`; a second purchase refused; audited `PROPERTY_EVENT_ADDED`, never a cost).
- **The cost-led mode** — `worksSums` in `src/web/pack-sums.ts`: what qualifies is the plant (main + special) with the builder's work (s.25) and the demolition of old plant (s.26) that follow it, plus the preliminaries and fees shared out over every direct cost in the same proportion; the repair part, with its share, is a deduction from profits. The parts must add up to the final account (`WORKS_PARTS_NOT_TOTAL`), every value given (N4). **One survey job carries both**, by choice: one claim, one property, one visit, one approval, one report and one return; one job per event would need several jobs and packs per claim (a second job is refused, a pack is one per job) and several reports and sign-offs. `packView` gives `events`, `purchase_applies` (a purchase recorded, or no events at all: every pack from before), `purchase_sums` and `works`; `sums` is the purchase plus every piece of work. A grant on a building with works stops the total (`GRANTS_ON_EVENTS`) until Phase 159i says which spend it paid for. Chapter 9 carries **Work done since, priced on its own cost**, `POST /staff/survey/{job}/pack/works` (`saveWorks`); chapter 4 lists each spend.
- **Approval, the report, the return** — the version keeps `events` (the purchase's own sums and each piece of work); the committed building figure adds what the work cost. The report's cover reads the total, section 01's section 562 sum the purchase's own, and **Each spend on the building** lists each (page and PDF). The return entries add each spend on its own row (`Spend that counts, added to the pool: the purchase`, `…: A refurbishment or refit, 2025`); which period each belongs to is Phase 159e's.
- **Chapter 20** runs per piece of work with its repair part, and reads the funnel state's `properties` (it read `buildings`, so the client's words were never shown).
- **Line 8 (E3)** — `submit()` stores no low end (`assessments.shown_low_pence` NULL): the client was shown "up to £X" only; the reconciliation's basis says so.
- **Questions for the tax adviser** — Word questions 251 and 252; register entry ACC-H5 (board item 13250219042).
- **Accept** site harness Suite AO (eleven checks, nine red on `.bak-phase159c`, two controls); `test/phase159c.test.ts` (fifteen tests, all red on the code before); `test/phase158a.test.ts` and `test/tasks.test.ts` restated (F3's words; `property_events` is not a second log).

### Phase 159b — 9 October 2026: the assessment figure and the engine's inputs made sound
- **Why** The engine stress test of 9 October 2026 (`04-phase-work/stress-2026-10-09/engine/`, OneDrive; E2 to E19). Its harness was re-run first and last: of 58 classes of finding before, the ones this phase owned are gone; what remains belongs to Phases 159c (works and several events, E1, E4, the stored low end), 159d (the two-year clock, E14) and the tax adviser (E15).
- **The engine checks what it is given (E7, E8)** — `CB_INPUTS` in the site's shared layer (`09-app/engine/src/generated/shared.js`): every sector's `runEngine` is now `CB_INPUTS.run(s, __runEngine)`. `clean` takes a money figure that is not a finite number of pounds, never negative (`base_amount` and the other amounts in `CB_INPUTS.MONEY`), as not given, and says so in the assumptions; it reads every `_fraction` between 0 and 1 (above 100% is 100%, the cautious end). After the run, a client with no UK tax to reduce (`is_taxpayer`/`is_uk_taxpayer` "no", or a charity) has a nil saving and cash and a referral; the client's own fixtures claim (`prior_claim_self`) has a nil spend, saving and cash and a referral — so a direct caller (`engaged.ts`, `allowance-profit-comparison.ts`) gets the answer the question flow gives.
- **One band table (E2, E11, E16, E18)** — `CB_BAND`: every sector engine's `YIELD_BAND` and supported living's `COMMON_PARTS` are read from it; supported living's online ceiling goes through its common parts (`SL_COMMON_PARTS`, a quarter), so the client sees the engine's figure; `rangeFor` rounds in basis points and returns no figure under £1; switch `care_home_new_build_top_at_purchase` (on: a care home's new build tops out at 40%, off 35%); `bandKey("extension")` is the new-build band. An empty trigger is a purchase in every sector.
- **The answers that cut the figure (E5, E6, E10, E12, E13)** — `CB_FUNNEL.cutFor`: no figure for a building where the client says they already claimed on its fixtures, where the seller of a purchase did not claim unless it is known to be before April 2014, where the spend is known to be before April 2008, where the date is in the future, or where the spend is over `PLAUSIBLE_MAX` (£100 million); each says why. `monthRange` reads "MM/YYYY" or "YYYY" as a range of months. The engine's `parseDate` (two copies) takes ISO dates only; `deriveAmendmentWindow` needs a whole ISO period end.
- **The back end** — `src/web/funnel4.ts`: `parseMoney` (650k, 1.5m, £1.25 million; refuses a minus, 1e6, 1,5, 1.000.000 and under £1, with a reason); future dates refused; the answer "We extended it (added new space)"; the claims screen asks "Have you, or your business, already claimed"; the figure page shows the engine's reason for a building it will not size and never "up to £0"; a loss-making building is told the relief may come as a loss. `src/web/funnel-session.ts` maps, for a funnel-4 assessment, the client's own "yes" to `prior_claim_self` and, on a purchase, the seller's "yes"/"no" to `prior_owner_claimed`; an extension to `new_build`. `CLIENT_RANGE_BASIS` in `src/web/router.ts` no longer says the figure only moves down.
- **Questions for the tax adviser** — Word questions 247 to 250; register entry ACC-H4 (board item 13249231366).
- **Accept** site harness Suite AN (fifteen checks, thirteen red on `.bak-phase159b`) and five P1/Q2/Q4 checks restated for the extension and the cuts; `test/phase159b.test.ts` (ten tests, all red on the code before); `test/funnel4.test.ts` restated for the extension's answer.

### Phase 159a — 9 October 2026: one version after sign-off, the "up to" figure everywhere, and the small faults
- **Why** The stress test of 9 October 2026 and the Lockwood Surgery live run (`04-phase-work/stress-2026-10-09/`, `04-phase-work/sim-2026-10-09-lockwood/`, OneDrive; C2, C3, C4, C5, C14, C16, C22, C29, E3, L1, L3, L6, L7).
- **One version after sign-off (C3, C5, L3)** (`src/web/valuation-lock.ts`: `valuationLock`, `voidLine`, `setAsideOnReopen`, `lastReopen`): once a claim is past the tax adviser's sign-off (Reports, Invoice, Defence pack, Closed; on hold, the stage it returns to) or has stopped at an exit, the survey pack takes no approval (`readiness` blocker `locked` in `src/web/pack-approve.ts`) and the legal check no answer (`answerLegal` in `src/web/pack-legal.ts`). **"Reopen the valuation"** on the claim page is the claim's own REOPEN to Valuation (ADMIN, `claim.reopen`, with a reason). In the same transaction a reopen to the sign-off or earlier marks the client report and the accountant's pack as issued SUPERSEDED; and every reader looks only past the latest such reopen — the sign-off (`signOffSinceEntry`), the release (`releasedVersion`, `releasedReportFingerprint`), the client's acceptance (`clientAcceptedNeed`, `defencePackRecords`) and an issued defence pack (`deliverableIssued`, `deliverableIssuedNeed`). Nothing is deleted. The claim page says the figures are fixed, offers the button with what it sets aside, and after a reopen says when, who, why and what it set aside (`valuationBlock` in `src/web/crm-pages.ts`; the move's audit row carries `set_aside` counts). The window reads the purchase date from the **signed** version's legal check before any working answer (`acquisitionGiven` in `src/web/claim-facts.ts`).
- **The sign-off names its version (C4)** (**migration 104**: `tax_adviser_signoffs.approval_id`, a trigger that it is a version on this claim; earlier sign-offs given the version the old rule released for them): `recordSignOff` records the newest approved version and refuses a figure that is not that version's own, and refuses a version (`approval_id`, posted by the sign-off form and passed by `recordAnswer`) that is not the newest. The gate out of sign-off (`signOffRecordNeed`) is shut while the newest version is unsigned. Release reads the version a sign-off names, at its own figure — never a version matched by its total.
- **Return entries (C29, C14, C16)** (`src/web/return-entries.ts`; **migration 104** `return_entry_contexts`, never changed or deleted): the period and claimant type a version's spreadsheet is made from are kept the first time it leaves (sent for sign-off, or its sign-off recorded) and read for every later copy (`returnContextFor`); worked in pence from the engine's rate for each year, so every row adds up; a main-pool rate stands only where every claimant reading agrees, and never beside a blank amount.
- **"Up to £X" (E3)** (`formatShownRange` in `src/web/shown-range.ts`): the lead, the claim record, the staff assessment pages and the portal show the ceiling the client saw, never a range; labels say "Figure shown". The stored low end is not drawn on any screen.
- **HM Land Registry's INSPIRE download (L1)** (`sendFollowing` in `src/connectors/http.ts`; `same_host_redirects: 2` and `redirect_hosts` on the HMLR_INSPIRE row of `src/connectors/sources.ts`): read live on 9 October 2026, the download answers a 302 to the same address setting an AccessToken cookie, then, with the cookie, a 302 to a signed address on HM Land Registry's own file store (`datapub-prd-s3-bucket.s3.amazonaws.com`). Both are followed, at most two; the cookie and the caller's headers go back to the same host only; any other host, http, or a third redirect is refused; still one audit row a call. Every other service still refuses every redirect. The live end-to-end lookup waits on a recorded switch-on (Phase 159j line 4): the day's three test lookups were used.
- **Small faults**: the window is open all of its last UK day (C2, `amendmentWindow` in `src/web/engine.ts` hands the engine the start of today's UK date — the site engine's own comparison is carried to Phase 159d); a line with a count, rate or share of 0 needs no pool (C22, `packSums`); the postcode once on the report, the pack and its header (L6, `propertyLine` in `src/web/survey-pack.ts`); chapter 11 reads the one purchase date and says where from (L7, `indexReading` in `src/web/pack-cost-model.ts`).
- **Accept** `test/phase159a.test.ts` (16; 14 red on the code before this phase, 2 controls green on both); `claim-fixtures` (`signOffRow` names the newest version; `releaseReportFor` records the sign-off again naming the version it makes), `lists` and `survey-report` restated.

### Phase 158d — 9 October 2026: the accountant's own confirmations, one purchase date, the floor area, the defence pack, and what the portal tells the client
- **Why** Phase 158c's second run of the invented client, with nobody ticking a step for somebody else (`04-phase-work/sim-2026-10-06/Friary-House-simulation-findings.md`, OneDrive; F43 to F47), and F48 found by this phase's own run.
- **What the portal says (F43, F48)**: signing the letter raises the client to "2 Engagement letter signed" at once, never lowering one past it (`signAs` in `src/web/signing.ts`); "New for you" says the letter is signed; "What we need from you" lists what the gate waits on from the client — each required document not yet received on a claim at Data gathering, asked for or not, once each, and the released report while not accepted (`alsoNeeded` in `src/web/portal-dashboard.ts`; `missingDocuments` in `src/web/mandatory-documents.ts`). The portal's claim tile reads the same step (`portalClaim` in `src/web/portal.ts`, through `clientStageForClaim`).
- **The floor area (F44)** (`floorAreaOnRecord`, `approvedClientAnswer` in `src/web/claim-facts.ts`): "Data gathering complete — floor area sourced" is refused until a floor area is on record — the property's energy certificate, Homedata's estimate, or the client's own answer to "how big is the building" with a number in it, checked by staff. It stays the analyst's own tick.
- **One purchase date (F45)** (`acquisitionGiven`, `clientDate`, `clientYearEnd`, `periodFromYearEnd` in `src/web/claim-facts.ts`): where the property record has no acquisition date, the window reads the surveyor's legal-check answer G9d, else the client's checked answer to "the completion date" (a whole date only); where no accounting period is recorded, the client's checked year end gives the period the purchase falls in. Read, never written over the record, and the window says which ("Read from ...").
- **The accountant confirms for themselves (F46)** (`src/web/accountant-confirm.ts`, `src/web/accountant-confirm-pages.ts`; **migration 102** `accountant_confirm_requests`, `accountant_confirm_answers`, never changed or deleted): Admin or Sales (`email.queue`) email one of the company's accountants a private link — RETURN_FIT at Reports, FILED at Invoice or the defence pack; only its SHA-256 is kept, 30 days, answered once, sent as the sign-off link is (outbox kind REPORT_FOR_SIGNOFF, blanked once sent). The accountant confirms (name, firm ending Limited, LLP or plc, and for FILED the day) or says not, with why. The Reports gate reads a confirmed RETURN_FIT since entering Reports (`accountantFitNeed`); `ACCOUNTANT_CONFIRMED_RETURN` is retired. A FILED confirmation writes the reconciliation's filing date, under the member of staff who sent the link, and raises the client's step; closing reads it (`filingRecordedNeed`), and typing a filing date is refused where the accountant files (`accountantFiles`, the Reports route of `src/web/client-filing.ts`). Where the client has no accountant, the client route is unchanged. Audit kinds `ACCOUNTANT_CONFIRM_SENT`, `ACCOUNTANT_CONFIRM_ANSWERED`.
- **The defence pack (F47)** (`defencePackRecords`, `MADE_HERE` in `src/web/claim-record.ts`; **migration 103** `defence_pack_manifests`): issuing it reads the claim's own records — the released survey report, the sign-off, the client report and the accountant's pack as issued, the client's acceptance, the fee invoice as sent, and the filing — refuses with what is missing, keeps the list, and records its SHA-256 as the deliverable's fingerprint; a typed one is not used. The Phase 132 generator (`src/web/defence-pack.ts`) is untouched and still off, waiting on the Defence Pack Template's sign-off; FOR-THE-ACCOUNTANT asks whether this list is a sound minimum.
- **Accept** `test/phase158d.test.ts`; `automation`, `claim-record`, `finance-claim`, `follow-on`, `phase132-dates`, `phase132-records`, `phase132e`, `phase151b-documents`, `phase158b` (a flaky check on a random id), `phase158c`, `pipeline`, `ui-tidy-portal` and `test/claim-fixtures.ts` (`floorAreaForClaim`, `accountantAnswers`, `defenceReady`) restated; `src/web/funnel-edges.ts` regenerated.

### Phase 158c — 7 October 2026: the steps staff ticked for other people
- **Why** Phase 158b's run of the invented client from Survey to Closed (`04-phase-work/sim-2026-10-06/Friary-House-simulation-findings.md`, OneDrive; F34 to F42): the journey ran end to end, but several gates passed on a tick a member of staff made for somebody else, or on a report the application did not hold.
- **The surveyor's valuation (F34, F35)** (`packApprovedNeed` in `src/web/pipeline.ts`): the Valuation gate reads an approval of the survey pack (`survey_pack_approvals`) on a survey job on this claim, "The surveyor approved and signed the survey pack"; only the surveyor's approval counts. The staff tick `VALUATION_SIGNED` is retired (`MILESTONES[...].retired`: refused, read by no gate, said on the claim page).
- **The reports the claim issues (F38, F36)** (`MADE_FROM_REPORT`, `releasedReportFingerprint` in `src/web/claim-record.ts`): the client report and the accountant's computation pack are issued from the survey report the surveyor approved, once a tax adviser's sign-off of the same figure is recorded after that approval and while its fingerprint holds; the fingerprint is that version's, computed, never typed (the issue form asks for the date only, `src/web/claim-record-pages.ts`). The deliverables table says so.
- **The client accepts the report (F39)** (`src/web/report-accept.ts`: `acceptReport`, `myAcceptance`, `ACCEPT_WORDS`; `POST /portal/report/accept`; `client_report.accept`, CLIENT for their own business only, refused on the view-only role): the client reads the released version at `/portal/report` and presses "I accept this report"; **migration 101** (`client_report_acceptances`: who, when, which approval and its fingerprint; never updated or deleted). The Reports gate (`clientAcceptedNeed`) reads an acceptance of the issued client report's own fingerprint, so a later version must be accepted again; the staff tick `CLIENT_ACCEPTED_REPORT` is retired, and the fee trigger's CLIENT_ACCEPTED_REPORT reading reads the same acceptance. The portal's working words at Reports ask for it (`workingWords` in `src/web/portal-dashboard.ts`). `CLIENT_REPORT_ACCEPTED` is logged.
- **The window, early (F37)** (`earlyWindowWarning` in `src/web/claim-facts.ts`): from Engagement until Reports, the claim page warns (`#window-warning`) when, on the client's own month at the assessment and the latest accounting period that month could fall in, the amendment window has most likely closed. It warns; the stop stays at Reports.
- **The invoice sent (F41)**: the Invoice gate needs a live fee invoice recorded as SENT on this claim and not credited in full, "Our fee invoice is sent to the client on this claim"; one raised and not sent says so.
- **The filing recorded (F42)** (`filingRecordedNeed` in `src/web/claim-record.ts`; `clientStageForClaim` in `src/web/pipeline.ts`): closing a claim at the defence pack needs the filing date on its reconciliation; the defence pack and Closed show the client "5 Claim filed" only once it is recorded (until then "4 Expert sign-off"), and recording it raises the client's step.
- **The next-step card** (`src/web/next-step.ts`) has plain readings for the four new needs.
- **Accept** `test/phase158c.test.ts`; `pipeline`, `claim-record`, `automation`, `finance-claim`, `follow-on`, `phase132e`, `phase132-records` and `test/claim-fixtures.ts` (`packApprovedOn`, `releaseReportFor`, `clientAccepted`, `filingRecorded`; `claimInvoiceRow` with status SENT) restated.

### Phase 158b — 7 October 2026: opening a survey job, the letter's party and property, and what the client is shown
- **Why** Phase 158a's run of the invented client (`04-phase-work/sim-2026-10-06/Friary-House-simulation-findings.md`, OneDrive; F22 to F33) reached Survey and stopped: nothing in the application opened a survey job or sealed the estimate a surveyor's commit releases (F31). The rest of its findings came with it.
- **Opening a survey job (F31)** (`src/web/survey-open.ts`: `openSurveyJob`, `surveyJobPanel`, `estimateBasis`; `POST /staff/claims/{id}/survey-job`, the existing `surveyor_job.assign`, Admin and Sales): at Survey, staff choose a SURVEYING login and open the job, one per claim. Opening it seals the assessment's estimate unless one is sealed (a sealed one is never re-sealed): the spend that counts is the "up to" figure the client was shown, and its share of what the client said they spent; the pools, the land and the tax saving are 0 and **migration 100** (`estimates.basis`, ADD COLUMN) records FUNNEL_CEILING, so the released estimate says "not estimated at the assessment" for them (`Workspace.estimate_basis`). `SURVEY_JOB_OPENED` is logged with no figure. The payload proof still finds none of the seven as surveyor and all seven as admin.
- **The letter's party, property and date (F22)** (`src/web/letter.ts` `loadLetter` takes `claimantType`, `property`, `date`; `src/web/signing.ts` `letterFacts`, `letterOptions`, `ShownLetter.party_unknown`): a partnership, sole trader or individual is described as one with no company number; an LLP is called one; the business's properties, the day the letter was sent and the figure shown ("up to £X") are filled in. The claimant is the client's own entity answer, else the organisation's record (`claimantTypeForGate`). `signingMissing` refuses a letter still naming the specimen company number in every mode, the test copy's included.
- **The client's "bought" answer (F28)** (`clientSaidBought` in `src/web/claim-facts.ts`; `src/web/mandatory-documents.ts`): where nobody has recorded BOUGHT_THE_BUILDING, the client's own "how did you come by this one?" answer decides whether the contract and the CPSE replies are asked for. A recorded fact always wins.
- **What the client is shown (F27, F30)**: the claim page quotes the client's step as the portal shows it, numbered (`portalStepLine` in `src/web/portal-steps.ts`); once a register has answered, the note under the figure says the checks have started (`rangeBasisFor`, `CLIENT_RANGE_BASIS_AFTER_RESEARCH` in `src/web/router.ts`), and a research run with an answer on a signed client raises the client's step to "We research your property" (`raiseToResearch` in `src/web/pipeline.ts`; raise only).
- **The wording (F23, F24, F29, F32, F40)**: the test letter's blank terms say what is true of the test copy; the Valuation Office rating list reads "Not used" on the lookups card and the Data gathering confirmation no longer names a VOA licence block; `STAFF_FOOTER` (`src/web/html.ts`); "St Judes" is a saint (`expandTokens` in `src/connectors/matching.ts`); "We issued your client report" (`issuedLine` in `src/web/portal-dashboard.ts`).
- **Accept** `test/phase158b.test.ts`; `phase146f`, `phase157d` and `signing-for-testing` restated (their invented clients are partnerships, so the letter says who is signing).

### Phase 157d — 6 October 2026: signing from an emailed link, a second signer, two upload checks and four document types
- **Why** The rest of Lakhbir's "docusign style" request, and three findings of the Friary House run (F16, F18, F19).
- **Signing from the emailed link** (`src/web/signing.ts`: `createSignLink`, `signLinkFor`, `signByLink`; `EngagementSigning.links`; `SIGN_LINK_DAYS` 7): where the signing setting says `links` — `ENGAGEMENT_SIGNING_TEST` only, so the test copy only — sending the letter emails each signer their own link, `/sign/{token}` (32 random bytes; only its SHA-256 kept, in `engagement_sign_links`). `GET /sign/{token}` (no login) shows the letter and the pad and uses nothing; `POST` signs once, as origin `EMAIL_LINK` with the link and no session, and marks the link used in the same transaction. Out of date, used, unknown or for a disabled login: "This link does not work". The certificate says the link was emailed to that address. Off for real clients until the solicitor answers Word question 246.
- **A second signer** (`setRequiredSigners`, `requiredSigners`, `signaturesNeeded`; `POST /staff/clients/{id}/engagement/signers`, `engagement.send`): staff tick one or two of the client's logins ("Who must sign", on the client page's letter section); none ticked means any one login, as before; refused once anyone has signed. A login not named is refused; nobody signs twice. The letter becomes SIGNED, and `checkSignature` (and so `assertSignedEngagement`) passes, only when every named signer has signed; each signature's PDF carries every signature so far and one certificate listing each signer, so the latest is the signed copy. Version 3 records also bind `signer_seq` and `link_id`.
- **Migration 098** rebuilds `engagement_signatures` (up to two a letter, `UNIQUE (engagement_id, signer_seq)`; origin `EMAIL_LINK`; the CHECK that a portal signature has a session and a link signature has its link and no session) and adds `engagement_required_signers` and `engagement_sign_links` (never deleted; only `used_at` ever set, once). Every row copied; the never-edited and never-deleted triggers made again; the foreign-key guard.
- **A file sent twice (F18)** (`acceptClientBytes` in `src/web/uploads.ts`; migration 097 adds `documents.content_sha256`): the SHA-256 of the bytes as they arrived is kept; the same bytes from the same business before are found, the copy is still kept, and the client is told "You sent us this same file on …" (the portal's `again=` date, and the upload page).
- **A damaged PDF (F19)** (`pdfLooksComplete` in `04-phase-work/phase3/caprebate-upload-handler.ts`, through the port): a PDF with no `%%EOF` in its last 1,024 bytes is refused before it is written, by `acceptClientBytes` and `receiveFile`, in plain words (`DAMAGED_PDF`).
- **Four document types (F16)** (migration 099, 056's rebuild; phase3 `DocType`, `DOC_SPECS`, `CLIENT_DOC_RECEIPT_POLICY`, `PERSON_REVIEW_BY_DOC_TYPE`, the name exclusion keep list): COMPLETION_STATEMENT (now carries a value; claim team), ACCOUNTS (claim team), CONTRACTOR_INVOICE and PLANS (claim evidence), each bound to the receipt policy of its own name with RET-7, on Phase 129's recommendation: strip the individuals, keep the figures. Five new segment kinds with heading rules appended last (COMPLETION_FIGURES, ACCOUNTS_STATEMENTS, FIXED_ASSET_NOTE, INVOICE_LINES, DRAWING_SCHEDULE); sixteen fields, none a name.
- **Accept** `test/phase157d.test.ts` (19); phase3 C1, C7, C8 and P21 restated; documents, phase129, phase151b-documents, web and phase157c restated.

### Phase 157b — 6 October 2026: the papers the 4 to 6 October decisions overtook, and the rate-card uplifts
- **Why** The Phase 157 sweep found seven papers still describing GoAddress, WhatsApp's live connection, Google Analytics, the Valuation Office's rating list and an unopened Homedata account, all overtaken on 4 to 6 October; and Word question 235 (the old extra charges) open with nothing built to check it against.
- **The papers** (OneDrive, each change marked in place and noted on its tracker row and board item): the privacy notice draft (12818279787), the list of who receives client personal data (12927931400, v1.5), Retention Determinations (12945903194), the Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery Plan (12552682536), Breach and Transparency Communications (12927894775), and the suppliers' breach duties (12927625052, v1.2: the published terms of Didit, Cloudflare, Zoom, Turso and Vercel, read 6 October 2026, §2D).
- **The job cost model** (`04-phase-work/phase9/caprebate-job-economics.cjs`): `homedata_cost_per_lookup` 0.17 (17 tokens a signed property: property 1, address 5, title check 1, planning 5, boundary 5; 19 where the address was typed), and the address search before signing on its own (`addressSearchCost`, `homedata_address_search_*`: 2p a press, 5 a visitor, £1 a day), carried by the enquiries behind a signed job into `loaded_including_address_lookup` only. The 8p list price, read once on 23 September and never again, is corrected wherever it was written as current (here, §5's two rows, and the plan).
- **The rate-card uplifts** (`src/web/fee-controls.ts`): `FEE_CONTROLS.uplifts` (off), `RATE_CARD_UPLIFTS`, `upliftReading`, `upliftPreview`; `FeeFacts.uplift_factors` and `manual_handling`; `ControlReadings.uplifts`. On, `computeFee` adds them after the first-year control and never on top of a fee that control lowered. The case stamp reads the switch. Word question 235 carries the worked figures.
- **Accept** `test/phase157b.test.ts` (7); phase9 D1 restated, D7 and D11 with the search, D12 and D13 new.

### Phase 157c — 6 October 2026: what the Friary House run found, sending the letter, and a DocuSign-style signature
- **Why** The end-to-end run of an invented client (`04-phase-work/sim-2026-10-06/Friary-House-simulation-findings.md`, OneDrive; F1 to F21) stopped at the engagement letter: nothing prepared or sent one (F21), and it could not be signed (F10). Lakhbir, 6 Oct 2026: "cant it be a docusign style document which captures a signed digital copy?" Signing itself stays off (`ENGAGEMENT_SIGNING`): the solicitor's answer and the letter's blanks still stand in the way.
- **Sending the letter** (`src/web/engagement-send.ts`; `POST /staff/clients/:id/engagement/send`, action `engagement.send`, Admin and Sales, never Read only): raises the engagement if there is none, marks it SENT with `sent_at` and `sent_by`, audits `ENGAGEMENT_LETTER_SENT` (`resent`, how many logins; no address), and emails every client login of the business the link `/portal/letter` through the outbox. Sending again re-sends the email and keeps the first sent time. Refused while the letter is signed or the client has no login. The client page's "Engagement letter" section (Files tab) shows each step with its time and who sent it. `markViewed` (`src/web/portal.ts`) keeps the first time the client opened it (`viewed_at`).
- **The signature, drawn or adopted** (`src/web/signing.ts`, `src/web/signature-pad-script.ts`, `src/web/pdf.ts`): the client draws in a box (strokes as whole numbers 0 to 1000; `parseInk`, `INK_LIMITS`: at most 60 strokes and 3,000 points; under 8 points or 6% of the box across is "too small") or ticks "Use my typed name as my signature"; neither is refused. A version 2 record (`record_version`) binds the method, every stroke, the signer's login email and the letter's prepared, sent and opened times into `record_sha256` (`caprebate-engagement-signature-v2`); version 1 records check exactly as before. The signed PDF draws the strokes (`PdfLine.ink`, stroked paths after the text) and ends with a certificate of completion: the four times, name, role, signed-in email, method, internet address, session and the letter's fingerprint. The signed copy, checked against its record first (`signedCopy`), downloads from `/portal/letter/signed.pdf` and `/staff/clients/:id/engagement/signed.pdf`.
- **The pad's script and the page's policy**: only while the letter can be signed (the form's pad is on the page) does the letter page carry `/assets/signature-pad.js` and `LETTER_PAGE_CSP` (every page's policy plus `script-src 'self'`); otherwise it is every page's policy and no script. The script sets no HTML or style, keeps nothing in the browser and sends nothing but the form.
- **Migration 096** (`096-phase157c-letter-sent-and-drawn-signature.sql`): ADD COLUMN only. `engagements`: `sent_at`, `sent_by` (users), `viewed_at`. `engagement_signatures`: `record_version` (1 or 2, default 1), `signature_method` (DRAWN or TYPED), `signature_ink` (valid JSON), `signer_email`, `letter_prepared_at`, `letter_sent_at`, `letter_viewed_at`. The never-edited and never-deleted triggers stand.
- **The run's other findings**: the waived free lookups ask the postcode check once per click for the nation (`nationFromCountry`, `src/connectors/nations.ts`; F12) and a stopped lookup keeps the reason its click wrote (F12b); the legal check reads the claimant type through `claimantTypeForGate` (F15); an unknown acquisition date shows the purchase month the client gave (`clientSaidPurchaseDate`; F14); a file the client sends goes on their claim when they have exactly one open (`CLIENT_ONE_CLAIM`; F20) and staff Home shows "New documents from clients" (14 days; `newClientDocuments`); a chosen file with no bytes is "that file is empty" (F17); on Zoom's free plan a call goes straight to the shared host (F5); the lead says to set Qualified before converting (F7); a converted claim takes the lead's earliest open follow-up with a due date as its next action, and the stage line says when one is still needed (F9); "Run the research" opens its tab (F11); the claim heading and the portal do not repeat the town (F8); the portal shows "The figure you were shown: up to £X" (F13); "A GP surgery holds…" (F2); the thank-you page says only the postcode was looked up and the call link is emailed only when sending is on (F4, F4b); the fourth enquiry check reads "Confirm no conflicting claim, ours or another adviser's, on this property" (F6); a hidden button stays hidden (F1).
- **Moved to Phase 157d**: signing from an emailed link, a second signer, duplicates (F18), a damaged PDF (F19), and the four document types nothing can receive yet (F16).
- **Accept** `test/phase157c.test.ts` (35); `phase146f`, `146h`, `146j`, `146k` sign with the typed tick; `phase153f`'s host per member of staff is on the paid plan; `phase133b`, `138` and `client-redesign` restated for the letter section and the research link.

### Phase 156d — 5 October 2026: Zoom on the free plan, one shared host
- **Why** Lakhbir: "yes change 156d so i dont pay", and "right now i dont need to add additonal staff contact@caprebate is enough". No Zoom licence is bought for testing; every call is hosted by contact@caprebate.co.uk (`ZOOM_HOST_EMAIL`). On the free plan a call stops at 40 minutes, Zoom keeps no cloud recording, and one Zoom user hosts one meeting at a time.
- **The plan setting** `ZOOM_PLAN` (key file and the test website's `TEST_DEPLOY_KEYS`; not secret): absent, or anything but `pro`, `paid` or `business`, is the free plan (`zoomPlanIsFree` in `src/connectors/zoom.ts`). Setting `ZOOM_PLAN=pro` once a licence is bought brings back the cloud recording and takes the 40-minute line away; nothing else changes.
- **The 40 minutes, said plainly** (`FREE_PLAN_LINE`, `zoomPlanLine` in `src/web/video-calls.ts`): on the call's page in the diary, on the host's video page, under the Communication tab's "Book and send the invite", and in the invitation email (`videoJoinWords(..., freePlan)`, from `sendVideoInvite`).
- **The walkthrough call on the free plan** (`WALK_WORDS`, `walkWords` in `src/web/survey-evidence.ts`; `src/web/evidence-pages.ts`): `makeZoomMeeting` asks for `auto_recording: 'cloud'` only on a paid plan, `'none'` otherwise. The client's pages say "A video call" and that we write down what we see for the surveyor, with the 40 minutes; the ask message, the filming guide and the staff chapter follow (no "Get the recording from Zoom" on the free plan; "Not recorded: our Zoom plan keeps no recordings."). The paid words are the ones approved on 2 Oct 2026, unchanged.
- **One call at a time** (`videoCallsAtSameTime`, `SAME_TIME_WORDS`): another live video call overlapping this one under the same Zoom user (a meeting's `host_user_id`, null the shared host; a call with no meeting yet counts as the shared host) is warned about on the call's page, on the host's video page, after giving or moving a time in the diary (it opens the call's page), and after booking from the Communication tab (`done=booked-zoom-same-time`). A warning, never a refusal.
- **How long it lasted** (`zoomPastCall` in `src/connectors/zoom.ts`; `checkCallLength`, `callLengthOf`, `callLengthWords` in `src/web/video-calls.ts`; migration 091 adds `call_minutes`, `call_people`, `call_checked_at`, `call_check_state` to `video_meetings`): `POST /staff/calendar/{id}/length`, for those who arrange calls, once the call has started. `GET /v2/past_meetings/{meetingId}` gives the minutes and `participants_count`; without a count, `GET .../participants?page_size=1` gives `total_records`. Two numbers are kept, never a name or an email; the audit line `VIDEO_CALL_LENGTH_CHECKED` keeps the state only. A refusal that names a paid plan is `NOT_ON_PLAN` ("Call length: not shown on our Zoom plan."); 404 or Zoom's code 3001 is `NOT_YET`; a later "not yet" keeps numbers already kept. The Communication tab shows the length on a call once asked. Scopes `meeting:read:past_meeting:admin` and `meeting:read:list_past_participants:admin`, added by Lakhbir on 5 Oct 2026.
- **The read-only command** `CAPREBATE_LOCAL_ZOOM=1 node src/cli.ts zoom-call-length <meeting id>` asks Zoom about one call and prints two numbers or Zoom's code and words (Phase 156d B1).
- **Accept** `test/phase156d.test.ts` (15); the free-plan walkthrough in `test/survey-evidence.test.ts` (four tests; its earlier tests now say `ZOOM_PLAN: 'pro'`, the paid path they describe); `test/phase151e.test.ts` lists `ZOOM_PLAN` among the test website's keys.

### Phase 154a1 — 4 October 2026: the faults the 3 October sweep found, fixed
- **A refused body closes its connection** (`src/web/router.ts` `readBody`, `bindResponse`; `src/server.ts` `readJson`): the rest of a body over its cap is read and dropped, up to 8 MB more, and the 413 carries `Connection: close`; a body more than that over is refused at once. The JSON API's cap is a 413 too (it was a plain error, a 500). Before, the next request on a kept-alive connection hung about 6 s and was reset.
- **Input** the compliance person search cuts its term to `SEARCH_TERM_MAX` (200) and says so (`compliance-rights.ts`; 50,000 characters was a 500); a contact, an introducer's person or a firm posted with no name field at all is refused (`crm.ts` `contactValues`, `introducers.ts` `firmValues`/`personValues`); control characters (not tab or line breaks) and direction marks and overrides are taken out of every form on the way in, never a password (`src/web/typed.ts`, read by `form()`, `multipart.ts` and `messages-pages.ts`).
- **A double click** makes one task: the same person, the same fields, within `DOUBLE_CLICK_MS` (10 s), inside the write's own transaction (`tasks.ts` `sameTaskJustMade`).
- **Screen readers** every label names its box (`compliance-views.ts` `input`/`area`/`choose` give each box a numbered id; the rest by `for=`, a named group or `aria-label`) and no heading skips a level; `test/phase154a1.test.ts` walks every page each login reaches and checks both. A saved contact goes back to its own company's page, read from the contact (`crm-pages.ts` `contactHome`). `code` text has a 12 px floor (`assets.ts`).
- **The test website's log** (`api/index.mjs` `outboxLogLine`): counts and kinds of email, never an address, a subject or a body.
- **The engine: the s.187A pooling requirement (R-B1)** (`Caprebate site.html`, `CB_S187A.poolingAssess`, asked first in `assess` and in each of the five flows before Stage 5): a purchase from a seller known to have been able to claim who never claimed is nil where it stopped owning after April 2014, and unresolved with nil at the bottom where that date, or whether it ever claimed, is not known (N4). The flag names s.187A(4) and what would meet it. The sale-date question is never hidden by the A-3 switch. The front door's "no, the previous owner did not claim" no longer sets "not entitled". Harness Suite G C1 is an ordinary check now (3 xfail remain).
- **The checks** the drift checker counts the back end over the test files git tracks and names any untracked one; the register check repeats that line. `whatsapp.test.ts` runs on a clock that never repeats an instant.
- **Accept** `test/phase154a1.test.ts`; harness Suite G (C1, C1u, C1d, C9, C1y, C1n, C1q, C1r), H10p and O6.

### Phase 153g — 4 October 2026: the survey pack's other ten chapters
- **Chapters 5, 8, 11, 14, 16, 17, 18, 19, 20 and 22** (`src/web/pack-sheets.ts`): each is a sheet of rows read from the pack, the claim (`claim-facts.ts`), the registers we hold (Land Registry title, the energy certificate, Homedata's estimate when fetched), the documents (`PLANS`), worked out from the pack's sums, fixed wording, or the surveyor's own words; each row says where it came from, and a missing one says "not on file", "not written yet" or "not worked out yet" (N4). No land, dwelling or qualifying fraction is drafted. Chapter 8 applies to supported living and care homes only; chapter 20 to refurbishment, fit-out or conversion only; each says why when it does not. Every chapter is now built and must be opened before approval.

- **The report**: the approved version keeps the ten sheets (`chapters`, `sheetsForVersion`); the client's report shows them as section 06, "The surveyor's chapters", on the page and in the PDF. A version approved before has none and shows none.
- **Accept** `test/phase153g.test.ts`.

### Phase 153f — 4 October 2026: the pack's chapters, the election's clock, each call's own Zoom host
- **Chapters 1, 3, 6, 12, 13 and 21** of the survey pack are built (`src/web/pack-chapters.ts`, `src/web/survey-pack-pages.ts`): the report and property; purpose and basis (with the evidence level); the room schedule from the job's room counts; fixtures item by item (pool, share, how it was seen, our test-default placements); the two pools; limits and assumptions (with the planning assumption). Each shows what the pack holds and "not on file", "not counted" or "needed" for anything missing. Each must be opened before approval. The other ten are Phase 153g.
- **The election window's clock** (`CB_ELECTION.windowOpen`, `Caprebate site.html`): reads the clock the engine is given (`s.__now`, as the back end's legal check passes it), the real clock only when none is given. Harness Suite AK.
- **Each video call's own Zoom host** (`src/web/video-calls.ts` `callHost`/`meetingHost`, `src/connectors/zoom.ts`, migration 084 `video_meetings.host_user_id`): one Zoom user hosts one meeting at a time, so a meeting is made under the assigned member of staff's sign-in email and the start token asked for that person; anyone not on our Zoom account falls back to the shared host, audited `VIDEO_CALL_SHARED_HOST`, and the call's page names the host. Call length and attendees need `meeting:read:past_meeting:admin` and `meeting:read:list_past_participants:admin` (Phase 156 A6/B6; nothing asks for them yet).
- **Accept** `test/phase153f.test.ts`; harness Suite AK.

### The survey pack — 4 October 2026, chapter 15 (grants) — Phase 153a
- **Why** F-15's remainder (Rulebook F §5: `.traced_elements[]`, "the field the whole module turns on"). Until now a grant on the claim was matched to its items in words only, and the pack's sums had no grant in them at all.
- **Route** chapter 15 of the pack, `GET /staff/survey/{job}/pack?ch=15` — read only: a grant is recorded, matched and removed on the claim (`/staff/claims/{id}/grants…`). Chapter 15 is built, so it must be opened before approval. Its VAT half is not built (Phase 153e).
- **The sums** `src/web/pack-grants.ts`, pure (no database, clock or switch): the claim's active grants come off the pack's sums in `packView`, so **every total, the legal check, approval, the approved version and the report read the figure after grants**; with no grant the sums are exactly `packSums`'s. **Off, as it ships** (`CB_CTA.grant_traced_to_elements`): every grant comes off in equal share — its amount as a share of the price paid, the `grant_fraction` route. **On**: a grant matched to fixture-list rows comes off only those counted lines' qualifying part (split by rebuild cost, capped at the line), then equal-share grants take their share of what is left. Insurance money is not taken off; a connected payer follows `connected_contributor_no_reduction`; **N4**: a payer or trace not known never relieves, and a reducing grant with no amount gives no total (`needed`, `where: 'GRANT'`). Whole pence in BigInt; the pools add to the penny.
- **Writes** migration 078: `claim_grant_elements` (the rows a grant paid for, written with the grant in one transaction by `F.recordGrant`, each id checked against the fixture list; never edited or deleted) and two claim facts, `GRANT_PROVIDER_CHECKED` and `GRANT_SPLIT_ADVISER_VIEW`. The "Add a grant" step 3 offers the sector's rows and the shared ones as tick boxes.
- **The gate out of valuation** (`src/web/grant-checks.ts`, read by `src/web/pipeline.ts`): no grant taken off in equal share without the grant papers received on the claim (`GRANT_FUNDING_AGREEMENT`) or the tax adviser's view recorded with the tax adviser as its source; and the client's confirmation that they checked with whoever paid. Both met with no grant left; not known never passes. The two answers are recorded from the grants section, `POST /staff/claims/{id}/grants/fact` (those two keys only).
- **Accept** `test/pack-grants.test.ts` (worked examples by hand, both switch positions, who paid, N4, 5,000 seeded packs: the pools add up, nothing negative, grants never add to a claim, on-with-nothing-matched equals off) and `test/phase153a.test.ts` (the claim record's rows, the pack, the approved version and the report, chapter 15, the gate, N4 on each). Red on the code before: `04-phase-work/phase153a/proofs/`.

### Finance — 3 October 2026: UI tidy phase 12 of 17
- **Why** Finance opened with a highlights strip, then a long note, then six tabs with Money first, and a right-hand panel whose three links were said again at the top and in the menu. On a laptop that panel pushed the eleven-column invoices table 15 px sideways.
- **Built** In `src/web/finance-pages.ts` `listPage`: a `pagehead` (Finance, its lede, Raise our fee where a claim may be raised, Run the VAT check); `kpiRow` — Invoiced, Received, Still owed and `lateBoxLabel(n)` ("Late, on 1 invoice" / "Late, on n invoices"), red only when an invoice is late, each landing on `#claim-invoices`; `readyLine` names every claim ready to invoice with its number, and Raise our fee where the login may raise one. The tabs now run invoices, money, vat, fees, settings ("Finance settings"), earlier, so Invoices opens first; `financeMenu` puts the same six in the left-hand menu under Finance, with the counts of invoices and earlier invoices, and from 900 px the menu replaces the tab row (`.financepage .rtabs`). Each claim's number sits under its company in both invoice tables; the Reference columns are gone. `invoiceDue`: no due date a grey label, late a red one, on time the words. The right-hand panel (`sidePanel`) is removed; nothing it offered is lost. The RECORDED, NOT COLLECTED note is a `help` line. Every section, id and heading `phase133b` checks is unchanged.

### Faster clicks — 3 October 2026 (asked by Lakhbir)
- **Why** Lakhbir: "The back end is really slow to react to clicks." Measured in a browser on the test website (read only): Home 4.6 s (10 s on the first click after a deploy), Clients 2.1 s, Pipeline 1.1 s, Tasks 0.9 s, for pages of 13 KB. The same pages build in 0.01 to 0.03 s on the Mac. The difference is the lasting database: on the test website every query is a trip over the network to Turso, and a click asked hundreds — Home 467 (Admin) to 737 (Sales), Clients 300, Tasks 234, Pipeline 161 — many of them the same question again ("which surveyor is on this claim?" 165 times on Sales's Home; a list label 73 times). The website runs in Vercel's iad1 (Washington); at 4 to 10 ms a trip the database is near it, so the count, not the distance, is the cause.
- **Built — part 1, one answer per question per click** `withReadMemo` in `src/db.ts`, wrapped round every request in `src/server.ts`: while one click is answered, a `get` or `all` with the same SQL and the same values is answered from what the click already has. It can never be out of date: any write (`run`, `execDdl`, and so every write inside `tx`) by any click in the process moves `#epoch` on and forgets everything; inside a transaction nothing is remembered or reused; each caller gets its own copy of the rows; a value that is a file's bytes is never remembered; outside a click (start-up, the seed, timers) nothing is remembered. `Db.trips` counts the engine's calls. Trips per click on the seed: Home 737 → 177 (Sales), 467 → 148 (Admin); Tasks 234 → 68; Pipeline 161 → 90; Clients 300 → 199; Leads 272 → 181.
- **Built — part 2, a local copy, switched off until the website's settings switch it on** `DbOptions.localCopy` in `src/db.ts`: for a remote database, libSQL's embedded replica — a file on the copy of the website that answers reads, with writes going to the lasting database — brought up to date once when opened and by `Db.refresh()` (one trip) at the start of every click (`src/server.ts`; a failed refresh is logged by name and the click goes on). `api/index.mjs`: `CAPREBATE_DB_LOCAL_COPY=on` in the website's settings asks for it (`lastingDatabase`); `openLasting` opens it in a temporary folder of its own and, if it cannot, opens the lasting database as before and says why, with the address hidden. NOT YET PROVED on Turso: there is no libSQL server on the Mac to test against; how the library's replica keeps foreign keys and transactions is to be checked on the test website before it is left on. Until the setting is added nothing changes.
- **Accept** `test/faster-clicks.test.ts` (10): one trip for a repeated read and its own for another; any write — run, a transaction committed or undone, DDL — forgets; a write by another click is seen at once; nothing remembered inside a transaction; own copies, no file bytes, two clicks never share; Home on the seed under 300 trips and the next click sees a new task; the setting's words; a local copy refreshed one trip at a time and forgetting this click's reads, never claimed by a database on the Mac; the website opening with a local copy, falling back with the reason, or without when the setting is off; every click refreshing first and going on when a refresh fails. 19 single-line mutations across both parts, all red (three first survivors closed: a database on the Mac given the option claimed a copy; the test now asks the same read again after DDL; the redundant forgetting at a transaction's commit and undo was taken out).

### Faster clicks 2 — 3 October 2026: "last seen" once a minute
- **Why** Measured on the test website after faster clicks merged (read only, signed in as Admin): Home 4.6 s → 2.2 s, but Tasks 0.9 s, Pipeline 1.1 s and Clients 2.1–3.5 s about as before. The times fit about 0.5 s a click plus about 11 ms a trip; Admin's Tasks and Pipeline had few repeated reads to lose. Every click also wrote the session's `last_seen_at` twice — the server and the page router each call `authenticate` — and each was a write to the lasting database's primary that also made the click forget every read it had.
- **Built** `authenticate` in `src/auth.ts` writes `last_seen_at` only when it is missing or at least `LAST_SEEN_EVERY_MS` (60 s) old. Its only reader is Admin's Home (`staffWorking`, "working now" = seen in the last 30 minutes; `peopleCard`, "last signed in", to the minute), so a minute's grain changes nothing shown; a session's end never read it (`expires_at`, revoked). An ordinary click now writes nothing: Admin's Tasks 28 → 24 trips, Pipeline 54 → 50, and no write among them.
- **Accept** `test/faster-clicks.test.ts` (11; one added): within a minute two clicks write no `last_seen_at`; past a minute one click writes it once, to the click's time; Admin's Home still draws. 4 single-line mutations, all red.
### `B9`/`B10` — the accounting view — extended by CRM 7 of 10
- **Route** `GET /staff/finance` · `GET /staff/finance/view.json` · `GET /staff/finance/{id}` · `POST /staff/finance/claims/{claim}/raise` · `POST /staff/finance/{id}/sent` · `/cancel` · `/credit-in-full` · `/credit-clause-13` · `/received` · `POST /staff/finance/received/{payment}/void` · `POST /staff/finance/vat-check` · `POST /staff/finance/settings` · `POST /staff/finance/{id}/status` (an organisation's invoice from before CRM 7 of 10 only).
- **Who** ACCOUNTING, ADMIN — **and nobody else**; the settings form is ADMIN's alone.
