- **Fields shown** by month: invoiced · credit notes · received · fees expected from claims at the invoice stage; outstanding and overdue; the VAT reading and its warning; the claims waiting at the invoice stage, each with its finance task; each claim invoice's number, date, amount, VAT, state, money read, outstanding and due — **UNSET** where the letter sets none; the fee controls and **what the letter says, BLANK where it is blank**; the finance settings with their date and source; model B's fees to firms, listed and never paid; an organisation's invoices from before, with **whether the fee exceeds the client's first-year saving** and the fee rate that would not.
- **Layout — Phase 133b** Highlights of the totals; six tabs — `#money` (section 1), `#vat` (2), `#invoices` (3 and 4), `#fees` (5 and 7), `#settings` (6), `#earlier` (8); each numbered heading carries an id — `money-by-month`, `vat-monitoring`, `to-invoice`, `claim-invoices`, `fee-controls`, `finance-settings`, `introducer-fees`, `earlier-invoices` — and Accounting's home cards link to `to-invoice`, `claim-invoices` and `vat-monitoring`.
- **Must never** Offer a field for an invoice's amount; join to the answers, the register data, the notes, the surveyor's workings or the estimate; show a plausible number where the letter is blank; send an invoice email.
- **Accept** `test/finance-claim.test.ts` and `test/finance.test.ts`: SALES and SURVEYING are refused the invoice and every route, writing nothing; ACCOUNTING is refused the assessment; the payload proof refuses the surveyor every CRM 7 route.

### `B11`/`B12` — settings, users and roles
- **Route** `GET /staff/settings` and `POST` `/profile` `/username` `/password` `/working-times` `/photo` `/photo/remove`; `GET /staff/users` · `POST /staff/users/create` · `/staff/users/{id}/role` · `/staff/users/{id}/disable`; and **Phase 146g** `GET /staff/email-addresses` · `POST /staff/email-addresses/lift` — **Addresses we cannot email**, ADMIN only, linked from the Administration block on this page with a count when any address is stopped. **Emails waiting to send now shows a bounce**: a hard one reads *Came back — that address does not work* with the server's code, a soft one *Came back — try again later*. Phase 146f recorded bounces and nothing showed them, so an email the server would not deliver looked exactly like one that had not been sent yet.
- **Roles** six since Phase 132e: the five, and `READ_ONLY`, which an administrator may assign and nobody holds. Migration 026 adds its row, dropping and recreating the `roles_fixed_insert` trigger around it; a seventh is refused at the database.
- **Must never** Let a role edit another user's identity. **Photograph handling stores nothing the user sent** — the bytes are decoded to pixels and a new file is written from those pixels, so a payload in a `tEXt` chunk or appended after `IEND` is gone **by construction** rather than by being stripped; decompression bombs are refused from `IHDR` before any inflate; PNG only, 2 MB.
- **Accept** `test/settings.test.ts`, `test/images.test.ts`.

### `B13`/`B14` — organisations — CRM 1 of 10
- **Route** `GET /staff/organisations` · `GET /staff/organisations/{id}` · `GET /staff/organisations/{id}/view.json` (the page's own object, since the CRM follow-on) · `POST /staff/organisations/{id}` · `POST /staff/organisations/{id}/contacts` · `POST /staff/contacts/{id}` · `POST /staff/organisations/{id}/properties`.
- **Phase 134** `GET /staff/organisations/{id}` asks `organisation.read` exactly as before and then answers **303 to `/staff/clients/{id}`**, the one client page (`B5`/`B6`), carrying its query. Its sections are drawn there by `organisationSections` in `src/web/crm-pages.ts`; the four POST routes redirect back to the tab they act on. The list links every name to the client page.
- **Phase 135** `GET /staff/organisations` (the list) asks `organisation.read` and answers **303 to `/staff/clients`**, the one Clients list (`B5`), carrying its query. A login refused the list is refused here, not sent on.
- **Who** `organisation.read` / `organisation.write` — ADMIN, SALES. Contacts panel `contact.read` / `contact.write` — ADMIN, SALES. **ACCOUNTING, SURVEYING and CLIENT are refused the section.**
- **Fields** trading name (the funnel's `name`, read-only here) · legal name · company number · claimant type · registered and trading address · website · client status · source · **referred by** — the firms whose referrals became its leads, read through those leads for `introducer.read` (the CRM follow-on) · owner. Each derived fact shows its source and **no input is drawn for it**.
- **Writes** `businesses`, `contacts`, `properties`. **Must never** Take a derived fact from a form; queue anything to a DO_NOT_CONTACT organisation; render the website as a link.
- **Accept** `test/crm.test.ts` — the refusal for every role not granted each action; the DO_NOT_CONTACT refusal through the transport and at the database, with its negative control; the stored cross-site scripting test on a contact's notes.

### `B15`/`B16` — properties — CRM 1 of 10
- **Route** `GET /staff/properties` · `GET /staff/properties/{id}` · `GET /staff/properties/{id}/view.json` · `POST /staff/properties/{id}` · `POST /staff/properties/{id}/claims` · `POST /staff/properties/{id}/site-point` · `POST /staff/properties/{id}/site-council` (Phase 151c) · `POST /staff/properties/{id}/title-register` · `POST /staff/properties/{id}/title-register/take-back` (Phase 146k).
- **Who** `property.enter`, then `property.read` — ADMIN, SALES, and SURVEYING `throughAssignedJob`. Writes and opening a claim: ADMIN, SALES.
- **`view.json` serves the object the page is drawn from**, as `payload.json` does for the survey, so N1 is verified on the wire.
- **Must never** Ask a jurisdiction the postcode already answers, or a prior-claim position the funnel's own answer already states (the CRM follow-on); store a person's name as a previous owner or occupier; store an acquisition price of zero.
- **Accept** `test/crm.test.ts` — jurisdiction derived and a recorded one refused; the N2 guard; N4 on the seed's UNKNOWN and never-asked properties, on the page and on the wire.
- **Phase 139 — the property page as coloured sections** (Phase 138's `section` component, carried): `#details` The property (WE NEED: when it was bought, freehold or leasehold, whether allowances were claimed before — each only while not known) · `#related` Claims on this property · `#documents` Documents · `#activity` Messages and activity. The old tab ids stay the anchors. Keys `property.details`, `property.claims`, `property.documents`, `property.activity` in `SECTION_KEYS`; `/staff/properties/{id}` is a return address `POST /staff/me/sections` accepts. **Accept** `test/phase139.test.ts`.
- **Phase 151c — where the site is, for its area** (`#site-point`, inside The property): the point the land boundary is read at — a British National Grid easting and northing a person enters, else the paid address lookup's own — and the councils whose files are read (the postcode's, and any neighbouring council a person adds for a site on a council boundary), each with who entered it and when. The two forms are **Admin's or Sales's (`property.write`) and only once the engagement letter is signed**, asserted on the server; before that the block says so. Kept in `site_area_inputs` (migration 052, append-only) and audited `SITE_POINT_ENTERED` / `SITE_COUNCIL_ADDED`. Saving sends nothing. **Accept** `test/phase151c.test.ts`.
- **Phase 146k — the title register, by hand** (`#title-register`, inside The property). HM Land Registry's title register is **looked up by a person, not by the API** (Lakhbir, 25 September 2026): they buy the official copy and type in what it says — the title number, freehold or leasehold, the price paid and its date, how many restrictions, the owner's company number where the owner is a company, and how many earlier owners it names. **There is no box for a person's name**, so Exception 2 of the name exclusion register is never used by hand. Stored through `ingestRegisterRecord` as source `HMLR_TITLE`, entity `by-hand:<title number>`, so every reader of register records reads it as before (the checks panel with the chip *HM Land Registry, entered by hand*; the surveyor's payload with the price withheld); the person's act is kept in `title_by_hand` (migration 055) and audited `TITLE_RECORDED_BY_HAND` with which facts were given, never their values. **Admin's or Sales's (`property.write`), only once the engagement letter is signed.** One entry stands per property; a wrong one is **taken back** with a reason (`TITLE_BY_HAND_TAKEN_BACK`), refused while a fact from it is approved, and what was worked out from it is listed, not un-derived. Not shown to the surveyor or the client. **Accept** `test/phase146k.test.ts`. **Phase 146l** adds the paperwork beside the typing: the block lists the official copies and title plans uploaded for the client and has its own upload box (document type `TITLE_REGISTER`, migration 056, read by a person, never a model). **Accept** `test/phase146l.test.ts`.

### `B17`/`B18` — claims — CRM 1 of 10
- **Route** `GET /staff/claims` · `GET /staff/claims/{id}` · `GET /staff/claims/{id}/view.json` · `POST /staff/claims/{id}` · `POST /staff/claims/{id}/team` · `POST /staff/claim-team/{id}/end` (the team, since the CRM follow-on) · `POST /staff/claims/{id}/events` (an event on the claim's building, Phase 159c).
- **Who** `claim.enter`, then `claim.read` — ADMIN, SALES, ACCOUNTING, and SURVEYING `throughAssignedJob`. Value: `claim.read_value`, ADMIN and SALES. Money: `invoice.read`. Writes: `claim.write`, ADMIN and SALES. The team's links: `claim_team.write`, ADMIN and SALES.
- **Writes** `claims` — owner, surveyor, priority, workstream, property, expected completion, hold and lost reasons. **Not the stage** — that moves in the claim page's pipeline panel, through `POST /staff/claims/{id}/stage` (B21) — **and not the next action**, which this form refuses with its reason (B23). `claim_contacts` (migration 020) — the organisation's own accountant and adviser linked to this claim, ended and never deleted.
- **Fields — the CRM follow-on** Came from · Source · Introducer (`lead.read`; the firm `introducer.read`) · **The claim team**: owner · surveyor, and who can reach the claim · tax adviser, as the sign-off record (`claim_figures.read`) · the organisation's accountant and adviser on this claim (`contact.read`), with **Add to the team** and **Take off the team** · **Our estimated fee** (`invoice.read`), until a fee invoice is live.
- **Must never** Put the value, the tax benefit or any sealed estimate field in a SURVEYING or ACCOUNTING response; let a claim point at another organisation's property (a composite foreign key refuses it); delete a claim or change its reference; store a firm, a source or a fee on the claim; link a contact of another organisation, or one not typed accountant or adviser; draw a surveyor anything on the team it could not already read.
- **Accept** `cd 09-app/backend && CAPREBATE_ALLOW_SEED=1 node tools/payload-proof.ts` exits 0 — see §6, N1 — and `test/crm.test.ts` and `test/follow-on.test.ts`.
- **Phase 139 — `#claim-checks` Check and tell the client**, after `#claim-facts`, for ADMIN and SALES: this claim's six steps, drawn by `claimChecksBlock` from the same `checksPanel` as the client page (`B5`/`B6`), with a link to the company's own step. And at phone width a long unbroken value in a list (the sign-off record's 64-character code under Figures) now wraps (`dd{overflow-wrap:anywhere}` in the 720 px rules) — it widened the page to 600 px with Figures open.
- **Phase 138 — the claim page as coloured sections.** The same `section` component as `B5`/`B6`, in this order, each built only where the claim's view carries its part: `#move` Stage and next move (workstream, expected completion, hold and lost reasons; the pipeline panel with the forward move open and "Other moves: close it, put it on hold, resume or reopen" and "Every move so far" folded; "Change the owner, surveyor or priority" folded) · `#claim-facts` The claim's facts (WE NEED lists the facts a client can tell us that are not known, each with an **Ask the client** that posts to the company's question route naming this claim, fact id `claim_fact.<key>`; `openClaimQuestions` in `src/web/engaged.ts` reads the open ones back so they read "Asked — waiting"; the recording forms folded under "Record a fact or an owner" since Phase 143) · `#claim-grants` Grants and contributions (Phase 143, `B50`) · `#claim-figures` Figures (the four figures, the sign-off, the value) · `#claim-property` The property (acquired, price, tenure, jurisdiction, claimed before) · `#claim-team` The team (and "Where this claim came from" folded) · `#claim-documents` Documents (the deliverables register folded) · `#claim-money` Money (the estimated fee, the invoices, the multi-site discount folded — Sales, who reads only the discount, sees that alone) · `#claim-aftercare` Aftercare · `#activity` Messages and activity. **Gone** (the bloat cut above): "The claim" fields that repeated the heading, the highlights and the right-hand panel; the Overview's priority and surveyor; the highlights' Property; the copy of the organisation's contacts. A surveyor's page has no Money, no Figures and no ask; Accounting's has Money and no facts or figures. **Accept** `test/phase138.test.ts`, and the payload proof.
- **Phase 134 — the company leads.** The claim page's heading and browser tab lead with the **company's name**; the property and the reference sit under it, the reference as a small grey label; a breadcrumb "Company > Property" frames it. The same page is drawn at `/staff/claims/{id}` and inside its company at `/staff/clients/{company}/claims/{id}`. The claims list's columns are **Company, Property, Stage, Next action, Owner, Reference** — the reference last and grey — and it sorts by company by default (`company`, "Company, A to Z"); the export's columns lead with the company and end with the reference. **No staff screen uses a claim reference as a heading, as link text or as a row's first cell**: `test/phase134.test.ts` crawls every screen every staff login reaches and reads the rendered HTML for it. Every claim link elsewhere — the board's cards, home, tasks, the dashboard, finance, the document centre, introducers and search — names the company or the property and puts the reference beside it in grey (`claimRef`, `claimLink` in `src/web/components.ts`).

### `B19`/`B20` — leads — CRM 2 of 10
- **Route** `GET /staff/leads` · `POST /staff/leads` · `GET /staff/leads/{id}` · `GET /staff/leads/{id}/view.json` · `POST /staff/leads/{id}` · `POST /staff/leads/{id}/checks` · `POST /staff/leads/{id}/convert`.
- **Phase 135** the list opens with the Leads filters (`LEAD_FILTERS`): All leads, and Filled assessments (`JS-B1`).
- **Phase 137 — cards in four sections** (`leadCardsPage` in `src/web/pipeline-pages.ts`, drawn by `leadSections` in `src/web/lead-cards.ts`). **New — not contacted yet** (status NEW, and every `website_enquiries` row at NEW for a login that may handle them; always first, never folded), **In progress** (CONTACTED, QUALIFIED; a status the list does not know lands here, never hidden), **Later** (NURTURE), **Closed** (CONVERTED, LOST; a folded `<details>`). Each heading carries its count and, where any, how many are red. **Order is waiting time — `lead_at` ascending in the three open sections, newest first in Closed — never value; no card carries a range** (the no-lead-score rule). A card is a `<details>`: the summary is the company (large), sector (`LeadListRow.sector`, new), source, waiting time, owner and next step; the body is the store's own `leadView` for that lead — how to reach them, what the assessment captured (the range only where `estimate.read` already allows it), the four enquiry checks — then **Log a call**, **Email** (disabled until Phase 141), **Add next action** (`POST /staff/tasks` with `lead_id`, owned by the person pressing it), **Make them a client** (`POST /staff/leads/{id}/convert`, drawn only when `may_convert`) and **Mark as lost** (`status=LOST` with a required `lost_reason`). A lead with no owner shows one button outside the fold, **Nobody owns this — take it** (`owner_id` = the actor, offered only when the actor is in `ownerChoices`), and none of the others. **Red**: New — `isLateNewLead` (more than one working day, `src/web/working-days.ts`, the function Home's New leads row reads); In progress and Later — the next step overdue or missing, Home's next-actions rule. The filters and saved views are `listControls` behind one **Filter** `<details>` (open when a filter or a view is applied; the order control is left out, since the cards have their own). **List view** is `GET /staff/leads?layout=list` — the table as it was, now `table.stack` so it becomes cards on a phone; `layout` is carried by the filter form (`opts.keep`) and the sortable headings (`sortHeading`'s `keep`), and neither `layout` nor `done` is passed to `readParams` to be reported as an unapplied filter. A POST carrying `back=leads` — take it, Mark as lost, closing an enquiry from its card — returns to `/staff/leads`; `back` is one fixed word, never a path.
- **Phase 137 — website enquiries are not folded into leads, and why.** An enquiry is not a lead until somebody opens it as one: it has no lead row, no owner, no enquiry checks, no audit line of its own as a lead, and it holds a person's name and email, which a lead never holds (a lead carries no person; the person becomes a contact on the lead when it is opened, through `createLead`). Its section is `lead.write` on `__website_enquiries__` — Read only, which reads leads, must not read it. So `/staff/enquiries` stays its own page and route, and the unanswered ones are **drawn as cards at the top of New** for the logins that may handle them, with the enquiry's own two buttons, because to the person working the page both are "nobody has contacted them yet". They are drawn only on the unfiltered page: a filter is a question about leads.
- **Who** `lead.read`, `lead.write`, `lead.convert` — ADMIN, SALES; READ_ONLY reads the cards (no button is drawn, and every write refuses it). The range shown asks `estimate.read`. **ACCOUNTING, SURVEYING and CLIENT are refused the section.**
- **Fields** organisation (or, for a referral, its name and sector) · what made it a lead · source · introducer (a column CRM 5 of 10 gives its foreign key) · lead date · owner · priority · next action and due · status · lost reason from the list · conversion. **No score, no probability, no person.**
- **Must never** Qualify on anything but four recorded YES; convert without asking the gate again; re-key what the assessment captured.
- **Accept** `test/leads.test.ts`; Phase 137's cards, sections, red rule, buttons and List view `test/phase137.test.ts` (18 tests; 15 fail against the `.bak-phase137` files).
- **Phase 139 — a lead's own page as coloured sections** (Phase 138's `section` component, carried): `#details` The lead (WE NEED: each enquiry check not yet a yes; its checks headed "Can we take this on? The checks"; the change and convert forms) · `#related` Where it came from · `#activity` Messages and activity. Keys `lead.details`, `lead.related`, `lead.activity`; `/staff/leads/{id}` is an accepted return address. **Accept** `test/phase139.test.ts`.

### `B21` — the pipeline board, and a claim's moves — CRM 2 of 10
- **Route** `GET /staff/pipeline` · `GET /staff/pipeline/view.json` · `POST /staff/claims/{id}/stage` · `POST /staff/claims/{id}/milestones` · `POST /staff/claims/{id}/checks`.
- **Who** `pipeline.read` — ADMIN, SALES. Moves and confirmations `claim.move` — ADMIN, SALES; a reopen `claim.reopen` — ADMIN alone. A card's value asks `claim.read_value`, card by card.
- **Dragging is a button.** The content security policy forbids script and every page works without JavaScript, so a card carries a form asking to move it one stage on; it posts the same move the claim page makes and **the server asks the gate again**.
- **Must never** Put a value on a card for anyone who cannot read the estimate; let the button stand for the gate.
- **Accept** `test/pipeline.test.ts` and the payload proof.

### `B22` — tasks and my tasks — CRM 3 of 10
- **Route** `GET /staff/tasks` (`?view=all` for Admin and Sales) · `GET /staff/tasks/view.json` · `POST /staff/tasks` · `GET /staff/tasks/{id}` · `POST /staff/tasks/{id}` · `POST /staff/tasks/{id}/status`.
- **Phase 135** the page opens with the Tasks filters (`TASK_FILTERS`): My tasks, Every open task (`?view=all`, for a login `mayCreateTask` admits) and Claims with no next step (`B24`). The Tasks tab carries the open-task count.
- **Who** read and progress: whoever `staff_task.read` admits to the task; create, change and cancel: Admin, Sales.
- **Fields** task · description · activity type · what it is about, each linked only where the reader may open it · owner, or the team it waits with · due date, with **OVERDUE** in words · priority · recurrence · status, with who completed or cancelled it, and why.
- **Must never** Delete a task; change a closed one; count a task with no due date as overdue or as on time.
- **Accept** `test/tasks.test.ts`.

### `B23` — a claim's next action, tasks, email and timeline — CRM 3 of 10
- **Route** panels on `GET /staff/claims/{id}` · `POST /staff/claims/{id}/next-action` · `POST /staff/claims/{id}/notes` · `POST /staff/claims/{id}/email`. A move to `POST /staff/claims/{id}/stage` may carry `next_action_title`, `next_action_owner` and `next_action_due`, written with the move.
- **Who** next action `claim.write`; note `internal_note.write`; email `email.queue`. `view.json` carries the panels as `tasks` and `timeline`, built forwards.
- **The organisation (B14), property (B16) and lead (B20) pages** draw the same timeline for their subject, and a task form. A surveyor on a property page is drawn no timeline.
- **Must never** Let the claim's own edit form write a next action — it is refused, with the reason; give Accounting more than its financial timeline; give a surveyor more than their own tasks and a timeline of stage labels.
- **Accept** `test/tasks.test.ts`, `test/crm.test.ts` and the payload proof.
- **Phase 141 — the claim's Email panel is a button.** It opens the company's Email page (`B48`) with this claim chosen. `POST /staff/claims/{id}/email` still merges a template to the outbox for any caller that posts to it, and its rules are unchanged.
- **Phase 134 — one timeline per company.** The company's timeline is on its one client page (`B5`/`B6`); a claim page draws that claim's part of it, headed so, with a link to the whole. A claim in a timeline is named by its property and ends " · CR-2026-0007", the reference drawn as the grey label; a note's "About" reads the same, or "the company in general". Everything that talks to the client — notes, questions, document requests — is stored on the company with an optional claim, so one timeline holds it all.

### `B24` — the one-next-action list — CRM 3 of 10
- **Route** `GET /staff/next-actions` · **Who** `pipeline.read` — Admin, Sales. Every open claim breaking the rule, then every claim keeping it with the next action overdue, each with who is waiting.
- **Phase 135** a view of **Tasks**, titled "Claims with no next step", headed by the Tasks filters; the Tasks tab is current on it. Its two blocks read "No next step, or no owner or date" and "Next step overdue".

### `B25` — automation rules — CRM 3 of 10
- **Route** `GET /staff/automation` · `POST /staff/automation` · `POST /staff/automation/{id}` · **Who** read Admin, Sales; write Admin. The forbidden effects are listed on the page with their reasons and refused by the store. What each rule did is below them. Since CRM 9 of 10 it is also listed on the admin settings screen, `B43`, and a rule may ask the client for a document of a type something can receive. Since the CRM follow-on the page says each forbidden refusal once, and a rule that asks for a document is saved with the document it names.

### `B26` — email templates — CRM 3 of 10
- **Route** `GET /staff/templates` · `POST /staff/templates` · `POST /staff/templates/{id}` · **Who** read Admin, Sales; write Admin. A template carrying bank details or an invoice is refused.
- **Phase 134 — an email's subject leads with the company.** `companyFirst` in `src/web/templates.ts` puts the company's name in front of any merged subject that does not already start with it; migration 032 rewrites the seeded subject to "{{organisation}} — the next step on your claim" while it still holds the seeded words. A **staff notice** from an automation rule is BUILT BOTH WAYS AND SWITCHED OFF: `NOTICE_SUBJECT_LEADS_WITH_COMPANY` in `src/web/automation.ts` is `false`, so the notice says what happened and ends with the reference — "A claim is now at Survey (CR-2026-0004)" — and names no company, until Lakhbir answers "Should an email to our own staff name the client company in its subject line?" in CAPREBATE QUESTIONS.

### `B47` — the client's files — Phase 140

Lakhbir: *"There is no folder icon which allows the user to see what the customer has uploaded, but also the ability to
upload and save documents ourselves within the portal."*

- **The button.** The client page's header carries **Files** — a folder icon, the word, and a count of the files this
  login may open (`filesButton` in `src/web/client-files-pages.ts`). The page's last section is now **Files**
  (`#documents`, kind Documents): one tile per folder (`folderTiles`), WORKING ON "Waiting for N files from the client"
  while any request is open.
- **The folders** (`GET /staff/clients/{id}/files`, `clientFolder` in `src/web/documents.ts`): **From the client** (a
  document whose first version a client made), **From Cap Rebate** (staff made it), **Asked for — still waiting** (open
  document requests, for `document_request.read` only), and **one per claim** (documents linked to the claim or its
  property), each a tile with a count; the claim's reference is a small grey label **outside** the tile's link. A folder
  is a way of looking, not a place: one lease can be in "From the client" and in its claim's folder. **Every file is asked
  of `document.read` on its own**, so a login sees only what it could open (Accounting: no lease; a surveyor is refused
  the page by `organisation.read`).
- **A file** is a card: its name ("Lease 2" — never the client's file name, N2), who sent it ("The client", or "Cap
  Rebate — <the member of staff>"), the date its newest version arrived, *"Read and then deleted — here is what we took
  from it."* (or **Open the original** where one is kept and the login may open it), **what we read from it in counts
  only** — pages read (step 4), parts of the text kept (step 5 `retained`), parts taken out (step 5 `removed`), from
  the handler's own `document_events` — never its words, and a badge **The client can see this** / **Only Cap Rebate
  can see this** with the button to change it (on files we sent, for `document.show_to_client`). A refused file says
  why in plain words.
- **Upload** (Admin, Sales): a file picker drawn as a large drop area (the browser's own picker takes a dropped file —
  no script, the content security policy allows none), **What is it?** (types with a receipt policy), **Which claim is
  it for?** (the whole company, or one of its claims), **Let the client see this file** — **unticked by default**.
  `POST /staff/clients/{id}/files` asks `document.upload` before reading the body, then `receiveFile`: a cheap size and
  content check first (so a refused file leaves no half-made document), then `startDocumentUpload` →
  `acceptClientBytes` (the single-use key from the link, never shown; the size cap and the content check again, on the
  bytes — that is the control) → `finishUpload` (the receipt contract). **The claim page's upload form and a document's
  "Upload a new version" are the same form now.** No screen shows an HTTP `PUT` address; the staff link page
  (`ticketPage`) is deleted.
- **Who may see a file** (migration 035): `document_visibility`, one row per decision, never updated or deleted; the
  latest row wins. With no row, a document the client made is theirs to see and one staff made is not. Staff uploads
  always write a row. `documentObj` carries `shown_to_client`, and `clientDocument` in `src/authz.ts` admits a client only
  where it is `true` — **absent is not true**. The issued client report released by the tax adviser's sign-off
  (`released_to_client`, CRM 6 of 10) is not asked a second time: the release is the decision to show it. Existing
  staff-made documents with no row now read "Only Cap Rebate" until somebody shows them — on the test site that is only
  invented data.
- **KEEP_ORIGINALS** (`src/web/originals.ts`, shipped off; stamped by the case stamp): see *Keeping the originals* in the list of switches
  above. `documents.original_kept_at`, `original_delete_after`, `original_deleted_at` (migration 035) stay
  empty while it is off. `node src/cli.ts sweep-originals` deletes the kept originals whose days are up.
- **Audit** `DOCUMENT_SHOWN_TO_CLIENT`, `DOCUMENT_HIDDEN_FROM_CLIENT`, `DOCUMENT_ORIGINAL_OPENED`,
  `DOCUMENT_ORIGINAL_DELETED` — ids only.
- **Must never** Show a client a file staff did not choose to show; read an upload's body before asking `document.upload`;
  keep an original in the clear, or without a period, or past it; show an upload address; read what a file says onto a
  screen.
- **Accept** `test/phase140.test.ts` (count: `node --test test/phase140.test.ts`); `04-phase-work/phase140/proofs/phase140-mutations.log` breaks seven of
  these rules one at a time and each turns a named test red.

### `B48` — free-typed email to a client, from contact@caprebate.co.uk — Phase 141

Lakhbir: *"When on a client page there needs to be the ability to free-type emails, which should come from
contact@caprebate.co.uk."*

- **The button.** **Email** (an envelope icon and the word) sits beside **Files** in the client page's header, on every
  open lead card (where "Email — Coming soon" was), and in a claim page's Email panel, which opens the company's Email
  page with that claim already chosen (`?claim={id}`). The claim page no longer merges a template in place. Admin and
  Sales only (`email.queue`); the view-only login, Accounting, the surveyor and the client see no button and are
  refused the page.
- **1 October 2026 — the email page in two halves, To filled in and changeable, replies in their conversation** (supersedes
  the To drop-down and the list below). Lakhbir: the To field "needs to be auto populated with the emails address, however
  it needs to allow users to overide the email if needed", and "the right hand side should show the chain of emails
  allowing a user to click on reply making them apart of the chain … if no current email chain (on the right) is
  selected then it fires as a new email". **Left** (`#compose`): **To** is a box (`to_email`), filled with the
  conversation's correspondent, else the contact asked for, else the company's main address where it is a contact's,
  else the first contact; the contacts are suggestions (`<datalist>`); any address may be typed. One that is a contact's
  (any case) is recorded as that contact; any other with no contact — and a reply from it waits under **Email replies to
  sort**, the filing rule unchanged. Not an address, or a lead's contact who did not agree to be contacted, is refused; do
  not contact as always. **Right**: every conversation with them (`emailChains`, `src/web/email-chains.ts`), newest
  first and open: our emails (every kind except a sign-in code or one-use link; to an address not theirs only where staff
  typed it) and their replies (`client_message.read` only), oldest first, theirs marked **New** until read. **Reply**
  (`?thread={key}`) makes the left a reply: "Re: " the subject, to whoever wrote last, and the POST carries `thread`, asked
  again on the server ("That conversation is not one of this company’s" — 400, nothing written). The email records
  `thread_key`, `in_reply_to` and `references_ids` (migration 065, never edited) and goes with **In-Reply-To** and
  **References** (`buildMessage`; only plain Message-IDs, so no header can be smuggled), so their mail program keeps it in
  one conversation and their answer comes back matched to it. A key is the first email's id, or the id of a reply of
  theirs filed by hand. Opening the page reads their emails (`channel_reads`, below). Audit `EMAIL_WRITTEN` adds
  `to_contact` and `reply` (true or false) — never an address. **Accept** `test/conversations.test.ts`,
  `test/phase141.test.ts` (the form and the To tests restated).
- **The page** (`GET /staff/clients/{id}/email`, `GET /staff/leads/{id}/email`; store `emailForm` in
  `src/web/free-email.ts`, page `src/web/free-email-pages.ts`): **From** — the one sender, shown; **To** — a drop-down
  of the company's own contacts that have an address, **never a box to type an address into**, and a line naming
  anybody left out and why ("no email address recorded"); **Which claim is it about?** — the whole company or one of
  its claims; **Subject** and **Message**, typed freely; **Attach files** — the company's files the client can already
  see whose original is still kept, up to five (with `KEEP_ORIGINALS` off, none, and the page says *"You cannot attach
  a file yet. We delete each file once we have read it."*); **Start from a template** — a small form that reloads the
  page with the template merged as plain text into the two boxes (`mergePlain`), visible gaps and all, for staff to
  edit. Below: **Emails to {company}**, the last twenty, each with who it went to, when, the claim, the number of files
  and whether it was sent. A do-not-contact company shows *"You cannot email this company."* and no form. From a lead
  with no company record yet, only a person who agreed to be contacted (`contact_consent = 1`) is offered, and nothing
  can be attached.
- **Saving** (`POST` to the same address; `saveEmail`): the contact asked by id and checked to be this company's (or
  this lead's) with an address; the claim checked to be the company's; a subject (one line, up to 300 characters) and a
  message (up to 20,000); **the same refusals as a template** (`freeTextRefusal` in `src/web/templates.ts`: no bank
  details or anything shaped like them, no invoice — it waits on the invoice-fraud procedure — and nothing that would
  tip somebody off); each file checked again. A refusal draws the page again **with what was typed**, status 400, and
  saves nothing. Saved, it goes through `outboxTransport` as kind `FREE_TYPED`, with the claim, the lead, the contact,
  the writer and the files (`outbox_attachments`), is audited `EMAIL_WRITTEN` (ids, a file count and whether sending was
  on — never the subject, the body or an address), and the page says ***"Saved — emails are not being sent yet."***
- **From and Reply-To, as one setting** (migration 036 `email_sender`; `currentSender` in `src/stubs/email.ts`): the
  latest row, and the first is **"Cap Rebate", contact@caprebate.co.uk for both**. It is stamped on **every** outbox
  row, whatever wrote it — a free-typed email, a template, a notice, a password reset — as `from_header` and
  `reply_to`. Admin changes it under **Who emails come from** on admin settings (`POST /staff/admin-settings/email-sender`,
  `setSender`, audited `EMAIL_SENDER_SET`); the old row stays, so an earlier email's sender is always known. No
  address is written in code.
- **On the timeline.** `outbox` in `src/web/timeline.ts` now names who wrote it and who it went to — *"An email to Sam
  Hart: “Your survey date” — saved, not sent"* — and scopes it to its claim where one was picked; a lead's emails
  (with no company yet) are on the lead's timeline. Unsent is staff-only, as before; sent reads *"— sent {when}"*.
- **The outbox is never edited** (migration 036): what an email said, who it went to and who it came from cannot be
  updated; a sent time, once written, is never changed; nothing is deleted; a claim or a file on an email is one of
  the email's own company's (two triggers). **One exception since Phase 151k (migration 058):** once a sign-in code
  (`SIGN_IN_CODE`) or a password reset link (`PASSWORD_RESET`) has really been SENT, its body — and nothing else — is
  replaced by one fixed sentence (`SENT_SECRET_BODY`, `blankSentSecrets` in `src/stubs/email-provider.ts`), so the code
  or link is not kept. Who it went to, when, the subject and the kind stay. **Since Phase 152 (migration 060) the
  sign-up confirmation link (`ACCOUNT_VERIFY`) is the third kind**, and the sentence names all three; a row 058 already
  blanked keeps 058's sentence.
- **Sending — `EMAIL_SENDING`, ON FOR TESTING since 30 Sep 2026 under a recorded waiver (see §1 above); built and shipped off before** (`src/stubs/email-provider.ts`, stamped by the case stamp). See
  the list of switches above. Off, every row is saved with `send_requested = 0` and `dispatchOutbox` returns `OFF`
  without opening a connection. The adapter built is **SMTP over TLS** (`smtpProvider`: EHLO, AUTH PLAIN, MAIL, RCPT,
  DATA, QUIT on `node:tls`, no dependency; plain-text UTF-8 body and each file base64, headers with a line break
  refused, non-ASCII headers RFC 2047-encoded), with its login in `CAPREBATE_SMTP_HOST`, `CAPREBATE_SMTP_PORT` (465),
  `CAPREBATE_SMTP_USER` and `CAPREBATE_SMTP_PASSWORD`. On, the page sends the email it has just saved and says
  **Sent.**, or keeps it with the server's reason; `node src/cli.ts send-outbox` sends whatever is waiting. **A row saved
  while sending was off is never sent later**, and do-not-contact is asked again at the moment of sending.
- **Where replies to contact@ will land, and how they will reach the timeline — BUILT BY PHASE 142, switched off (`INBOUND_EMAIL`); see `B49`, which is what was built. The design as Phase 141 set it:**
  A reply goes to the Reply-To, **contact@caprebate.co.uk**, which is a real mailbox at the email provider (with the
  recommended IONOS mailbox, the same place the mail is sent from). Nothing in this build reads that mailbox. The design:
  (1) **every email we send carries a `Message-ID`** (`dispatchOutbox` stores it as `outbox.provider_ref`); a reply's
  `In-Reply-To` / `References` header names it, which is how a reply is matched to the email, the company and the
  claim it answers; (2) a **poller** reads new mail in the contact@ mailbox over IMAP (IONOS) — or a webhook, if the
  provider chosen offers inbound parsing — on a schedule, with a read-only login of its own; (3) a matched reply is
  written to a new append-only table (`inbound_email`: the outbox row it answers, the sender's address, the time, the
  subject, the text part only, any files **through the receipt contract** as an upload by that company — never
  stored raw), and read into the company's timeline by `timeline.ts` as *"A reply from {contact}"*, tagged to the same
  claim; (4) a reply that matches no email, or comes from an address that is not one of the company's contacts, goes
  to a staff queue to be filed by hand, and is **never** attached to a company by its sender's address alone; (5) a
  reply from a do-not-contact company is recorded and nothing is sent back. The mailbox itself stays where a person
  can read it: the poller copies, it does not delete.
- **Must never** Write to an address that is not one of the company's contacts; send a file the client cannot see;
  send from an address written in code; send a row saved while sending was off; send to a do-not-contact company;
  edit what an email said; put an address or an email's words in the audit log or in an address bar.
- **Accept** `test/phase141.test.ts` (23 tests, including the SMTP adapter against a fake mail server);
  `04-phase-work/phase141/proofs/phase141-mutations.log` breaks six of these rules one at a time and each turns a named
  test red.

### `B49` — chat on every client: "Team only" and "With the client" — Phase 142

Lakhbir: *"There needs to be a chat feature internally and externally for all clients. Clients should not see internal
chats, however they should be able to send and receive communication, also with the ability to upload documentation
which automatically saves within their client portfolio."*

- **Two threads per company, in two tables — that is the control.** **Team only** is `internal_notes`: it already was a
  table of its own, read only through `internal_note.read` (Admin and Sales). Every note ever written is already a
  message on it, with its date, author and claim, so **nothing was copied** — a copy would be the same personal data
  stored twice with two retention clocks. Migration 037 makes a note never edited and never deleted (nothing ever did
  either). **With the client** is `client_messages` (migration 037): company, claim (one of its own, by trigger),
  direction (`FROM_CLIENT` / `TO_CLIENT`), source (`PORTAL`, `STAFF`, `EMAIL`), author or contact, words (up to 8,000),
  time; never edited, never deleted; a client login writes only on its own company's thread (trigger).
- **Who** (`src/authz.ts`): `client_message.read` / `.write` — Admin, Sales, and the client on its own company
  (`ownBusiness`). **Phase 142b, Lakhbir's answer of 22 September 2026: the surveyor reads With the client** —
  `client_message.read` only, `throughAssignedJob` on the company (the object carries the surveyors with a survey job
  on it, and fails closed without the list), on a read-only **Messages** tab of their job page (`surveyorThreadPanel`);
  never `.write`, never the Team only thread, and no "waiting for a reply" tile. Accounting and the view-only login see
  neither (`NO`). `inbound_email.read` / `.file` — Admin and Sales.
- **1 October 2026 — CHAT BECOMES COMMUNICATION** (`src/web/communication.ts`; supersedes the Chat section's three tabs
  below, whose routes all still work). Lakhbir, from an approved mock-up: "a chat window, drop down box choosing whats
  app, email, or portal or internal team only … a message box (and subject line if needed) … on the right hand side … a
  whole conversation flow … log a call can be on here as well. and the chat word turns in to communication"; "clicking
  essentially acts like a reply … the email and also number should auto populate"; "zoom calls on here also … starts to
  call them, or allows us to book it in … see when a zoom call was held"; "a search feature". **Left, one form** (`POST
  /staff/clients/{id}/communicate`, `communicate`): **Send by** — Email · WhatsApp · Portal message · Team only (internal
  note) · Zoom call · Log a call, each offered only where its own rule allows (`email.queue`; `client_message.write`;
  `internal_note.write`; Zoom also `mayArrangeCalls`); the boxes for the choice show by CSS `:has` (no script). Each does
  what that way always did, with every rule it had: email → `F.saveEmail` (To filled in with the company's main address,
  changeable; a reply carries the conversation's `thread`) and sent now if sending is on (`sendSavedEmailNow`, shared with
  the email page); WhatsApp → `W.sendWhatsApp` (the person and number chosen; the 24-hour rule; the opening message);
  portal → `M.postClientMessage`; team → `addNote`; **Zoom** → start now (a meeting at once, `entry_point = 'staff'`
  (migration 069), the host's page opens) or book a free hour from **your own** diary (`staffSlots` for the next fortnight,
  `isBookable` asked again), and the invite with the join link goes to the person chosen (`sendVideoInvite`, now with a
  recipient); **Log a call** → `call_logs` (migration 069: who, Spoke to them · No answer · Left a voicemail, notes, never
  edited or deleted), with an optional next step made a task (`createTask`, activity CALL). A refusal draws the form again
  with what was typed and why (400). **Right, the whole conversation** (`feed`): portal messages, emails both ways (never a
  sign-in code or one-use link, nor the portal's "you have a new message" alerts), WhatsApp both ways, team notes, logged
  calls, telephone and Zoom calls booked, and a Zoom call **started** when its host first opened it
  (`video_meetings.host_opened_at`, `host_opened_by`, migration 069; audited `VIDEO_CALL_OPENED`). Newest at the bottom
  (drawn newest first in a column-reverse list, so it opens there). Day by day; a red line "New since you last looked";
  each box marked by kind. **Each box is a link that makes the left a reply** — the way it came, the address, subject or
  number filled in (`?via=`, `&thread=`, `&contact=`); a Zoom call open to join links to the call. **Filters** by kind
  (`?show=`) and **search** (`?q=`, every box's words, names and subjects; the words found marked). Also kept from Chat:
  Check for new email replies with the last check's words, WhatsApp from an unknown number, and the files that came by
  email. Opening Communication (`?chat=`) reads it all: the red dot goes. The button and the tab read **Communication**. A
  claim is named in a box without its reference (a box is a link). The view-only login reads no client's messages, as
  before. Audited `BOOKING_CREATED`, `CALL_LOGGED`. **Since 1 Oct 2026** the Zoom choice says why Zoom is off, above its
  buttons (`zoomBlockers`: the keys, the transfer, or *"This copy is not the test website, so it does not call Zoom …"*),
  and a call booked with no meeting comes back `done=booked-no-zoom`, *"Booked, but Zoom made no meeting, so the invite
  has no join link."* (it said the join link was on its way). **Accept** `test/communication.test.ts` (eight tests);
  `test/phase142.test.ts`, `whatsapp`, `conversations` and `client-redesign` restated.
- **On the client page** (`src/web/client-page.ts`, `chatBlock` in `src/web/messages-pages.ts`): a **Chat** section
  (key `client.chat`, anchor `#chat`; the old `#company-notes` anchor is kept inside it) after the questions and
  before **Activity**. Two tabs that are plain links, `?chat=team` and `?chat=client`, each with its unread number.
  With none asked, the page opens on **With the client** when the client has written something this person has not
  read, and on **Team only** otherwise. **Team only** carries the line *"Only your team sees this. The client never
  does."* in the warning colour, the conversation, and **Write to your team** with **About which claim?** — posted to
  the note route, `POST /staff/clients/{id}/notes`, unchanged, which now returns to `?chat=team`. **With the client**
  carries the conversation (our side on the right), each file with its link, and **Write to {company}** with **About
  which claim?** — `POST /staff/clients/{id}/messages` (`postClientMessage`). A do-not-contact company shows *"You
  cannot write to this company."* and no form; the client can still write to us. The quick action "Add a note" is now
  **Message your team**. The notes panel left the Activity section, which is now called **Activity**.
- **Read and unread** (`thread_reads`, a row each time a person opens a tab with something unread — never because the
  page was drawn with the section folded). Staff count what the client sent; the client counts what we sent; the team
  tab counts what colleagues wrote. Writing is reading.
- **PHASE 156c (5 October 2026) — WHATSAPP BY MANUAL UPLOAD; the live connection to Meta switched off on the page.**
  Lakhbir: "go with manual upload, keep photos". Staff talk to clients on the WhatsApp phone; to keep a conversation they
  export that one chat (Export chat > Attach media) and add it on the client's WhatsApp page. **Reader**
  `src/connectors/whatsapp-export.ts` (`parseChat`, no network): the iPhone layout `[dd/mm/yyyy, hh:mm:ss] Name: text`
  (CRLF between messages, LF inside one, U+200E marks, `<attached: FILE>`, "name • 1 page" captions, "You deleted this
  message." / "This message was deleted.", `<This message was edited>`) and the Android one `dd/mm/yyyy, hh:mm - Name:
  text` (`FILE (file attached)`, `<Media omitted>`); WhatsApp's own notices skipped (kept narrow: a view-once notice is
  shown, not skipped); day first, with a warning when no day is after the 12th; UK local time to an exact moment across
  the clock change. **Steps** (`src/web/whatsapp-import.ts`, `src/web/whatsapp-import-pages.ts`, migration 090
  `whatsapp_imports` and `whatsapp_import_lines`, all `client_message.write`): `POST /staff/clients/{id}/whatsapp/import`
  (the export whole, up to 100 MB, kept sealed under `whatsapp-imports/`; or, for a .zip over 3.5 MB, only its text and
  file names, sent by the page's script `src/web/whatsapp-import-script.ts`, `/assets/whatsapp-import.js`, which reads
  the .zip on the person's computer — the test website takes about 4.5 MB a request); a group chat (more than two names),
  a name that climbs out, a .zip inside the .zip or a file that is not a chat is refused; `GET …/import/{iid}` the check
  (counts, first and last date, which name is us, **keep from a date** — an export holds the whole history); `POST
  …/import/{iid}` save (or cancel): each message into `whatsapp_messages` (IN `RECEIVED`, OUT `SENT` written by the
  uploader, the message's own time), tied to its upload by a fingerprint so a newer copy adds only what is new; files of
  the kinds kept (photo, video, voice note, PDF, Office) from the kept .zip at once, else one at a time to `POST
  …/import/{iid}/file` (`GET …/waiting` lists them), each sealed as a fetched WhatsApp file is (`whatsapp_media`,
  `media_id` `export:<name>`). Imported messages never count as unread and never open the 24-hour window. While the live
  connection is off (`whatsappMissing` not empty) the WhatsApp page has no reply box and no opening message — "Reply to
  … on the WhatsApp phone" — and carries its own policy (`script-src 'self'; connect-src 'self'`); the sending code is
  unchanged and returns when the keys are set. Answers recorded 5 Oct 2026 (Lakhbir, for Sadique to confirm): the
  copies follow the client-message retention rule; messages before the client ticked the WhatsApp box are kept.
  **The company's conversation too** (Lakhbir, 5 Oct 2026: "doesnt it make sense to have the upload option on the
  companies conversation"): `src/web/communication.ts` shows "Add a WhatsApp chat" under "Send a message", a button
  that opens the upload on the WhatsApp page with the person last on WhatsApp chosen (the client page stays script-free,
  as test/phase133a requires of every page reached from home; the upload needs the script for a big export); while
  WhatsApp is not live it is not offered in "Send by" (a post of it is refused, "Choose how to send it."); imported lines
  are marked "From the phone", never "New", our side "<name> (on the phone)", and a WhatsApp line opens the WhatsApp page. `test/phase156c.test.ts` (27 tests, the page's script run in a stand-in browser);
  `test/whatsapp.test.ts`, `test/conversations.test.ts` and `test/communication.test.ts` restated (the last proves the
  live reply-by-WhatsApp path with invented keys, and the not-live conversation).
- **30 September 2026 — "Everything", and WhatsApp** (`src/web/whatsapp.ts`, `src/connectors/whatsapp.ts`, migration 063
  `whatsapp_messages`). Lakhbir: "each client when we view back end has full communication via email or whats app". A
  third Chat tab, **Everything: portal, email and WhatsApp** (`?chat=all`, `client_message.read`): portal messages both
  ways, the emails we sent the company's own addresses (never a sign-in code or link, never a colleague's alert) and
  their emailed replies, and WhatsApp both ways — oldest first, each marked with how it travelled. Under it: **Write an
  email**, **Write in their portal**, and WhatsApp to a contact with a mobile number — `POST /staff/clients/{id}/whatsapp`
  (since 1 October 2026 the tab offers three buttons — **Email**, **WhatsApp**, **In their portal** — and WhatsApp has its
  own page, **`GET /staff/clients/{id}/whatsapp`** (`src/web/whatsapp-page.ts`, `client_message.read`): the message on
  the left (To, the 24-hour rule in words, Send and the opening message), the whole conversation with one number on the
  right, oldest first, with a link to each of the company's people on WhatsApp (`?contact=`, an id, never a number). The
  POST now comes back to that page. **The red dot on Chat** (`newFromClient`, `src/web/messages.ts`): messages from the
  client — portal, emailed replies, WhatsApp — this person has not read, on the client page's Chat button and tab and
  beside the company in the Clients list (whose link then opens the Everything tab). Read means: the portal thread
  (`thread_reads`), or the email page or WhatsApp page (`channel_reads`, migration 065, never edited), or the Everything
  tab, which reads all three)
  (`client_message.write`; refused for a do-not-contact company). Within 24 hours of the person's last WhatsApp to us, the
  words typed; otherwise only our approved opening template (WhatsApp's own rule, error 131047). Every attempt is a row,
  SENT with Meta's id or NOT_SENT with why. **Meta calls `GET · POST /hooks/whatsapp`** (no login): GET answers Meta's check
  with our verify word; POST is believed only under `X-Hub-Signature-256` over the exact bytes, else 401, audited
  WHATSAPP_REFUSED, nothing written. A message is filed under a company only when exactly one company has a contact with
  that number; otherwise it waits at **`GET /staff/whatsapp`** and is filed by a person, once —
  `POST /staff/whatsapp/{id}/file`. **Phase 153f (4 Oct 2026): a photo, video, voice note, document or sticker** is
  kept as Meta's media id with the message (migration 085, `whatsapp_media`); Admin or Sales press "Get it" —
  `POST /staff/whatsapp/{id}/attachment` fetches it from Meta (`GET /v25.0/{media-id}`, then Meta's file host
  `lookaside.fbsbx.com`, service `WHATSAPP_MEDIA`, at most 16 MB, the SHA-256 checked) and keeps it sealed in the file
  store (`whatsapp-files/`); `GET /staff/whatsapp/{id}/attachment` gives it as a download (never inline, `nosniff`,
  sandboxed). Fetched once, never changed or deleted until a retention rule is set. The same message delivered twice is kept once; a status only moves forward. **Built;
  the transfer APPROVED for testing on 30 Sep 2026** (Sadique Maskeen, relayed by Lakhbir: "Sadique approves WhatsApp, the
  contact box is enough for testing" — the funnel's "you may contact me" box stands as the permission while testing);
  nothing is sent until the six `WHATSAPP_*` keys are in the key file (on the test website, its settings).
- **In the portal** (`GET /portal/messages`, `portalMessagesBody`): a **Messages** tab beside **Your claims**, with the
  number of our messages the client has not read; *"Send us a message"* at the top of the portal and in **How to
  contact us**. The page: the conversation, and **Write to us** — the message, **Which claim is it about?** ("All of
  them, or not sure"), and **Send a file too (you do not have to)**: the same drop box as the portal's upload, with
  **What is the file?** (the receivable types). `POST /portal/messages` (`readPortalSend`, `sendFromPortal`): every
  refusal is asked **before** the file is received, so a refused message leaves nothing behind and is drawn again with
  what was typed (400); an empty file box is a message with no file; a file needs **What is the file?**; the file goes
  through `receiveFile` — `startDocumentUpload`, `acceptClientBytes`, `finishUpload`, the receipt contract — tagged to
  the claim picked, and is linked to the message (`client_message_files`) in the same transaction as the message.
  `GET /portal/messages/{id}` shows one message, looked for **only** in `client_messages` and only on the session's own
  company: a team message's id, another company's message and an id that does not exist all answer **404**.
- **Files.** A file that came with a message from the client is in **From the client** whoever saved it (a reply's file
  is saved by staff), and its card has **See the message it came with** (`FolderFile.message_href`).
- **Home.** A company whose client has written and not been answered for **more than one working day** (the same rule
  and bank holidays as a new lead, `src/web/working-days.ts`) is a red tile on **Today and overdue** — *"A message
  waiting for a reply · Waiting 2 working days"* — opening the client's tab (`clientMessagesWaiting`). Only for a login
  that may read the thread. Email alerts are built behind `EMAIL_SENDING` (Phase 142a, `B49a`), so none is sent today.
- **Timeline.** Every message on the thread with the client is a `MESSAGE` event — *"The client sent a message in their
  portal — with 1 file"*, *"We sent the client a message in their portal"*, *"A reply from {contact}, by email"* —
  tagged to its claim, **never with its words**. Team messages stay apart, as notes always were.
- **Audit.** `CLIENT_MESSAGE_WRITTEN` (the message, the company, the claim, the direction and a count of files — never
  the words); a team message keeps `INTERNAL_NOTE_ADDED`. `INBOUND_EMAIL_RECEIVED`, `INBOUND_EMAIL_FILED`,
  `INBOUND_EMAIL_FILE_SAVED` — ids, the outcome, counts; never an address, a subject or the words.
- **1 October 2026 — a reply from the address we wrote to is filed.** Now that To can be typed, a reply answering our
  Message-ID from **the very address that email went to** is filed on the company's conversation even when that address
  is not one of its contacts (`ingest`, `toWhomWeWrote`); from any other non-contact address it still waits under Email
  replies to sort. Found when Lakhbir's own reply, to a test email sent to his typed address, reached the inbox but not
  the site. **Accept** `test/conversations.test.ts`.
- **1 October 2026 — a reply to one of our emails is never skipped, and the inbox is read wherever staff look.** Lakhbir:
  "the email sent to icloud, i cloud responded but it didnt push through to back end". Checked read-only: both replies
  were in the inbox answering Message-IDs this site generated. Found: the inbox was read only from the Everything tab,
  Email replies to sort and the Check button — not the new Email page — and the first read, which notes where the inbox
  stands and reads nothing before it, could happen after such a reply arrived and pass it over. Now: (1) the Email page,
  every visit to a client's page and Home read first (at most every two minutes; `readRepliesIfDue`); (2) every read also
  asks the inbox, read-only, for messages answering a Message-ID of our domain at or below the mark (`Mailbox.answering`,
  `UID SEARCH HEADER IN-REPLY-TO`; IONOS answers it) and takes in each one not already read that answers an email this
  site sent — nothing else from before the mark (`repliesToOurs`); a server that cannot search that header does not stop
  the read; (3) the Email page has **Check for new email replies**, and it and Email replies to sort say what the last
  check found ("Last checked …: No new email", or "Could not read the inbox: …") — a failed read is never silent
  (`lastReadWords`). **Accept** `test/reply-reading.test.ts` (now five tests).
- **1 October 2026 — a reply set aside before the rule existed is filed by it.** Lakhbir's iCloud replies (inbox 27–29,
  each from the address our email went to, each answering our Message-ID) had been read before the same-address rule
  went live and waited in Email replies to sort; a message is read once. Every read now also files each reply still
  waiting that answers one of our emails from the address it went to, or from a contact of that company (`fileByRule`),
  on that company's conversation with our email's claim. Migration 068 rebuilds `inbound_email_filings` (same rows and
  triggers) with `rule`: a filing records the person who filed it, or the rule (`ANSWERS_OUR_EMAIL`), never neither.
  Audited `INBOUND_EMAIL_FILED` with `by_rule`. And the Everything tab now shows an email staff wrote to an address they
  typed (it showed only those to the company's own addresses), so the history is whole. **Accept**
  `test/conversations.test.ts` (now eight tests).
- **1 October 2026 — "How email reading stands"** (`readingState`, on Email replies to sort, Admin and Sales): whether
  reading is on and the mark; the last twelve emails received — inbox number, when, from, subject, what became of each
  (filed on which company and how: when it came in, by the rule, or by whom; or waiting, and why) and whether it answers
  one of our emails (and from which company that was sent); the last eight emails sent — to, company, subject, sent or
  why not, and whether the Message-ID a reply is matched on was kept. Never an email's words. Built when the test
  website's own records were the one thing that could not be seen from outside: run locally against the real inbox, the
  three iCloud replies were all filed. **Accept** `test/reply-reading.test.ts` (now six tests).
- **1 October 2026 — THE ROOT CAUSE: the test website was not using its lasting database.** The panel said "Records:
  this copy's own memory … the lasting database holds a seed that never finished", and the deploy's build log said
  `build: the lasting database was emptied and built again, and its seed still did not finish (Error: Hrana(Api("status=404
  Not Found … stream not found …")))`. Every deploy emptied the half-filled Turso database and refilled it, and Turso
  dropped the connection part way (a stream it no longer held), so every copy ran on its own memory: an email sent on one
  copy was unknown to the copy that read its reply, sign-ins did not last, and the reading mark started again on each
  copy. Fixed in `src/db.ts` (`droppedConnection`): on a remote database, a call that meets a dropped connection opens it
  again and is made once more; a transaction that meets one is begun again from the start, once (the server undid it);
  a file or memory never retries. Proved on the libsql engine: the refill is 382 calls, 16 of them in 4 small
  transactions, with no pause over 14 ms. **Accept** `test/db-reconnect.test.ts` (four tests, three mutations red).
- **1 October 2026 — THE REPLY TIMER.** Lakhbir: "do i have to press check for new email every time? … cant this
  automatically be done?" — and chose a free outside timer (cron-job.org) over Vercel Pro's scheduled jobs (the Hobby plan
  allows one a day). **`POST` or `GET /hooks/read-replies`**, no login, with the header `Authorization: Bearer
  <REPLY_TIMER_TOKEN>`: the word is a new key (`REPLY_TIMER_TOKEN`, owner `REPLY_TIMER`, 32 characters or more, read on the
  test website from its settings — `TEST_DEPLOY_KEYS`), compared in constant time (`timerMayRead`). Without it, or with a
  short one set: 403 "refused", nothing read. With it: `readRepliesIfDue` — at most one read every two minutes however
  often it is called — and an answer of counts only ("read N, filed N, to sort N"; "read in the last two minutes";
  "could not read the inbox"; "reading replies is off"), never an address or a word of any email. In a header, not the
  address, so the word is not written into web logs. The word is taken with or without "Bearer " in front, and a
  refusal says why — no word set on the website, no word sent, or a word that does not match — never the word
  (`timerRefusal`). **Accept** `test/reply-reading.test.ts` (now seven tests);
  `test/phase151e.test.ts` (the test website's key list).
- **Replies to contact@caprebate.co.uk — `INBOUND_EMAIL`, ON FOR TESTING since 30 Sep 2026 (see §1: from switch-on onward only, read when staff look); built and shipped off before** (`src/stubs/email-inbound.ts`,
  `src/web/inbound-email.ts`). A dependency-free IMAP reader over `node:tls`: `LOGIN` (quoted, no line break), **`EXAMINE
  INBOX`** (read-only), `UID SEARCH UID {n+1}:*`, `UID FETCH {uid} (UID BODY.PEEK[])` — it never selects, stores, copies,
  moves or deletes, and does not even mark a message read. It keeps the last number it read (`inbound_email.mailbox_uid`,
  unique, so a message is read in once). **Kept:** the sender's address, the subject, **the text part only** (no HTML),
  and each file's type and size — **never its name**. **Filed** onto the company's thread (as `FROM_CLIENT`, source
  `EMAIL`, tagged to our email's claim) only when In-Reply-To or References names an `outbox.provider_ref` of an email
  we sent **and** the sender is one of that company's contacts. **Anything else is queued** (`NO_MATCH`,
  `NOT_A_CONTACT`) at **`GET /staff/replies` — "Email replies to sort"** (a red Home tile while any wait), where Admin or
  Sales read it and file it under a company by hand (`POST /staff/replies/{id}/file`, `inbound_email_filings`, once
  only). **Never by the sender's address alone.** A do-not-contact company's reply is recorded (`DO_NOT_CONTACT`) and
  **nothing is ever sent back** by this code. **A file** stays in the mailbox until staff say what it is on the chat
  (`POST /staff/replies/files/{id}`): that one message is read again (read-only), the file goes through `receiveFile`
  as that member of staff, is linked to its message, and is shown to the client (they sent it) by a
  `document_visibility` row. `node src/cli.ts read-mailbox` reads what is new.
- **Personal data.** Messages are new personal data. The thread is added to the record of processing
  (`08-monday-doc-exports/12552682424__ICO-registration-content.md`, 3.2 and 6). **Their retention period is not set**:
  it is put to the data protection officer, *"How long do we keep client messages and the team chat?"*. Until it is
  answered nothing is deleted. Phase 142a built the clean-up job that applies the answer, switched off, and made it
  the only code that can delete a message — see `B49a`.
- **Must never** Let the portal read `internal_notes`; answer a client's request for any id not on its own thread with
  anything but 404; show Team only to the surveyor, or With the client to a surveyor without a job on the company; show either thread to Accounting or the view-only login; put a message's words in the
  audit log, the timeline or an address; take an attached file any way but `receiveFile`; file an emailed reply by its
  sender's address alone; send anything back to a do-not-contact company; change the mailbox.
- **Accept** `test/phase142.test.ts` (20 tests: the rule table, the team thread as the notes, never edited or deleted,
  the portal's Messages with and without a file, the 404s, **every portal response searched on the wire for a team
  message's words** with the control that the words are on the staff page, the surveyor, the timeline, Home's working-day
  rule, the IMAP reader against a fake server, filing, the queue, saving a reply's file, do-not-contact);
  `tools/payload-proof.ts` runs the same wire search as CLIENT and SURVEYING, with Admin's page as its control. A
  planted leak (the portal's message lookup falling back to `internal_notes`) turns the 404 test and the wire test red.

### `B49a` — the chat follow-on: a message clean-up job, new-message alerts, and one name for the notes — Phase 142a

Four things Phase 142 could build and did not. Two are switched off, because each waits on somebody else's decision.

- **The message clean-up job — `MESSAGE_RETENTION`, built and SHIPPED OFF** (`src/web/message-retention.ts`,
  migration 038). `messagesDue` reads what is past its period and writes nothing; `sweepMessages` deletes it; `node
  src/cli.ts sweep-messages` is the one line a schedule runs. **Off — the shipped setting, or any of its four
  conditions missing — it returns `OFF` and touches nothing, not even the audit log.** On:
  - *A company with a claim* follows the claim file. Each claim's clock starts on the **later** of its accounting period
    end and the client report's first issue — RET-7's trigger, read by `claimFileDays` in `src/web/documents.ts`, the
    same reader the document clock uses. The company's messages on **both** threads go `with_claim_years` after the
    **latest** claim's start. **If any claim's date is not known, nothing on that company is due** (N4: an `UNKNOWN`
    period end and no answer read the same). A message written after that day keeps `without_claim_months` from its
    own day, and is kept for good while that rule is off.
  - *A company with no claim*: everything on it goes `without_claim_months` after its **last** message, on either thread.
  - *An emailed reply nobody filed* (the `/staff/replies` queue) goes `without_claim_months` after it arrived.
  - *A live legal hold* (`legal_holds`) on the company (record kind `COMPANY`) keeps every message on it; one on a
    single message (`CLIENT_MESSAGE`, `TEAM_MESSAGE`, `INBOUND_EMAIL`) keeps that one.
  - "Past its period" is the day **after** the period ends, by the UK calendar day of the run.
- **The only path that can delete a message.** Migration 038 replaces 037's seven "never deleted" triggers — on
  `client_messages`, `client_message_files`, `internal_notes`, `inbound_email`, `inbound_email_files`,
  `inbound_email_file_saves` and `inbound_email_filings` — with ones that admit a delete **only** where
  `deletion_log` (migration 022) already holds an entry for that very record under the rule `MSG-RETENTION`. The
  job writes the entry first, then deletes, in one transaction: one entry per record, its kind and id, the day its
  period ended, who approved the period and where, the store (`database`), and — while the host's backup cycle is
  not set — backups pending with no expiry. **Never a word of the message, never a person.** A delete from anywhere
  else is refused exactly as before; the "never edited" triggers are unchanged. An emailed reply holds the same words
  as the message it became, so it goes with it. **A file sent with a message is not deleted**: it is a document, keeps
  its own document rules, and stays in Files — only the row saying which message brought it goes.
- **Audit.** One `MESSAGES_DELETED` entry per run that is on: every id removed, per kind, and the counts. Never the words.
- **New-message alerts — built behind `EMAIL_SENDING`, so SHIPPED OFF** (`alertNewMessage` in
  `src/web/messages.ts`). Called after a message is written — by staff, by the client in the portal, or by the
  mailbox reader filing a reply. **Off, it returns `OFF` and writes nothing at all**, so nothing piles up "saved, not
  sent". On, every alert is saved with `send_requested = 1` as a `NOTIFICATION`, and `send-outbox` sends it. A
  client's message tells **the company's owner** where the owner is an active Admin or Sales login, and **every active
  Sales login** otherwise; our message tells **each active portal login** of the company (a contact with no login
  could not read it). The email says only that a message is waiting, with a link (`CAPREBATE_PUBLIC_ORIGIN`; with
  none set, no link) — **never the words, never who wrote them**. Subjects: *"A client has sent a new message"* and
  *"You have a new message from Cap Rebate"*; the company leads an email to our staff since Phase 151 (`NOTICE_SUBJECT_LEADS_WITH_COMPANY`, on), and never an alert to a client.
  A do-not-contact company is refused by the outbox, as everywhere: the client's own message is still taken. Audited
  `CLIENT_MESSAGE_ALERT` — the message, who was told by user id, the outbox rows; never an address.
  The chat's help line under **Send to the client** says which is true: *"We do not email them about it yet."* while
  sending is off.
- **One name for the notes.** The claim page and the property page title their notes panel **Team only** (it was
  *"Internal notes — never shown to the client"*), with the line *"Only your team sees this. The client never does."*
  and a link, **See the whole team chat about this client**, to `/staff/clients/{id}?chat=team#chat`
  (`notesBlock` in `src/web/tasks-pages.ts`). Each page still shows its own notes — a claim's, or a property's.
- **The privacy notice's line about messages** is drafted for the data protection officer in the notice itself —
  Sections 3 and 7 of `08-monday-doc-exports/12818279787__Section-1-Who-we-are-and-how-to-contact-us.md`, marked DRAFT,
  with a note for the officer at its end. The notice is still DRAFT — NOT APPROVED, NOT PUBLISHED.
- **Retention list.** `/staff/compliance`'s retention list now carries both threads, **not covered** until the
  officer answers, and says the table refuses a delete except from the clean-up job, which is off.
- **Must never** Delete a message any other way; delete a message's file; put a message's words, a sender or an
  address in an alert, the deletion log or the audit log; queue an alert while sending is off; treat a claim date not
  known as a date.
- **Accept** `test/phase142a.test.ts` (19 tests since Phase 142b — 16 as Phase 142a built it: off in each of five ways deletes nothing and writes nothing; the
  calendar; no claim — not a day early; with a claim — not known is never due, the latest claim governs, a later
  message keeps its own period; a legal hold; a message's file stays; an emailed reply goes with its message and an
  unfiled one after its period; the database refuses a delete not logged by the job, or logged under another rule, or
  for another record; a grep proves nothing else in `src/` deletes; alerts off, on, to the owner, and refused for
  do-not-contact; "Team only" on both pages). With migration 038 removed, three of them go red.

- **Phase 142b — Lakhbir's three answers, 22 September 2026.** The clean-up job is **on**: seven years from the claim
  file's clock, 84 months from the last message on a company with no claim, `approved_by` Lakhbir, the officer's
  approval still asked (SDP-P10's register entry). The providers are set, IONOS (`'SMTP'`, `'IMAP'`); sending and
  reading stay off until the mailbox exists. The surveyor reads **With the client** on an assigned job, read only.
  `test/phase142a.test.ts` is now 19 tests; with the surveyor's rule removed, two go red.

### `B50` — grants and contributions, a screen that is easy to use — Phase 143

Lakhbir, 18 September 2026: *"Grants and contributions — this section is a mess, and although great and it works, the
UI is terrible."* **The screen changed and nothing under it did**: the same table `claim_grants`, the same fact
`GRANTS_EXIST`, the same stores `recordGrant` and `withdrawGrant`, and the same valuation check `grantsTraced`, which
the gate out of VALUATION reads (`src/web/claim-facts.ts`). No migration.

- **Where** `#claim-grants` on the claim page, key `claim.grants` in `SECTION_KEYS` (`src/web/sections.ts`), built in
  `claimPage` (`src/web/crm-pages.ts`) from the facts panel — so Admin and Sales alone (`claim_facts.read`); a
  surveyor's released list and Accounting's page never build it. Body: `grantsBlock` in `src/web/claim-record-pages.ts`.
  Status: **Done** only when `grantsTraced` is met; **Waiting for the client** when the answer is not known and the
  question is with the client; otherwise **Working on it**. WE NEED is *"Whether anyone else paid for any of this
  work"* with **Ask the client** (fact id `claim_fact.GRANTS_EXIST`), and it is no longer listed under The claim's facts.
- **The question** *"Did anyone else pay for any of this work?"* — **Who told us?** (required) and three buttons,
  posting to `POST /staff/claims/{id}/grants/answer`. Yes and No record `GRANTS_EXIST` = `YES`/`NO` through
  `recordFact`; **Not sure records `UNKNOWN`** (N4: it reads exactly as never asked, and never passes) and, where the
  login may ask (`internal_note.write`) and no question on that fact is open, puts one question to the client through
  `sendTaskToPortal`. Once answered, the buttons fold under **Change the answer**.
- **The cards and totals** One card per grant not removed: who paid (a class, never a name), how much, one sentence,
  where the figure came from and who told us, **Matched to the items it paid for** (`traced` = `YES`) or **Not matched
  yet** (`NO` or `UNKNOWN`), and the effect line. Totals over grants not removed: **Grants in total**, **Matched**, **Not
  matched yet**; **an amount not known is never summed as nothing** — it is counted apart, "and N with the amount not
  known". Removed grants sit under **Removed (N)**, with the reason. `GrantLine` gained the stored keys
  `payer_key`, `amount_pence`, `traced_key` and `note`, read only.
- **Add a grant** `POST /staff/claims/{id}/grants/new` — four steps, one question each (who paid and grant or other
  payment; how much and how we know; what it paid for; matched), then **Check it, then save**, which posts every field
  to the unchanged `POST /grants`. Every step is a POST and carries what was typed in hidden fields, never in the
  address. `grantFormClaim` authorises `claim_facts.record` before a step is drawn; each step is checked, and a
  hand-made post cannot reach a later step with an earlier one unanswered. Matched with nothing written against it is
  sent back to **What did it pay for?** (and `recordGrant` refuses it as before).
- **The words follow Rulebook F** (`grantEffect`): a public body — `PUBLIC_PAYERS`, F-R8 — *"This will reduce the claim
  on the items it paid for."*; an insurer (F-R7) *"usually does not reduce the claim"*; anyone else (F-R9, F-R10)
  *"may reduce the claim … Our tax adviser checks whether it does."*; not known, *"We need to know who paid."* The prompt
  asked for the chip "Matched to parts of the building"; the screen says **"Matched to the items it paid for"**, because
  Rulebook F traces a grant to the items of spend it met (s.532 "to the extent that", F-R1, F-R12), not to parts of the
  structure — the rulebook's meaning wins.
- **Remove** A **Remove** button opens a pop-up (the HTML `popover` attribute, so no script and nothing in the CSP
  changes) asking *"Why are you removing it?"*; the post is the unchanged `/grants/{grant}/withdraw`, which refuses no
  reason, keeps the row, and audits `CLAIM_GRANT_WITHDRAWN`.
- **Phone width** the cards go one to a row, the totals wrap, the steps two to a row, the pop-up fits the screen.
- **Accept** `test/phase143.test.ts` (fifteen tests, twelve of which fail against the `.bak-phase143` copies), and
  `test/phase138.test.ts`'s fold now reads "Record a fact or an owner".

### `B51` — plain words a 12-year-old can read, on every screen — Phase 144

Lakhbir, 18 September 2026: *"Some of the wording in the back end sounds more like front-end design wording, and this
should not be the case. It needs to be readable at a 12-year-old level and to the point."* **Words changed; no rule,
route, role, store or audit event did.** No migration.

- **Where** every person-facing string in `src/web/*.ts` (staff and portal), the email service's words in
  `src/stubs/email.ts`, `email-provider.ts` and `email-inbound.ts`, and the demo data in `src/seed.ts`. The
  `.bak-phase144` siblings hold the words before.
- **The list** `PLAIN_SWAPS` in `src/web/voice.ts`, beside the brand avoid-list: each row is a pattern, the word and the
  plain word to use — fired → looked up; tier → free or paid; provenance → where this came from; derived → worked out;
  gate → check; receipt strip → "we read it, then deleted the file"; hash / chain → audit log; outbox → emails waiting
  to send; deletion clock → "deleted on (date)"; UNSET → "No deletion date set yet"; stub → not switched on yet;
  sealed → locked; N4, payload, YYYY-MM-DD, rule codes and the rest. Code, comments, SQL, ids and audit event names keep
  their words; a screen never shows them.
- **The check** `node 03-app/caprebate-plain-words-check.cjs` reads each file through the TypeScript parser and takes
  what a person reads — text between tags, `title`/`placeholder`/`aria-label`/`alt`, and any string that reads as a
