  sentence or label (a `'…' + '…'` join is read as one string). Exit 1 on a listed word, or on a sentence of eight words
  or more above Flesch-Kincaid grade 9 (familiar words — capital allowances, surveyor, the company — count at two
  syllables at most; an email or web address as one). `--self-test` plants twelve faults and six clean controls;
  `--suffix .bak-phase144` reads the pre-change copies and must go red; `--list` prints every string; `--file` one file.
- **Exempt, by name inside the check** the engagement letter (`letter.ts`); the business flow's own step names
  (`stages.ts`); until Phase 144a, also the Help pages and the public assessment (see below); the data protection officer's receipt recommendations
  (`receipt-recommendations.ts`, identical to migration 021's rows); the three ways to complain sent in rights letters
  (`ONWARD_ROUTE_TEXT`, verbatim from the notices page); the versioned consent text `SHARE-PROGRESS-1`; the
  pre-diligence ceiling caveat; one question title quoted from CAPREBATE QUESTIONS; one stored value (`'cash gate'`).
- **The carried screens** Reports (`dashboard.ts`): unknown estimates are said — *"3 open claims have no estimate yet —
  not known, so not counted as £0"*. A document's page (`documentPageHtml`): **What we did with the file**, **When files
  of this type are deleted**, **When this version is deleted**; a clock nobody set reads **No deletion date set yet** —
  a state, never a date; RET-7's reading says *"Worked out by us — not yet approved by the data protection officer"*.
  The Search list's states (`CENTRE_STATES`) and the types page likewise. **The portal document page** (`documentPage`
  in `router.ts`) no longer draws the twelve-step table: it shows `readLines` from `documents.ts` (now exported) —
  counts only, *"We read 3 pages."*, never the file's words, a note or a figure; the portal's document list says
  **Read**, **Not read yet** or **We could not use it**. Your account's list (`staff.ts`) is **Emails waiting to send**,
  **Saved — not sent yet**, a **What it is** column in words, and a link to the company's Email page where the row has a
  company (an id, nothing personal). The claim-email refusal says **Not sent**. The property page's `<title>` names the
  company once, through `propertyIn` (`components.ts`). The retention list (`retentionList` in
  `compliance-identity.ts`) shows each parameter's new `says` line — **No rule for this yet**, **Worked out by us — not
  yet approved…**, **No deletion date set yet**; the stored `trigger` text is unchanged. The valuation check's grant
  sentences (`grantsTraced` in `claim-facts.ts`) and the labels of `GRANTS_EXIST` and the grants row of
  `RELEASED_FACT_LABELS` use the grants screen's words — matched to the items it paid for, remove, not sure — and not
  sure never passes. The audit page: **Audit log checked — nothing changed** or **Audit log problem — … Tell the Admin
  now.** (`tools/payload-proof.ts` reads the new words; the chain check is the same.)
- **The client's step names are on** `NAME_SWITCHES.portalSteps` is `true` (Lakhbir, 22 September 2026, in CAPREBATE
  QUESTIONS): the portal reads *1 Your estimate … 6 Aftercare*. `test/phase133c.test.ts` passes both positions.
- **Must never** Change a rule's meaning to make it plain; show an unknown as a date, £0, a pass or a limit; show the
  client more than before.
- **Accept** `test/phase144.test.ts` (nine tests: the check green, its self-test, the list, the switch and the carried
  screens) and `test/phase144-crm.test.ts` (the tab title) — both red against the pre-change tree; the payload proof.

**Phase 144a, 22 September 2026 — beyond the back office's screens.** Words changed, and one migration; no rule,
route, role or audit event did.
- **The guide and Help** `09-app/CAPREBATE-ADMIN-AND-USER-GUIDE.md` rewritten at grade 7 or below, every `### Training —`
  heading and id kept; Help rebuilt from it (`tools/build-help.cjs`). The check now reads the guide as markdown
  (`--guide` alone; a code span is one word, a fenced block is not read, a table cell is one piece, a line that is only
  a bold label is its own piece) and reads `help-content.ts` the same way, both at **grade 7**. The table of old words
  in the guide's `B51` sits between `<!-- plain-words: quotes-old-words -->` and `<!-- /plain-words -->`: the grade is
  checked there, the banned list is not. `--self-test` now plants eighteen faults and keeps ten clean controls;
  `--suffix .bak-phase144a` must go red.
- **No longer exempt** `help-content.ts` and `assessment.ts`. Three assessment help lines — Q1's, Q1a's and the
  cosmetic-work note — were split into shorter sentences **in the site file's renderer and in `assessment.ts` at
  once**, and `test/phase144a.test.ts` now fails if the two ever differ. Q1's question is unchanged and exempt by its
  exact words: its plainer version (*"Have you already claimed capital allowances on this building's fixtures, such as
  its wiring or heating?"*) waits in FOR THE ACCOUNTANT, because a question's words can change the answer.
- **Stored words** migration 039 changes five `reason_lists` labels and two automation task texts, each only while it
  still holds the seeded words. The next-step template already signed off **Cap Rebate** (migration 030).
- **Six small things** Admin settings shows `settingLabel(key)` (`configuration.ts`), not the key — the key stays the
  form value; the identity store is **People named on documents** (menu, page, search button, retention rows, the
  search page's note); the fee box (`feeSwitchesBox`, `finance-pages.ts`) chooses its words from the two switches and
  `amount_untouched`; the reward page counts **three** models; the portal's unplaced step reads
  `stageInWords` (`portal.ts`), never the code; `P.documentTrace`, which no route called, is removed.
- **The property page** names the company once in the breadcrumb and the highlights, through `propertyIn`.
- **Accept** `test/phase144a.test.ts`; the site harness in both modes; the brand and extraction checks.

### `B33`–`B39` — the compliance registers — CRM 8 of 10
- **Route** everything under `/staff/compliance`: the landing page and its `view.json`; `/aml` and `/aml/checks/{id}`; `/rights` and `/rights/{id}` (with `view.json`); `/complaints` and `/complaints/{id}`; `/breaches`, `/breaches/{id}` and `/breaches/{id}/ico-sheet`; `/identity` and `/identity/{person_ref}`; `/consent/{contact}`; `/retention` and its `view.json`.
- **Who** `compliance.read` and `compliance.write` — ADMIN today. S2 asks `compliance.identity_read` or `compliance.identity_write`, which nothing inherits. **Since Phase 129 a `DPO` role is defined to hold these four and `disclosure.read` and nothing else, and it is switched off** — `DPO_ROLE_SWITCH` in `src/authz.ts`; no user can hold it.
- **Every register page carries the one-person notice** — the landing page, each register, S2, a contact's consent history and the retention list; the CRM follow-on added it to the last three.
- **Fields** the settings, dated with their source; each register's fields as its document defines them — the AML/KYC Position Paper section 5, the rights-request pack's Tables A and B and its complaints log, the breach procedure's Tables A and B with the four Article 34 facts; the Article 12A working per right and the thirty days shown as arithmetic; holds with a reason and a review date; cross-references in both directions; the retention clock of every record kind, UNSET shown as unset. **Since Phase 132, RET-1 to RET-4 read as the Retention Determinations draft them** (sections 12.2–12.6): each is `DERIVED_NOT_APPROVED`, with its period, trigger and owner, and the open question named where the determinations leave one. **None is approved**, which is the data protection officer's act. The deletion log is a record kind on the list, with no screen of its own.
- **Must never** Show S2 as a list, or a name in a search result; store a deadline; record bank details; let an insurer be recorded as told on silence; let PSC data stand for beneficial ownership without this check's dated decision; offer a delete; say to a client or an accountant that a check exists.
- **Accept** `test/compliance.test.ts`, `test/compliance-clock.test.ts` and the payload proof.

### On `C6`, the portal — the firm that referred you — CRM 8 of 10
- **Route** `POST /portal/share-progress` · **Who** `share_consent.client_record`, the client on their own organisation. Shows, per firm that referred the client, whether progress is shared and the words; a button to stop while the answer is yes; a button to say yes only once its switch is on.

### `B40` — Reports, the management dashboard — CRM 9 of 10, on Home's Reports tile since Phase 136
- **Phase 136** the page is `GET /staff/reports`, titled **Reports**, reached from the **Reports** tile on Home; Home is the current tab on it. `GET /staff/dashboard` answers **303 to `/staff`**, or to `/staff/reports` with its query when it carried one (a bookmarked report). `view.json` answers exactly as before, and the payload proof reads it. Each tile's reading is behind a Why? toggle.
- **Route** `GET /staff/reports` · `GET /staff/dashboard/view.json`, each taking `period` — MONTH, QUARTER, FINANCIAL_YEAR with `fy`, or RANGE with `from` and `to` — since the CRM follow-on; my work on `GET /staff`. **Who** `dashboard.enter` — the four staff roles — and then each tile's own actions.
- **The period — this is the reports screen** (the CRM follow-on). There is no second reports screen. The period comes from a fixed table, anything else is dropped and said, and no period is exactly the dashboard as it was. A tile that counts what happened in time — leads by their date, claims completed, days to completion, expected completion, exits and lost reasons, money by calendar month — reads the period and says so; a tile that counts what stands today says it is as at today; a lost lead, which carries no date it was lost, is counted apart.
- **Fields** per tile: its lines, what is counted apart, and one sentence saying how it is read. Pipeline: open value, average and won value, by stage, owner and introducer, leads and conversion by source, days to completion and the slowest stage, expected completion, exits and lost reasons. Money: estimated fees; invoiced, paid, outstanding, overdue. Reconciliation: the cases, the gaps, deliverables not issued. Work: overdue tasks, outstanding client questions and document requests. Compliance: the clocks running. A surveyor: my survey jobs. My work adds my claims with their next action and what each waits on, and every open question I asked.
- **Must never** Store a figure; show a surveyor anything estimate-derived; add an unknown in as nought; place an undated row inside a period.
- **Accept** `test/dashboard.test.ts`, `test/follow-on.test.ts` and the payload proof.

### `B41` — global search — CRM 9 of 10
- **Route** `GET /staff/search?q=` · `GET /staff/search/view.json?q=`. **Who** every staff role, each kind searched only where the rule table lets that role read it; the page says which kinds were not searched.
- **Must never** Search the identity store or its log, a compliance register, document text, a note body without `internal_note.read`, or the estimate; filter after the query instead of inside it; take a wildcard. An invoice is found by the start of its number — `CB-0` — since the CRM follow-on.
- **Accept** `test/search.test.ts` and the payload proof.
- **Phase 134** A claim is found by its reference **and by its company's name**, and is named "Company — Property", its reference in the detail. The hits are ordered **company-name matches first** — the company itself, then anything whose company's name matches, then the rest — which changes the order only. An invoice hit reads "Company — invoice CB-000001".

### `B42` — the claims and leads lists, saved views and export — CRM 9 of 10
- **Route** `GET /staff/claims` · `GET /staff/leads` with filter and sort parameters; `POST /staff/views` · `POST /staff/views/{id}/retire`; `GET /staff/claims/export.csv` · `GET /staff/leads/export.csv`. **Who** the claims list and its views, the four staff roles; **the leads list and its views, ADMIN and Sales** (`lead.read`); sharing a view, ADMIN; export, `list.export` — the claims for ADMIN, Sales and Accounting, the leads for ADMIN and Sales.
- **Fields** filters: owner, stage, source, introducer, sector, property type, jurisdiction, dates, status, and value where the role reads it; sorts: next action, age, value, due date, priority. A parameter the list does not take is named as ignored. A filter on a column the reader cannot read is not applied to the export, and since the CRM follow-on the page says so beside the link and the export's audit line names it.
- **Must never** Build an ORDER BY from a request value; export a column the page would not show the reader; leave a cell beginning `=` `+` `-` or `@` live.
- **Accept** `test/lists.test.ts` and the payload proof.

### `B43` — admin settings — CRM 9 of 10
- **Phase 135** Admin reaches it from the **Switches** tile on Settings (`B46`); it is on no menu.
- **Route** `GET /staff/admin-settings` · `GET /staff/admin-settings/view.json` · `POST /staff/admin-settings/values` · `/values/{list}/{code}` · `/reasons` · `/reasons/{list}/{code}` · `/setting`; and on a firm's page `POST /staff/introducers/{id}/merge`. **Who** `configuration.read` — ADMIN, Sales; `configuration.write` and `introducer.merge` — ADMIN.
- **Fields** the five lists and the two reason lists, each value with its code, label, state and where it came from; the document types, the automation rules and the templates, each linked to its own page; the finance settings and the compliance settings as dated rows with their source; what no setting may change, each with its reason.
- **Must never** Delete a value or change its code; retire a value the application writes; move a gate, a switch, the reward model or a role boundary — the request is refused by name and recorded; move a fee, a notice or a client's answer in a merge.
- **Layout — Phase 133b** a left-hand section list: one section per list (`#list-{code}`), per reason list (`#reasons-{code}`), per summary (`#summary-…`) and last `#may-not-change`. A save redirects to its list's section.
- **Accept** `test/configuration.test.ts` and the payload proof.

### `B44` — the document centre — the CRM follow-on
- **Route** `GET /staff/documents` · `GET /staff/documents/view.json`. **Phase 140:** `GET /staff/documents?organisation={id}` with no other filter redirects to `/staff/clients/{id}/files`, which asks its own rules again; the list is on no menu (`test/phase135.test.ts`) and is reached from Search ("Find a document across every client", for a login that may open it). **Who** `document_centre.enter` — the four staff roles — and then `document.read` on **each row**, so each role's list holds exactly the documents its own pages would open. CLIENT is refused: the portal is the client's list.
- **Fields** document · type · organisation · the claims it is linked to, or *on no claim* · state — received, not received yet, or refused · newest version, when and by whom (a role) · how many versions. Filters: type, organisation, claim, state; orders: newest version first, type then number, organisation then type. A parameter the list does not take is named as not applied.
- **Must never** List a document the reader cannot open; search what a document says; offer an export; build an ORDER BY from a request value.
- **Accept** `test/follow-on.test.ts` and the payload proof.

### `B45` — the audit log — the CRM follow-on
- **Route** `GET /staff/audit`, with `kind` and `outcome`. **Who** `audit.read` — ADMIN alone.
- **Fields** the chain's reading — intact, recomputed from the genesis, or broken at a named entry · for each of the newest two hundred lines that match: number · when · kind · role · who (an id) · what it was about (a type and an id) · outcome.
- **Must never** Read or draw a line's detail; offer an edit or a delete; leave its own read out of the log.
- **Accept** `test/follow-on.test.ts` and the payload proof; `npm run verify-audit` beside it, from the database file.


### `B46` — Settings, Admin's page of tiles — Phase 135
- **Route** `GET /staff/admin`. **Who** `user.list` — ADMIN (`adminSettingsGate` in `src/web/admin-tiles.ts`). The last tab of Admin's menu.
- **Fields** eight tiles in this order — Users (`/staff/users`), Switches (`/staff/admin-settings`), Document types, Automation, Email templates, Compliance, Audit log, Introducer fees (`/staff/introducer-rewards`) — each its name and one line on what it is for. A tile is drawn only where its screen's own gate admits the login.
- **Must never** Decide anything: a tile names the gate its screen's store calls first. Grant a screen: every screen behind a tile answers its own address, for every login its own gate admits, exactly as before.
- **Accept** `test/phase135.test.ts` — the eight tiles in order, each opening; Settings current on a tile's screen; the page refused to every other login.
---

# 3 · What every API does

**Two kinds, and they are not blurred.** *Internal* is every route the two
applications call. *External* is every register and supplier. **Nothing in the
external table fires today** — see §4.

## 3.1 · Internal — the routes

**How a refusal looks, everywhere.** `authorise` throws `NotAuthorised`, which is
not caught by any handler and reaches the server's own error handler: **HTTP 403,
body `{"error":"not authorised"}`**, with no detail about what exists. A missing
row on a collection a role may not use throws `NotFoundAsRefusal` (`E_NOT_AUTHORISED`)
and produces **the same 403** — *not found* and *not yours* are deliberately
indistinguishable, so an id cannot be probed. An unauthenticated request to
`/staff` or `/portal` **redirects to `/login`**; to `/api/*` it is **401** with
the generic failure. A body over the cap is **413**. **Signed out, an address that does
not exist also answers 401 with the generic failure; signed in, it is 404** — so a public route
that is switched off, such as `/refer`, reads exactly as a missing page either way.

### Public — no session

| Method | Path | Takes | Returns | Refuses |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GET | `/` | — | **the home page** — `Caprebate/09-app/site/home.html` (`HOME_PATH` in `src/web/home-site.ts`), when it is beside the build: its **Am I eligible?** buttons go to `/assessment`, its contact form to `/enquiry` with the same fields, its **Log in** to `/login` (29 Sep 2026). Without it, **the website** — `Caprebate site.html`, read from its fixed path (`SITE_PATH` in `src/web/public-site.ts`) and served without its in-page assessment, with its style and script as the two assets below, under the back end's policy plus `script-src 'self'`; its assessment links go to `/assessment`, its form to `/enquiry`, its **Log in** button to `/login` (Phase 133j). With no site file beside the build, the back end's own front page | the edition is refused if anything inline is left |
| GET | `/assets/site-page.css` · `/assets/site-page.js` | — | the website's one `<style>` block and one `<script>`, cut from the same file; never a path mapped onto the disk (Phase 133j) | — |
| GET | `/assets/home.css` · `/assets/home.js` | — | the new home page's one `<style>` block and its `<script>` blocks in order, cut from `Caprebate/09-app/site/home.html` (src/web/home-site.ts). When that file is there, `/` is the home page under its own policy: the back end's, plus scripts and frames from `https://challenges.cloudflare.com` (the contact form's security check) and nothing else (29 Sep 2026) | — |
| GET | `/assets/gsap.min.js` | — | the animation library the home page draws with, from the `gsap` package in the root `package.json`; not installed, the route is not there and the page shows its drawings still (29 Sep 2026) | — |
| POST | `/hooks/identity-check` | Didit's result (Phase 151b for Veriff; Didit since Phase 151f), signed in `X-Signature-V2` (the HMAC-SHA256 of the sorted, compact JSON) or `X-Signature` (of the exact bytes), and dated in `X-Timestamp`. Set this address as the webhook in Didit's console | `200 ok` and our own copy of the result in `identity_check_results` (migrations 047 and 053): the decision's fields and the body's SHA-256, never a name, a document number or an image; a PASS on an open due diligence check fills its "How it was verified". `200 ok` and nothing kept for a check still under way (Not Started, In Progress, Awaiting User) or a webhook about anything else (audited `IDENTITY_RESULT_NOT_KEPT`) | **401** when no signature holds or the result is more than five minutes old (audited `IDENTITY_RESULT_REFUSED`, nothing written); **404** for a session we never sent; 64 KB cap. No login: the service has none |

Without either, nothing is checked and the start-up log says so |
| GET | `/enquiry` | — | **303** to `/#contact`: the thank-you page reloaded or bookmarked goes back to the form (29 Sep 2026, the stress test) | — |

| GET | `/favicon.svg` · `/favicon.ico` | — | the house mark on ink, as the browser tab's icon, named by every page; `/favicon.ico` is **204** (29 Sep 2026) | — |

Stage 2 opens on what the buildings hold, in words; stage 3 on "your figure is ready" with the amount hidden — **the number is in no page before stage 4**. A screen past the first incomplete one sends the person back to it; a follow-up that does not apply is not kept; every building's owner paying no UK tax is the reasoned negative. The old six screens (`/assessment/Q1` … `Q3`) still work for a draft that uses them | **400** listing each building's missing answer in words |

Calls are **an hour**, `SLOT_MINUTES` = 60, on the hour) for a four-stage draft, only when stage 3 is done: **the climbing reveal** (a reel of digits per place, CSS only), each building's "up to" figure (a building whose owner pays no UK tax named and not counted), the engine's assumptions with each building's condition under its name, then **how to reach you**: first name, surname, position, and telephone (a time and the number), video call (a time and an email address) or email | **303** back to the first incomplete screen |

| GET | `/assessment/figure` | draft cookie | the ceiling, its qualifier, the breakdown | no draft → redirect to `/assessment`; **a draft the gate stopped → redirect to `/assessment/no-claim`** |

The person is the contact on each; **one** booking, on the first; a video call holds its time like a telephone call, and the team sends the link. The thank-you says the figure as it was shown, "up to" the total, never a range the person did not see | **400** in words: a name, a position, a way to be reached, its number or address, or the permission |

Open: a lead of origin REFERRAL on a new firm row marked PUBLIC_FORM. CRM 5 of 10 | **400** with the form and a sentence: no client name, no email, no confirmation |

### Client portal — `CLIENT` on their own business, or staff

| Method | Path | Who | Returns / writes |
|---|---|---|---|
| GET | `/portal` | `business.read` + `ownBusiness`; each claim `claim.progress_read` | the company's name as the heading · **Your claims**, one tile per claim (Phase 134) · range · letter state · appointment and its move control · open questions · the documents we asked for · documents |
| GET | `/portal/claims/{claim}` | `business.read`, then `claim.progress_read` + `ownBusiness` on the claim | one claim's property and progress in the client's six steps (Phase 134). **Looked for only among the company's own claims**: another company's claim and a missing one both answer the same **404** |
| GET | `/portal/letter` | `engagement.read` | the letter parsed from the `.docx`, its open terms and its specimens marked. Records `VIEWED` |
| POST | `/portal/letter/sign` | `engagement.read` | `engagements.state = SIGNED`. **Stubbed provider; real state machine** |
| GET | `/portal/letter/signed.pdf` | `engagement.read` | **Phase 157c.** The client's signed copy, the PDF the signature record holds, checked against its record first; 404 before signing |
| GET · POST | `/sign/{token}` | none — the link is the key | **Phase 157d.** Signing from the emailed link: GET shows the letter and uses nothing; POST signs once, as `EMAIL_LINK`. Only where the signing setting says `links` (the test copy only, today); otherwise a page that says so |
| POST | `/portal/bookings/{id}` | `booking.move_own` + `ownBusiness` | move it to a chosen `starts_at`, `release=on` to give the time back and keep the enquiry, or `cancel=on` to cancel it outright (Phase 132e). Re-asks `isBookable` **at the write**, with `ignoreBooking` set to this booking. At any time for the call held; a NEW time must be two hours away |
| POST | `/portal/tasks/{id}` | own business | `client_tasks` → `ANSWERED`, with the answer state and who gave it. **A document request is refused here: it is answered by an upload** |
| POST | `/portal/documents` | `document.upload` on own business | a short-lived, single-key, single-use upload URL for **a version of a document** of a type with a receipt policy — by `type_code`, by `request`, or as a new version of `document`. **No file name is taken.** CRM 4 of 10 |
| PUT · POST | `/upload/put` | signed in, that key, `document.read` on the row | the bytes — by `PUT`, or from a browser by the portal form's multipart `POST` through the same handler and the same key (the CRM follow-on). Server-side allow-list, size cap, **content sniffing** — **then steps 2 to 7 on this request, and the request it answers RECEIVED**. A used link answers **409** |
| GET | `/portal/documents/{id}` | `document.read` on its document | what was done with it — the `document_events` trail |
| GET | `/portal/documents/{id}/download` | `document.read` on its document | **410 under fail-closed settings**: the original was deleted and there is nothing to serve |
| any | `/portal/settings/*` | `self` | the same implementation as `/staff/settings` |
| POST | `/portal/share-progress` | `share_consent.client_record` | the client withdraws a yes; giving ships OFF. CRM 8 of 10 |

| POST | `/portal/welcome/dismiss` | the user themselves | hides the welcome card for good: `users.welcome_dismissed_at` (migration 030), audited `WELCOME_DISMISSED` once (Phase 133c) |

| POST | `/staff/clients/{id}/research/pick` | `research.run` | Phase 146h. Shows the ranked candidates for ONE lookup whose match landed in the REVIEW band. A POST because it fetches the list from the register again, which is a call outside and, on a paid lookup, money. Refused unless the engagement letter is signed, the lookup itself recorded `MATCH_REVIEW`, and `PICK_SWITCHES.MATCH_REVIEW_PICK` is on — it is off. Nothing is stored |
| POST | `/staff/clients/{id}/research/pick/choose` | `research.run` | Phase 146h. Takes one candidate's own key in the register, or none. The same three conditions, asserted again on this request. The lookup runs once more and the key is looked for in the list it gives NOW; the chosen record is stored through the one door at grade B at most, and the choice, the person and the score go on the audit chain |
| POST | `/staff/clients/{id}/research/pick/take-back` | `research.run` | **Phase 146j.** Takes back a person's pick that stored a record — a separate act on the lookup's own row that **asks why** (Phase 139's shape), not a second pick screen. The form carries the lookup and the reason and nothing else. It removes the **one** `register_records` row the pick wrote, found through `register_match_picks.record_id` and never re-derived from the key, through `removeRegisterRecord` in `src/stubs/registers.ts` (the one door in is the one door out); puts the lookup back to FAILED, `MATCH_REVIEW`, so the list can be offered again with the taken-back entry marked; and **keeps the pick row** with who, when, why and what it removed (migration 054). Audited `REGISTER_MATCH_TAKEN_BACK`, carrying the reason. **Refused, in words**: before a signed engagement; for a pick of an entry we already held from another lookup (it wrote nothing of its own — and since Phase 146j `takePick` records no `record_id` for one); for a record older than the pick; and where a person has **checked and approved** a fact from it — the tick comes off first. Needs **no** pick switch: it calls nothing outside, and a switch turned off must not lock a wrong choice in. What was worked out **from** the record while it stood (a site area from Homedata's point, the check of who signed, a step the client was told was done, an issued report, a surveyor's job) is **listed, not un-derived** — kept on the pick row as kinds and counts, and written on the lookup's own line (`src/web/take-back.ts`) |

Since Phase 159f `/value`, `/line` and `/add` also take a typed rate's source — `rate_basis`, `rate_ref`, `rate_priced_at`, all three or none. **Every change needs a reason** and is kept for ever under the surveyor's name (migration 070, never edited or deleted). | POST | `/staff/documents/{id}/vouch` | `document.read` on the document, then `document.vouch` (ADMIN, SALES) | **Phase 146i.** One person answering, for one document, whether a name belongs to an **organisation** rather than to somebody — the footing on which `grant_funder_name` and `brc_issuing_body` are kept at all, and the one thing about a document that may never be inferred from it. Three buttons (yes, no, not sure) and no script; a later answer replaces an earlier one, in `document_field_vouches` (migration 043), audited `DOCUMENT_FIELD_VOUCHED` with the field, the answer and what it replaced and **never the value**. Only `yes` keeps the field: `no`, `not sure` and never asked are the same decision (**N4**). A field of another type, or an answer that is not one of the three, is refused and writes nothing. The client is never asked — they would be vouching for their own document **Phase 146j:** a `yes` that has already let a reading keep the name is **not** turned into a `no` by these buttons — it is taken back by the route below. And the same question is now asked **before the first reading**, where the type is chosen: on the staff upload forms (the client's files and the claim page), on the form where staff say what a file that came by email is, and on the document request. The upload writes the same row against the document it creates (`asked_on` UPLOAD); a request writes it against the request (`asked_on` REQUEST, `request_id`), and the **same row is adopted** by the document that answers it, before that document is read — one store, not two. The page's answer governs: where the document already has one, an earlier answer is neither written nor adopted |
| POST | `/staff/documents/{id}/vouch/withdraw` | `document.read` on the document, then `document.vouch` (ADMIN, SALES) | **Phase 146j.** Takes a `yes` back, with a reason. Takes **that field and no other** out of every `CLIENT_DOC` record the yes let it into (one per reading of a version of the document, found through the document's own versions) through `removeRegisterField` in `src/stubs/registers.ts`, and keeps the answer on its row marked withdrawn, with who, when and why (migration 054) — so the next reading leaves the field out and the row still says it was a yes. Refused where a person has checked and approved that field. Audited `DOCUMENT_FIELD_VOUCH_WITHDRAWN` with the field, the reason and how many records, and **never the name**. The result page lists what to look at again (a grant recorded from a client document since, a step the client was told was done, an issued report) |

| POST | `/staff/clients/{id}/checks/approve` | `fact.approve` — ADMIN, SALES | ticks one of the company's facts "Checked and approved": `fact_approvals` (migration 034), audited `FACT_APPROVED`; the fact is looked up on the request and a reference not this company's, a fact already ticked or a fact not known is refused; a client's answer to a claim fact is recorded as the fact by `recordFact` (source `CLIENT_ANSWER`) and ticked in one transaction (Phase 139) |
| POST | `/staff/clients/{id}/checks/unapprove` | `fact.approve` — ADMIN, SALES | takes a tick off, a reason required: `fact_approvals`, audited `FACT_UNAPPROVED` with the reference alone (Phase 139) |
| POST | `/staff/clients/{id}/steps/tell` | `client_step.tell` — ADMIN, SALES | tells the client a complete step, once: `client_step_notices` (migration 034), audited `CLIENT_STEP_TOLD`; an incomplete step, an empty one or one already told is refused (Phase 139). Since 30 September 2026 ("Tell the client and move on", `src/web/tell-and-move.ts`) it also emails the company and moves each claim whose current stage is now fully told forward one stage through `moveClaim`, when its checks allow |
| POST | `/staff/clients/{id}/steps/take-back` | `client_step.take_back` — ADMIN only | takes a told step back, a reason required: `client_step_notices`, audited `CLIENT_STEP_TAKEN_BACK`; the portal then says "being re-checked", never the reason (Phase 139) |
| POST | `/staff/settings/{profile\|username\|password\|working-times\|photo\|photo/remove}` | `self`, except working times | password change **rotates the session** |
| GET | `/staff/users` | `user.list` | **ADMIN alone**. Phase 151b: the roles the super admin made are offered by their own names, beside the built-in ones, when creating a user and when giving a role |
| GET · POST | `/staff/admin/roles` · `/new` · `/{ROLE_KEY}` · `/{ROLE_KEY}/retire` | `role.manage` — **the super admin alone**, and never given to a made role | Phase 151b, `src/web/role-editor-pages.ts`: list, make, change, retire. Each thing a role can do is a plain choice grouped by area, borrowed "Like Admin / Sales / Accounting / Surveying" from a built-in role that does it; a refusal is `roleRefusal`'s own sentence (400), audited `ROLE_REFUSED`, and saves nothing |
| POST | `/staff/compliance/identity-checks` · `/staff/compliance/aml/checks/{id}/identity` | `compliance.write` (ADMIN) | Phase 151b, `src/web/identity-checks.ts`: send an online identity check from the client's page or an open due diligence check. **Off with `IDENTITY_CHECK`**: refused in words with what is missing, audited `IDENTITY_CHECK_NOT_SENT`, nothing leaves the server. Sent: `identity_checks` (migration 047), no name kept; `IDENTITY_CHECK_SENT` |
| GET | `/staff/clients` · `/staff/clients/{id}` — **for Accounting** | `client_billing.read` (ADMIN, ACCOUNTING), drawn only for a login without `organisation.read` | Phase 151b, `src/web/billing.ts`: the billing view of the clients area (Word answer 40) |
| POST | `/staff/finance/{invoice}/chase` | `invoice.record_received` (ADMIN, ACCOUNTING) | Phase 151b: records a chase a person made — day, how, a short note — in `invoice_chases` (migration 048), append-only; audited `INVOICE_CHASED` without the note. Only an invoice still owed; not after today. **Sends nothing** |
| POST | `/staff/users/create` · `/{id}/role` · `/{id}/disable` · `/{id}/temporary-password` · `/{id}/reset-two-factor` | `user.create` · `user.assign_role` · `user.disable` · `user.update` | disabling **ends live sessions at once**. **Phase 151j:** creating shows a temporary password once (7 days) instead of taking a typed one; a new temporary password, or resetting a person's second step, ends their sessions |
| GET | `/staff/email-addresses` | `user.list` | **ADMIN alone.** Phase 146g: every address an email bounced back from for good, with the server's CODE and the email that bounced. Never the server's words |
| POST | `/staff/email-addresses/lift` | `user.list` | **ADMIN alone.** A member of staff says the address works again: **a NEW `email_suppressions` row**, never an edit — the bounce and the stop both stay on record. Lifting one that is not stopped changes nothing and is not an error. Audited `EMAIL_ADDRESS_LIFTED`, and the row carries no address |

### The JSON API

| Method | Path | Who | Returns |
|---|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/health` | anyone | `{ ok, stubs }` — **the stub list, machine-readable**, and it is the same array §4 is written from |
| POST | `/api/login` | anyone | `{ email, password }`. With nothing left to do, **200** and the user, never a hash, and the session cookie (`SameSite=Strict` here). **Phase 151k: with a step left, 202 and NO session** — `{ step, needs, method, sent_to, app_key, otpauth_uri, expires_at, step_token }`, where `needs` is `CHOOSE_PASSWORD`, `CHOOSE_METHOD`, `ADD_APP` or `GIVE_CODE`. **401** the generic failure, or the expired-temporary-password words |
| POST | `/api/login/step` · `/api/login/step/new-password` · `/api/login/step/method` · `/api/login/step/code` · `/api/login/step/resend` · `/api/login/step/recovery` | whoever holds the `step_token`, **in the JSON body, never the address** | Phase 151k, on **the same functions as the website's steps** (`src/web/sign-in.ts`: `describeStep`, `submitNewPassword`, `chooseMethod`, `submitCode`, `resendCode`) and the same rows: `{ step_token }` alone says where the step stands; then `{ password, again }`, `{ method: "EMAIL" \| "APP" }` (an app's key and QR address come back once, while it is set up), `{ code }`. **202** with the step still to do; **200** and the session cookie only when the last step is done. **400** a wrong code or password, in words; **401** when the step has gone — 15 minutes, 5 wrong codes, or finished. **Phase 151m:** the answer that finishes setting a step up carries `recovery_codes` (ten) **once**; a session's answer carries `recovery_codes_left` for a person with a second step; a VERIFY step's answer says `recovery_code: true`, and a recovery code goes to **its own route**, `/api/login/step/recovery` with `{ step_token, code }` — not `/code`, so a six-digit code and a recovery code are never guessed apart — sharing the 5 wrong codes |
| POST | `/api/account/recovery-codes` | the session (`user.change_own_password`, self) | **Phase 151m**, the same function as the website's button (`makeNewRecoveryCodes`): ten new recovery codes in `recovery_codes`, **once**; every unused old one thrown away | **400** with no second step; **401** with no session |
| POST | `/api/logout` | signed in | |
| GET | `/api/whoami` | signed in | the user **and the actions this actor may perform**, computed from `can()` rather than hard-coded per role |
| GET | `/api/invoices/{id}` | `invoice.read` on that invoice | the invoice. 404 if absent, **403 if not theirs** |
| GET | `/api/surveyor-jobs/{id}` | `surveyor_job.read` on that job | **the surveyor payload, from the same assembler the screen uses.** Corrected on 9 September 2026 — it returned 501 and said the workspace was a future phase |

> **`GET /api/whoami` is worth copying rather than reinventing.** It answers *what
> may this actor do* by asking `can()` for every registered action, so a client that
> needs to know whether to draw a button asks the same function the server enforces
> with. There is no second list of permissions to keep in step.

## 3.2 · External — every register and supplier

### THE ONE PAGE — every account, key, switch and precondition, read from the code on 23 September 2026

**Read this page before you open a single account.** _Phase 146g, 23 September 2026._ Every
connector below is BUILT AND SWITCHED OFF: the code is real, the tests are real, and not one
call fires. Turning one on is a one-word change **and a decision**, and the decision is the
"what must be true first" column — never the key.

**Every row was read from the code, not from memory.** The switch names are
`REGISTER_SWITCHES` (`src/connectors/registers.ts`), `CLAUDE_SWITCHES`
(`src/connectors/claude.ts`) and — since Phase 146h — `PICK_SWITCHES`
(`src/web/match-review.ts`), which is precondition 4 below; the key names are `SECRET_KEYS` (`src/secrets.ts`) and
`09-app/backend/secrets.example.env`; the addresses, rate limits and costs are `CALL_POLICIES`
(`src/connectors/sources.ts`).

**THE KEYS LIVE IN ONE FILE AND IT IS NOT IN THIS REPOSITORY.** Copy
`09-app/backend/secrets.example.env` — which carries **names and never values** — to
`~/.caprebate-secrets.env` (or the path in `CAPREBATE_SECRETS`), `chmod 600` it, and fill in
only what you have. **The back end refuses a key file inside the code folder.** A name left
empty means that service is not set up, its facts stay as questions, and nothing breaks.

**THE SMOKE TEST IS ONE COMMAND**, and it is the same command for every register row. **Since Phase
151g (28 September 2026) it keeps the five conditions Lakhbir allowed test lookups on** (Word question
170, answered 25 September 2026), **and it refuses rather than warns:**

```text
node src/cli.ts smoke-switched-on <SOURCE> --who "<name>" --why "<reason>"   # from 09-app/backend, once per switch-on
CAPREBATE_LIVE_SMOKE=1 node src/cli.ts smoke <SOURCE> --who "<name>" --why "<reason>"
   [--postcode P] [--company-number N] [--company-name X] [--address A] [--town T]
   [--nation england|wales|scotland|northern_ireland] [--council C] [--not-a-client]
CAPREBATE_LIVE_SMOKE=1 node src/cli.ts smoke OS_FEATURES --who "<name>" --why "<reason>"   # the public test point only
CAPREBATE_LIVE_SMOKE=1 node src/cli.ts smoke HMLR_INSPIRE --who "<name>" --why "<reason>"
   [--easting E --northing N] [--council <ONS code or HM Land Registry file name>] [--not-a-client]
```

**Phase 156a (5 October 2026):** `--nation` and `--council` are said by the person running it. Without the ONS
postcode table (`CAPREBATE_POSTCODE_DB`, installed on neither the Mac nor the test website), EPC and CQC could never
know the nation, and CQC never the council, so their test lookups ended "no call was made". `OS_FEATURES` asks
Ordnance Survey for the building outlines round the public test point and takes no point, postcode or address.
First real runs, 5 Oct 2026: EPC PASS, CQC PASS (`test/phase156a.test.ts`).

It makes **one real call to one service on public test data** — by default our own company,
CAP REBATE LTD 17307313, and 70 Whitehall, London SW1A 2AS — and says whether the service took
it. **A PASS means the address, the key and the account are right. It does not mean the
register holds anything about what you asked: "nothing matched" is still a pass.** It opens no
gate and changes no switch that ships. **The five conditions, and what enforces each**
(`src/connectors/test-lookups.ts`):

| Lakhbir's condition | What the command does |
| --- | --- |
| No more than three lookups per connector each time it is switched on | Counts the connector's lines since its last "switched on" line and **refuses a fourth**. A connector with no such line is refused until the person who switched it on runs `smoke-switched-on` — the only thing that starts the count again |
| Never a client's property | With `CAPREBATE_DB` set, **refuses** a postcode, company number or company name held there against any business, property or lead, however it is typed. Without it, **refuses anything but the defaults** unless `--not-a-client` is given, and the log says it was |
| Keep only pass or fail, the time and the connector | One line per lookup in **`~/.caprebate-test-lookups.jsonl`** (or `CAPREBATE_TEST_LOOKUP_LOG`) — **refused inside the repository**, like the key file. The line holds the connector, the time, PASS or NO, who and why: **never the values asked, never the answer**. The call's own audit row is written to a database in memory and thrown away with the answer |
| Log who ran it and why | **`--who` and `--why` are required** and refused when empty |
| Never use it to show the product to clients | **Said on every run, first**, and the why is on every line |

A lookup that cannot be logged is not made: the log is read, and opened for writing, before the
call, and a line in it the command did not write stops it, because the count is then not known.
**Tests:** `test/phase151g.test.ts`, run `cd 09-app/backend && node --test test/phase151g.test.ts` to count them.
**The land boundary is a source here too (Phase 151l):** `smoke HMLR_INSPIRE` downloads one council's file under the
same five conditions, with a point and a council in place of a company and a postcode — see its row below.
**Tests:** `test/phase151l.test.ts`.

Nothing else | Free. 600 requests per 5 minutes, shared between the two Companies House APIs | `smoke CH_COMPANY` |

One bearer token from the account page; no e-mail address is needed | `EPC_API_TOKEN` | `EPC` | The free gate | Free. Published 6,000 per 5 minutes; we use half | `smoke EPC` |
| **HM Land Registry Price Paid** | None. Open data, no key | — | `HMLR_PPD` | The free gate. **Supported living only** — it is residential sales, and the prescribed attribution must appear word for word | Free | `smoke HMLR_PPD` |

PlanIt's owner also writes that "it would not be wise to base any commercial services on the API" — a thing for whoever decides to turn this on | Free. PlanIt asks for **one request a minute and about 300 a day**, and we hold ourselves to exactly that | `smoke PLANNING` |

There is no default path, no bundled copy and no download-on-first-use | — (a path, not a key: `CAPREBATE_POSTCODE_DB`) | `ONS_POSTCODE` | The free gate. **Nothing is contacted — it is read from disk.** With no file, everything still works and nothing pretends to | Free | `smoke ONS_POSTCODE` |
| **Homedata** — the paid address lookup (UPRN, address, coordinates) | A Homedata account with a **prepaid balance**. **Lakhbir** — and read the instruction under this table before paying anything | `HOMEDATA_API_KEY` | `HOMEDATA_ADDRESS` | A **signed engagement** asserted server-side **and an operator's click**. Never before signature | **Per endpoint, not per call: 100 tokens = £1, so a token is a penny.** One lookup = the postcode search (2) + `address` (5) + `lr_title` (1) = **8 tokens, 8p** (as read 23 September 2026). **Corrected 6 October 2026 (Phase 157b): since Phase 156f a signed property costs 17p** (property 1, `address` 5, `lr_title` 1, `planning_history` 5, `title_boundary` 5; 19p where the address was typed, as the postcode search runs again), **and "Find my address" before signing costs 2p a search, at most £1 a day** (`ADDRESS_SEARCH_CAP`). **No quota and no monthly allowance — only the prepaid balance.** Empty, it answers **HTTP 402** with `required`, `available` and a `topup_url` in the body; an optional auto top-up is the safety net | `smoke HOMEDATA_ADDRESS` |
| **HM Land Registry Business Gateway** — the title register | **RETIRED BY PHASE 146k (25 September 2026). NO ACCOUNT TO OPEN.** Lakhbir decided the title register is looked up **by hand**, and took the Business Gateway details out of the key file. A person buys the official copy and title plan on HM Land Registry's own portal, **uploads them** (document type `TITLE_REGISTER`, Phase 146l) and records what they say on the property page (`/staff/properties/{id}/title-register`). The certificate still needed is that paperwork, not a Business Gateway client certificate. The connector is kept, retired, in `PAID_RETIRED` (`src/connectors/paid-registers.ts`) — in no map a click reads, never priced into a click — and its four key names are marked `retired` in `src/secrets.ts` and are **not** in `secrets.example.env`. Reversing it is putting the entry back in `PAID_CONNECTORS` and deleting the word `retired` | — (retired: `HMLR_BG_USERNAME`, `HMLR_BG_PASSWORD`, `HMLR_BG_CERT_PATH`, `HMLR_BG_CERT_PASSPHRASE`) | `HMLR_TITLE` (turns nothing on) | A signed engagement, for recording by hand. **Onward supply of title data to the client's accountant: the solicitor said YES on 27 September 2026 (relayed by Lakhbir; Phase 146m)** — the licence no longer awaits legal review; the client report stays shut | **About £7 a title**, bought by hand, not by the system | none — nothing calls it |
| **HM Land Registry INSPIRE Index Polygons** — the land boundary, for the site area _(Phase 150, 24 September 2026)_ | None. Open data under the Open Government Licence, no key and no account: one `.zip` of GML per local authority, from the published download page. Only the council's **name** is sent, never anything about the client | — | **`BOUNDARY_FETCHING_SWITCHED_ON`** in `04-phase-work/phase9/caprebate-boundary-import.ts`. `REGISTER_SWITCHES.HMLR_INSPIRE` **is that same constant**, read so the panel shows what the fetcher obeys: one switch, not two | The free gate. **“Can we make a few small, logged test lookups to check a data connector works?” (Word question 170) was answered by Lakhbir on 25 September 2026: yes, on five conditions** (Phase 151g). The first download, which confirms the file's shape, is a test lookup under those conditions, and **the tool for it is built (Phase 151l, 28 September 2026): `smoke HMLR_INSPIRE`**, see the Smoke test cell. Nobody has run it yet: running it is Lakhbir's, or a person he names. **Switching the fetcher on stays Lakhbir's**, and the test lookup does not do it. **Wired by Phase 151a and still off: `siteAreaFromLandBoundary` (`src/connectors/site-area.ts`) is the one caller of `inspireBoundaryFetcher` in the application; the only other file under `src/` that may name it is the test lookup, `src/connectors/boundary-test-lookup.ts`** — a test fails if any third file does, if the test lookup switches it on anywhere but its one call, or if anything but the smoke command reaches the test lookup. It refuses at once while the switch is false; checks `checkBoundaryUse('INTERNAL_CALCULATION')`; turns each council code into HM Land Registry's file name through `src/connectors/inspire-councils.ts` (all 318 ONS codes, derived from ONS's April 2025 names-and-codes list and HM Land Registry's 318 files, eight named by hand with the reason; a code with no row is refused); reads **both** councils' files when a person adds the neighbouring council for a site on a boundary; injects the fetcher into `fetchAndImportBoundary`; and stores `site_area_m2` with `site_area_m2_grade` (B at best) and `inspire_id` through the one door under `HMLR_INSPIRE` (name exclusion register subitem 15). **The point**: a British National Grid easting and northing a person enters, or an **ETRS89** latitude and longitude converted by Ordnance Survey's OSTN15 (`src/connectors/ostn15.ts`) — **which needs `CAPREBATE_OSTN15_FILE` naming OS's `OSTN15_OSGM15_DataFile.txt` on the server, outside the repository, checked once with `tools/ostn15-verify.ts` against the pack's test points.** **On the Mac it is at `~/caprebate-data/ostn15/`**, from OS's full developer pack (`OSTN15-OSGM15-DevelopersPack.zip`, downloaded 24 September 2026, 15,499,083 bytes, SHA-256 `67069a6d6efe74f637249366f400f3192c03853c78d5a9c3eac2c78ede88364d`), and every one of OS's forty ETRS89-to-OSGB36 test points agrees to the millimetre. Copy the same file to the server and check it again there. **The demo launcher (`tools/demo-server.cjs`) names the Mac's copy in `CAPREBATE_OSTN15_FILE` when it is there and nothing else is named; the live server must name its own.** `tools/ostn15-verify.ts` exits 0 when every point agrees, 1 when one does not, and 2 with a plain sentence when nothing could be checked; `test/phase151c.test.ts` proves all three on a generated, invented full grid (Phase 151c, which also found and fixed a blank answer cell being read as 0). Without it a latitude and longitude is refused, never approximated. **Homedata's own easting and northing are used as the point after signing** (`homedataPointOf`; its schema publishes them, and the address lookup now keeps them). **Homedata's latitude and longitude are refused**: its schema labels them WGS84, and OSTN15 converts ETRS89 (`HOMEDATA_COORDINATE_DATUM`, null, until Homedata says in writing they are ETRS89). No point, no lookup: the site area stays asked. **One file a council a month**: `CAPREBATE_INSPIRE_STORE_DIR` names a folder outside the repository (one inside it is refused) where a council's `.zip` is kept by council and release month for no more than the licence row's 35 days; absent, every lookup downloads. **Its one caller is the research run after signing (Phase 151c, `src/web/site-area-run.ts`, `runSiteAreas`)**: on the operator's one click, for each property, it passes the postcode's council (`councilOfPostcode`), every neighbouring council a person added on the property page, and the point — the easting and northing a person entered there, else Homedata's own — with the grid from `CAPREBATE_OSTN15_FILE` and the store from `CAPREBATE_INSPIRE_STORE_DIR`. It waits on the switch, then a signed letter, the free gate and the client's own "answer it myself" choice, and the research panel carries **one line in words per property for every state** (switched off, waiting, not permitted, no point, no council, ready, stored, already held, not found), drawn without any call; only stored, already held and not found are kept (`site_area_runs`, migration 052). Nothing refuses and nothing throws. **The site area is for our own checks: the licence row keeps everything from this source out of client reports, and `checkBoundaryUse` now says the same — whether the number may go in a report waits on Lakhbir's answer to the Word question "May the site area we work out from a land boundary go in a client's report?" (Phase 151c).** **HM Land Registry does not publish the GML's element names: the first allowed download must confirm the shape**, and the reader refuses the whole file if any polygon in it cannot be read. The site area is for our own checks only; the boundary, a map or its coordinates never go in a client report | Free. Not a rate: whole-file downloads, five in five minutes and forty a day, our own cautious limit | **`CAPREBATE_LIVE_SMOKE=1 node src/cli.ts smoke HMLR_INSPIRE --who W --why Y`**, after `smoke-switched-on HMLR_INSPIRE` (Phase 151l): the same command, the same log and the same five refusals as every other row. By default it downloads **City of Westminster's** file and looks for **70 Whitehall's published grid reference, TQ 30120 79956** (easting 530120, northing 179956; Historic England, National Heritage List entry 1267063). Only the council's name leaves; the point stays here. `--easting E --northing N` and `--council <ONS code or HM Land Registry file name>` replace them, and are checked as a postcode is: against `CAPREBATE_DB` (a point within 250 metres of one held for a property, or a council added for one, is refused), else only with `--not-a-client`. **A PASS means the file downloaded, every polygon in it was read — the shape check this row asks for, because the reader refuses the whole file on one it cannot read — and the polygon holding the point was found. Nothing more.** A NO says how far it got (not downloaded, shape refused, not found). The file and the polygon are thrown away: nothing is kept in `CAPREBATE_INSPIRE_STORE_DIR` and nothing is stored. `BOUNDARY_FETCHING_SWITCHED_ON` stays false. `test/phase151l.test.ts`, `test/phase150.test.ts` and `test/phase151a.test.ts` prove it on invented fixtures |
| **Homedata** — the paid planning route | The same account and the same key | `HOMEDATA_API_KEY` | `HOMEDATA_PLANNING` | **BUILT AND NOT CHOSEN.** PlanIt stays the source of record. Whether planning moves to the paid tier — where it would stop firing before signature — is **Lakhbir's decision, not a build one** | `planning_history` is 5 tokens (5p) plus the address chain | `smoke HOMEDATA_PLANNING` |
| **The Claude API** — reading client documents | An Anthropic account. **Lakhbir** | `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` | `CLAUDE_SWITCHES.DOCUMENT_READING`, and `SCANNED_PAGES` separately for a scanned PDF's page pictures | **The data protection officer approving the provider and its terms, and a director signing** — the Word question "Which AI provider reads client documents, and on what terms?". Until then `src/connectors/sources.ts` holds `CLAUDE_DOCUMENTS` at region **US** with `international_transfer_approved: false`, and **every call is refused before it is made**. `SCANNED_PAGES` is a **second** decision: a picture of a page is sent before anything has been stripped from it | Per token, on the account's own tier | No smoke command: it is not a register. `node --test test/phase146e.test.ts` proves the wiring on recorded answers; a live check is one document through the upload page with the switch on |

**Where the key file is, on the Mac (24 September 2026).** `~/.caprebate-secrets.env` is a link to `Health Capital Allowances/Private - keys (do not share)/caprebate-secrets.txt` in Lakhbir's OneDrive, which is outside the code folder, so `loadSecrets` reads it; a key file inside the code folder is still refused. On 24 September it held `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and the four `IONOS_SMTP_*` names; every other key in the table was still to be created.

**Four preconditions that are not obvious from the table, and each has cost somebody time:**

1. **CA7 — THE 200-PROPERTY MATCHING MEASUREMENT COMES BEFORE ANY FREE LOOKUP IS SWITCHED ON.**
   Every free register is matched to a typed address at thresholds (0.90 / 0.70,
   `MATCH_THRESHOLDS` in `src/connectors/matching.ts`) that are the specification's **guesses**.
   `09-app/backend/tools/match-measure.ts` is built and refuses to run: it looks up 200 real
   addresses, so it waits on the data protection officer's answer to "May we run our
   address-matching test on 200 real addresses?". **Run it, set the thresholds from its result,
   then switch a free lookup on — in that order.** `--plan` says what it would do; `--score`
   and `--self-test` need no network.
2. **THE ONS POSTCODE TABLE IS A PRECONDITION OF THE CQC LOOKUP, not a nicety.** The care
   regulator's API **cannot be searched by postcode** — `/locations` filters by local authority
   — so the connector searches the **council area the ONS table gives**. With no
   `CAPREBATE_POSTCODE_DB`, the CQC lookup has nothing to search on and the fact stays asked.
3. **THE contact@caprebate.co.uk MAILBOX IS A PRECONDITION OF PLANNING**, because PlanIt blocks
   a request whose User-Agent carries no contact identifier, and it is a precondition of email
   in both directions.
4. **A PERSON'S PICK FOR A CLOSE MATCH IS A SWITCH WITH NO ACCOUNT AND NO KEY, AND IT IS THE
   DATA PROTECTION OFFICER'S TO TURN ON.** _Phase 146h, 23 September 2026._
   `PICK_SWITCHES.MATCH_REVIEW_PICK` (`src/web/match-review.ts`) is **false**. Switched on, a
   lookup whose best candidate scored in the REVIEW band — close to the client's address and
   not close enough for us to choose it — can hand its **ranked candidate list** to the back
   office, where a member of staff chooses one entry or none.
   **What must be true first, and none of it is a key.** A signed engagement on that client,
   asserted server-side on the request (safeguard S15 of the assessment rules out showing a
   prospect other buildings' records, so this is never offered before signature, whatever the
   source's tier); the source's own connector switch on; and the data protection officer's
   agreement that a member of staff may **see** a handful of neighbouring register entries to
   resolve one address — which is a different question from CA7's test lookups and has not been
   asked yet.
   **What it never does.** It stores no candidate list, logs none, and shows none to a client.
   A choice is stored through the one door at **grade B at most**, and the choice, the person
   and the score go on the audit chain. **A stored pick cannot be undone on that screen** — the
   lookup leaves the REVIEW band once a record lands — **and since Phase 146j it is taken back
   by its own act** (`takeBackPick`, route `.../research/pick/take-back`), which asks why,
   removes the one record the pick wrote, and keeps the pick with the reason. Taking back does
   not need this switch on.

#### AND ONE INSTRUCTION FOR WHOEVER OPENS THE HOMEDATA ACCOUNT — IT IS WORTH £50

**THE FIRST PAYMENT AN ORGANISATION EVER MAKES IS MATCHED 100%, ONCE, EVER.** £50 becomes
**10,000 tokens**. £20 becomes 4,000. **Tokens never expire, and later one-off top-ups earn
nothing at all.**

**OPEN THE ACCOUNT WITH A £50 FIRST PAYMENT, NOT £20.** Opening it casually with a small test
payment throws away £50 of credit permanently, and it cannot be undone.

_What 10,000 tokens buys, re-derived from `HOMEDATA_SLUG_TOKENS` and `HOMEDATA_PATH_TOKENS` on
23 September 2026 rather than inherited:_ **this build asks for two slugs**, so one property
costs the postcode search (2) plus `address` (5) plus `lr_title` (1) = **8 tokens**, and 10,000
tokens is **1,250 properties**. _(Corrected 6 October 2026, Phase 157b: since Phase 156f a signed property is 17 tokens, so 10,000 tokens is about 590 properties, less the address searches before signing, at most 100 tokens a day.)_ If every slug the supplier sells were added — and none is added
without a decision — one property would cost 2 + 38 = **40 tokens**, and 10,000 tokens would be
**250 properties**. **At 120 jobs a year that is between two and ten years of lookups**, and
either way the £50 is the difference between one of those and none of it. _(Phase 146g's own
prompt said "roughly 312 fully enriched properties", which is 32 tokens a property. No
combination of the published weights comes to 32, so the two derivations above are given
instead, from the weights themselves.)_

#### What the build needs from a host — and nothing more

**HOSTING IS THE DEVELOPER'S.** Lakhbir, 22 September 2026. **This section recommends no host**
and names none. It says what this build requires of one; anything not on this list is the
developer's to choose.

1. **UK DATA RESIDENCY FOR EVERY STORE.** The database, the document store, backups and logs.
   This is a requirement, not a preference: it is what clause 7 of the engagement letter and
   section 4 of the privacy notice tell a client.
2. **A SECRET STORE IN PLACE OF THE KEY FILE.** `~/.caprebate-secrets.env` is right for one
   developer's laptop and wrong for a server. `CAPREBATE_SECRETS` points at another path today;
   a host's secret manager replaces the file. **The back end refuses a key file inside the code
   folder**, and no key is ever printed, logged or written into a record.
3. **HTTPS IN FRONT.** The application sets `Secure` on every cookie and terminates nothing
   itself. Certificates, renewal and HSTS are the host's.
4. **`CAPREBATE_TRUST_PROXY=1` BEHIND THE HOST'S PROXY.** Only then does the address a signature
   records become the client's rather than the proxy's (`clientIp`, `src/web/router.ts`). Set it
   **only** where a proxy you trust sets `X-Forwarded-For` — otherwise anyone can type one.
5. **PERSISTENT STORAGE FOR TWO THINGS, AND A SERVERLESS HOST NEEDS BOTH REPLACED.**
   - the database — **today one SQLite file**, `CAPREBATE_DB`;
   - the documents — **today a folder**, `CAPREBATE_DOCS`, outside the web root.
   **On a host with no durable disk, both must become managed UK services** (a managed
   database, and object storage behind the existing `ObjectStore` port). **Do not write a second
   upload handler:** the port is `src/web/upload-handler-port.ts` and
   `assertContractCovered()` fails if the contract grows a step it does not cover.
6. **BACKUPS THAT HONOUR THE DELETION RULES — SETTLE THIS WITH THE HOST BEFORE YOU CHOOSE IT.**
   A document unlinked from disk and alive in a 30-day whole-site backup **has not been deleted,
   and the deletion record would say it had**. `BACKUP_CYCLE` is a **named blank** — NOT SET — and
   `assertRestoreApproved` refuses a restoration without the data protection officer's approval.
   **The host must be able to leave the two client-document folders out of its backups**, and
   that question is open: "Can the host leave our two client-document folders out of its
   backups?" (Word question 155). **The questions to put to the host, in writing, are drafted**:
   `08-monday-doc-exports/12949133180__Email-to-the-host-backups-DRAFT.md` (rewritten 24 September
   2026 by Phase 150 for whichever host you choose; its only question is the backup one). Its
   answer sets `BACKUP_CYCLE`, which the Retention Procedure (4.1, point 4) needs before a
   deletion can be called finished. The data protection officer answered "Not needed" to "Is the
   backup risk serious enough that we must first ask the Information Commissioner?" (Word
   question 158), so no prior consultation is planned and the host's written answer is the only
   record of how long a deleted file survives. **If the holding folder cannot be left out, do not
   put the uploads on that host.**
7. **THE SCHEDULED JOBS THE RETENTION REGISTER RUNS.** Each is one command, and each is safe to
   run while its own switch is off — it does nothing and says why:
   ```text
   node src/cli.ts sweep-originals    # kept original files whose time is up
   node src/cli.ts sweep-messages     # chat messages and emailed replies whose time is up
   node src/cli.ts send-outbox        # anything saved while sending was on and not yet sent
   node src/cli.ts read-mailbox       # replies to contact@, filed or queued
   node src/cli.ts verify-audit       # the audit chain — run it after every restore
   ```
8. **WHAT THE HOST IS NOT ASKED FOR.** No card processing and no PCI scope — invoice and bank
   transfer only. No search service, no queue, no cache and no container registry are required
   by this build. One Node process serves both applications.

---

**NOTHING BELOW FIRES. NOT ONE CALL — and since 22 September 2026 that is a statement about the
switches rather than about missing code.** _Corrected by Phase 146g, 23 September 2026: this
paragraph used to read "the connectors do not exist", and Phases 146b to 146f built them._ The
engine still makes zero network calls; `derive()` consumes a `bundle` of what connectors
returned, and **the connectors now exist, are tested on recorded answers, and are every one
switched off**. This table is what each source answers and on what terms; the page above is how
each one is turned on.

**The tiers are read out of the source register, not restated here**:
`04-phase-work/phase3/caprebate-source-register.ts` is the register of what we
consult and holds each source's publisher, dataset, licence, permitted fields,
excluded fields and name exceptions. `09-app/backend/src/web/research.ts` maps
those ids onto tiers.

### Tier 1 — free. Fires once the client has been **informed**

| **Companies House** — company profile | entity type, number, status, **year end**, incorporation date, registered-office postcode, group links | OGL v3, self-serve key, 600 req / 5 min | **No Companies House match routes to *ask*, never to "sole trader"** — a name that does not match is far more often a trading name, a misspelling or a group subsidiary. That is an N4 fix, not a preference |

Partnerships and sole traders file nothing. **Handle both as ordinary outcomes, not as errors** |

Its download address is undocumented and may change. No registration status or service registration date in the file. **Wales only** |
