**What the suppliers' own pages said on 23 September 2026, when Phase 146c built these connectors against them** (every page and its date is in the source register's `SUPPLIER_DOCS`). Where the table above and this paragraph differ, this paragraph is the later reading. **The CQC API has no postcode filter** — `/locations` filters by local authority and others, and its list items carry only the location id, name and postcode — so the connector searches the council area the ONS postcode table gives, which makes **loading that table a precondition of the CQC lookup**; the current portal publishes **no rate limit and no API terms**, and CQC's own page says the data is under the Open Government Licence and "updated daily", so the currency obligation flagged above could not be found in writing and the cache window is held at **0** until the solicitor answers. **An energy certificate search carries no floor area or building type**: only the certificate itself does (`/api/certificate`), and a domestic certificate's age band and tenure are codes this build does not decode, so those questions stay asked. **HM Land Registry's prescribed attribution has a fixed year**: "Contains HM Land Registry data © Crown copyright and database right 2021. This data is licensed under the Open Government Licence v3.0." **planning.data.gov.uk's planning application dataset says of itself that it "is incomplete and is not yet ready for use"**, so the lookup goes on to PlanIt, **whose owner asks for one request a minute, about 300 a day, an email address in the User-Agent, and writes that "it would not be wise to base any commercial services on the API"** — a thing for whoever decides to switch planning on. **Companies House publishes no "filleted" flag**, so a small company's accounts are read as *may leave out the profit and loss account*, never as filed or not.

**Two fields in `CB_REGISTRY.SOURCES` are post-signature only, although their sources sit in this free tier.**
_Added 15 September 2026._ `psc_name` on the `companies_house` source (name
exception 1) and `proprietor_name` on the `hmlr` source (name exception 2) are
the only two entries in any source's `permitted` list. Neither may be retrieved
before a signed engagement, checked on the server. Condition CA2 in section 15 of
the legitimate interests assessment says so in terms: before a signed engagement
only the free sources and endpoints it lists are called, and the list of persons
with significant control, the officers endpoint and officer search, the title
register and the paid address lookup are never called. So at the address question
`companies_house` means the company search and the company profile only, and
`hmlr` means Price Paid only, for supported living. **The site's source table does
not carry that distinction today**: both sources have `gate:null`, and
`psc_name` and `proprietor_name` sit in the same `fields` lists as the free
facts. `CB_REGISTRY.precondition` gates the free tier as a whole, so opening it
must not open these two names. Keep them behind the signed-engagement check that
gates Tier 2.

**Built 15 September 2026 (Phase 132c): the site's source table now carries that
distinction.** The paragraph above, which says it does not, is kept as the record of
that morning. Both sources keep `gate:null`, for two reasons: the rest of each source
is free, and `research.ts` (`licenceGateFor`) reports any `gate` string as a
*licence* blocker. Instead, every source carries a field-level `postSignature` list:
`["proprietor_name"]` on `hmlr`, `["psc_name"]` on `companies_house`, and an empty
list on the others. `CB_REGISTRY.classifyField(source, field, engagement)` takes the
record `assertSignedEngagement` returns on the server. The two names return
`NOT_PERMITTED_YET`, marked `postSignature: true`, unless that record says
`signed === true`. So no record, `signed:false`, "unsure" and a bare "yes" all keep
them shut. That is a state, never a throw: the field is simply not consulted. With a
signed record the two names are permitted as before. Tenure, price paid, completion
date, entity type and year end need no engagement. `CB_REGISTRY.precondition` still
gates the free tier as a whole, and opening it does not open the two names. The site
harness asserts all of this both ways in suite AA (AA1 to AA3). The back end was the
one gap left that day: `TIER_BY_SOURCE` in `src/web/research.ts` tiered `CH_PSC` as
`FREE`, so the sources panel would have shown the list of persons with significant
control as waiting only on the privacy notice.

**Closed 16 September 2026 (Phase 132f): the back end reads the same distinction off
the engine.** `CH_PSC` stays tiered `FREE`, because the tier is about cost and the
list is free of charge. Each catalogue entry now carries `post_signature_fields`.
`postSignatureFieldsFor` in `src/web/research.ts` derives it from the engine's own
`CB_REGISTRY.SOURCES[].postSignature` and the source register's
`personal_name_exceptions`. It is not a second hard-coded list. There is one
vocabulary join, `REGISTRY_FIELD_OF`, because the register spells the title
register's personal proprietor `proprietor_name_personal`. That gives `["psc_name"]`
on `CH_PSC`, `["proprietor_name_personal"]` on `HMLR_TITLE` (already `PAID`), and an
empty list on every other source, including `CH_COMPANY` and `HMLR_PPD`, whose free
facts are untouched. `stateUnderGates` asks `postSignatureOpen`, which calls
`CB_REGISTRY.classifyField(source, field, engagement)` with the record
`assertSignedEngagement` returns. Before signature the source is `NOT_PERMITTED_YET`,
with the reason (CA2, not consulted). With the notice unpublished as well, the row
names both. It is a state, never a throw. The same change fixed an N4 slip in the
paid branch: `!engagement.signed` read a "not sure" as truthy and threw on an absent
record. Both now read as unsigned, because only `signed === true` opens. The proof,
both ways, is `test/phase132f.test.ts`, line 5 (signed opens; unsigned, "not sure",
null and an absent record stay shut; taking `psc_name` off the engine's list removes
it from the panel).

### Tier 2 — paid. Fires only after a signed engagement, on one operator click

| Source | What it answers | Cost | Limits |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Homedata** address lookup | **UPRN**, full address, postcode — keys every call above | **The rate card is on file, read 23 September 2026** (Phase 146g; this said "Rate card not on file. It is an open question" and that is no longer true). **Per endpoint, not per call: 100 tokens = £1.** One lookup is 8 tokens — 8p (read 23 September 2026; **since Phase 156f, 6 October 2026, 17p a signed property and 2p for "Find my address" before signing, capped at £1 a day** — Phase 157b). **No quota and no monthly allowance, only a prepaid balance**; empty, it answers HTTP 402 with `required`, `available` and a `topup_url`. See the one page above, and the £50 first-payment instruction with it | **Covers Scotland; nothing else here does.** **In the source register since Phase 129** as `HOMEDATA_ADDRESS`, under the supplier's published Terms of Service. The supplier publishes no licence name, no attribution wording, no cache window and no permission to reproduce its data outward. The register holds each gap as a refusal: a cache window of 0 days, where null would mean no limit; every outward channel closed; and legal review required. Its own coverage pages also disagree about Scotland. See §5 |
| **HMLR title register** (official copy) | price paid or value stated for transfers registered since April 2000 — **commercial included** — tenure, proprietor (**name exception 2**), prior-owner chain | **£7 per title**, per property; was £3 until 9 Dec 2024 | ~~Business Gateway account, network-access agreement, **lead time**.~~ **Since Phase 146k no Business Gateway account, agreement or certificate is needed: a person buys the official copy on HM Land Registry's own portal (updated 28 September 2026, Phase 153).** **Onward supply to a third party is restricted — the accountant's pack is CLEARED: the solicitor said yes on 27 September 2026 (Phase 146m).** A prior-owner chain may need a second title **Since Phase 146k: bought and recorded BY HAND, not through Business Gateway** |
| **BCIS** elemental cost rates — **the cost source, chosen over Spon's on 24 September 2026 (Word answers 6 and 8)** | £/m² elemental rates, location factors, contract-size adjustment | BCIS Ltd subscription (Annex One of its terms of sale, read by Phase 151) | **Named licensed users may use it internally; extracts may go to clients only where they relate to projects created in BCIS's own service; no grant to the client's accountant or HMRC, no attribution wording and no API licence are published. Since 10 Oct 2026 BCIS has confirmed our use (relayed by Lakhbir; Word question 218, closed), so the Phase 160 sweep opened `BCIS_COMMERCIAL` to the client report, the accountant's pack and the HMRC pack; our credit names BCIS on every rate. Rates may be USED to compute; the underlying table may not be reproduced** |

### Explicitly out of scope, so nobody spends a week on it

- **GMC** has no public API — a licensed daily download at roughly **£815/yr + VAT**. **GDC is web search only.** Clinician verification is out of scope. **Do not write a scraper.**
- **HMRC "Check a UK VAT number"** — v1 withdrawn 17 February 2025; v2 is authenticated with a ~2-week registration lead. It returns the **business** name and address and **no people at all**: an entity-existence check, never a role check.
- **LinkedIn** — a human check on a specific enquiry, recording only the conclusion, is defensible. **A scraper or a bulk enrichment tool is not**, and would need its own entry in the record of processing. Keep it manual, keep it rare, keep the result and not the source.
- **Practice managers, financial controllers, bookkeepers and external accountants appear on no public register anywhere, free or paid.** That is most of the signer dropdown, and it is the fact that determines the whole design of role corroboration: a check that treated a non-match as suspicious would insult the majority of genuine enquirers.

### When a source is down — and this is a rule, not a preference

**Record the gap. Never substitute a mean.** A source that cannot be consulted
reports `NOT_PERMITTED_YET` or `FAILED` and **the facts it would have supplied
stay `asked`**. Nothing refuses and nothing throws. **If your build produces a
failed estimate, an error on a client screen, a source rendered as *unavailable*,
or a `0` or `null` standing in for a fact a paid call would have supplied, it is
built wrong** — and the last of those is an N4 breach, because it routes an
unknown to the favourable branch.

### The correction this document makes to the Provenance Map

**The API & Data Provenance Map told a developer to fire the title register
"without thinking about it". That instruction is withdrawn and is not carried
here.** It was written before the two-tier rule of 4 September 2026, which makes
consideration the whole point: **a paid call fires only after a signed engagement
and only on an operator's click.** What survives of the old sentence is its actual
point — that **no per-call budget approval is wanted** — and that is now expressed
as the rule expresses it: *the operator picks the moment, not the calls.*

### The one measurement nobody has taken, and it decides a threshold

The address lookup is paid and its UPRN is what the free registers are keyed on,
so tier 1 looks as though it depends on tier 2. **It does not, quite:** EPC, the
care regulators, Companies House and Price Paid can all be searched on a typed
postcode or name. **The paid UPRN buys match certainty, not access.** So free
calls run early on a fuzzy match against a confidence threshold and the paid
lookup upgrades it after signature. **The matching specification says to measure
the real failure rate over 200 properties, and nobody has.** Do not assert the
fuzzy match is good enough, and do not assert it is not — **price the measurement
as work and set the threshold from its result.**

---

# 4 · What is real, what is stubbed, and exactly where

**IF IT IS STUBBED, IT IS IN THIS TABLE. Everything else is real and the tests
prove it.** _(Phase 146g, 23 September 2026: **the table gained a fifth row**, and it gained it
for the reason the table exists. Reading a client document was built by Phase 146e, had no
caller in `src/` at all, and was named nowhere a reader would look — so a developer who ran this
build and watched an upload finish could reasonably have concluded that uploads were read. They
are not. Row 5 says so.)** A convincing stub that is not listed here is the failure mode this
document exists to prevent — and it is not hypothetical: **writing this table
found one.** The text extractor opened with the words *"and it is a STUB,
declared as one"*, declared itself in its own file, and appeared in no list a
reader would look at. It is row 4 now, it is in `STUBS`, and there is a test that
a module calling itself a stub in prose is in that array.

**AND ROW 4 GOT SMALLER ON 9 SEPTEMBER 2026 WITHOUT GOING AWAY, which is the
harder half of keeping a table like this honest.** Phase 113c built a real
`.docx` and PDF reader behind that port. OCR and scanned PDFs remain unread, so
the row **changes wording and stays**: deleting a row to make a list shorter is
exactly the failure this table exists to prevent, and a reader who found three
rows where there had been four would conclude that the fourth thing now works.

**The machine-readable copy of this table is `STUBS` in `src/app.ts:177`**, served
at `GET /api/health`.

**FIVE LINE REFERENCES IN THIS TABLE HAD MOVED, AND CRM 10 OF 10 FOUND THEM BY RUNNING A CHECK
RATHER THAN BY READING.** Every name they pointed at was unchanged; the lines were not —
`outboxTransport` had gone from line 39 to 103 and its label from 57 to 124, `STUBS` from 72 to
79, the surveyor's signature notice from 997 to 1062, `declaredTypeClassifier` from 98 to 111. A
reference that lands on the wrong line sends a developer to the wrong code while looking exact.
**`node 03-app/caprebate-crm-fold-check.cjs` now reads every `file:line` in this section and fails
when the name beside it is not on that line.** It failed on the signature notice again after the CRM
follow-on added six lines above it, and the reference now reads 1068. **It failed on four on
15 September 2026, after Phase 132**: the signature notice had moved from 1068 to 1150, and the data
protection officer role's three names eleven lines down — the switch from 885 to 896, its rules from 890
to 901, the decision from 910 to 921. Each name was unchanged, and each reference now reads its line. **It failed on seven on
16 September 2026, after Phase 132e** added the view-only role's rule table above the officer's, and lines above the
case stamp, its audit kind and the partnership row: the switch from 896 to 1089, its rules from 901 to 1094, the decision
from 921 to 1114, `DPO_ROLE` from 70 to 80, `writeCaseStamp` from 229 to 230, `CASE_STAMPED` from 217 to 227, and the
partnership row from 187 to 203.

Try each lookup on the vendor test environment (`HMLR_TITLE_TEST`, which costs nothing) before either switch is turned on~~ **(Updated 28 September 2026, Phase 153: since Phase 146k the title register is done by hand, so nothing for it goes in the key file and there is nothing to install or confirm — the four `HMLR_BG_*` names are retired, and `SOAP_PATHS` stays unconfirmed only for the retired connector. Only the address lookup is left to switch on.)** |
| **4** | **Text extraction from the documents this build still cannot read.** **NARROWED on 9 September 2026 (Phase 113c) and deliberately not deleted.** `.docx` files, and PDFs whose text sits in **uncompressed or FlateDecode** content streams, are now **read and stripped for real** — proved end to end by a name planted in each, found by the extractor and removed by the receipt policy, with the removal in `document_events`. **What still yields one marker line:** a **scanned** PDF (page images, no text operators — that is OCR; **Phase 146e, 22 September 2026, built a Claude vision path for it and switched it off on its own**, see row 5: with it off, as shipped, a scan keeps its marker), an **encrypted** PDF, LZW/ASCII85/JBIG2/CCITT-filtered content, `.doc`, `.xlsx`, `.pptx`. On those the strip runs over a marker rather than over the document. | `src/web/uploads.ts` (`documentTextExtractor`) → `src/web/extract-text.ts`; the shared zip reader is `src/web/zip.ts` | **Nothing pretends to have read a document, and nothing returns an empty page either.** Every unreadable path returns the marker: an empty reading would say downstream *we looked and there was nothing to redact*, which is the favourable branch **N4** forbids an unknown from taking. There is a test for each refusal and one asserting the marker is never an empty page | Add OCR behind the same `TextExtractor` port — it is one named module, so this is a swap and not a search. **Priced in §5 as DS-W2, and DS-W2 is now smaller than it was.** For a scan whose pages are JPEG pictures, the vision path is already behind the port (`documentTextExtractor` at `src/web/uploads.ts:104`, its pictures from `pdfPageImages` at `src/web/extract-text.ts:520`); an encrypted PDF, a JBIG2 or CCITT fax scan and the other formats keep the marker whatever its switch says |
| **5** | **Reading a client document — BUILT by Phase 146e (22 September 2026), GIVEN ITS CALLER by Phase 146g (23 September 2026), and switched off.** **What Phase 146g found, and it is the reason this row exists:** the upload flow **never ran steps 8, 9 or 10 of the receipt contract**. `processUpload` ended at DISCHARGED; `openTransparency`, `runExtraction` and `writeClaims` were exported by the port and called from **nowhere in `src/`**. So every finished upload stopped with the strip done and nothing read, and Phase 146e's reader had no caller at all. It has one now, **behind `CLAUDE_SWITCHES.DOCUMENT_READING`, which is `false`**. Switched on, a finished upload opens its transparency record, sends **only the segments the strip retained** to the Claude API, turns the answer into claims, and writes them by the one door (`ingestRegisterRecord`) as a `CLIENT_DOC` record keyed on the document. **Switched off — as shipped — a finished upload behaves exactly as it did before: the same row, the same state, the same audit rows, nothing sent.** | `CLAUDE_SWITCHES` at `src/connectors/claude.ts:96`, `prepareClaudeModel` at `:357`, `readScannedPages` at `:414`, `toRawExtraction` at `:235`; the caller, `readAndClaim`, at `src/web/uploads.ts:530`, its outcome type `ReadingOutcome` at `:483`, and what a route hands it, `readerFor`, at `:188` | **Every refusal is a STATE and every failure leaves the field ASKED (N4).** No key, the transfer unapproved, a refusal, a time-out, an unreadable answer, a field with no locator or a confidence outside 0 to 1, a value that looks like a person's name: the field goes to `not_found` and stays asked of the client — never a nought, never a guess, never a screen error. The **payload guard** still runs before the network, so a segment the strip did not pass is refused before anything is sent. `CPSE_REPLIES` and `SALE_CONTRACT` are never sent: a person reads them. A refused claim — an uncatalogued field, a conditional field with no discriminant — is an outcome, not a failed upload. **PHASE 146i, 23 September 2026, closed the two things that made that outcome useless.** (a) **The two fields nobody could vouch for.** `assertClaimsWritable` keeps `grant_funder_name` and `brc_issuing_body` only on the footing that the value names an **organisation**, and nothing supplied either — so a grant funding agreement and a building regulations completion certificate had **every** claim refused, not merely that field, and could never be captured at all. A person answers it now, on the document’s own page (`src/web/document-reading.ts`, migration 043, route above), and `readAndClaim` takes an unvouched field **out of the extraction** before the claims are built, so one field nobody has vouched for no longer discards the rest. **The ingestion module’s refusal is untouched** and still fires on anything that reaches it unvouched; what reaches it is a set of claims already made writable. The field that came out is **reported, not swallowed**, and stays asked of the client (**N4**). (b) **A reading that did not work now says so on a screen** — one `document_readings` row per reading that actually started, drawn on the staff document page in words with its reason, never for the client and never a word of the document. **No row is written where the reader was not wired in or where the switch is off**, because with the switch off a finished upload must behave exactly as it did before Phase 146g, and a row is a difference. **PHASE 146j, 25 September 2026, closed the two one-way doors 146i left.** (c) **A `yes` can be taken back** — its own act, with a reason, which takes that one field out of every record it went into and keeps the answer marked withdrawn (route above). (d) **The question is asked before the first reading**, on the staff upload forms and the document request, so the first reading of a grant funding agreement can keep the funder's name; a request's answer is the same row, adopted by the document that answers it | **To switch it on**: the data protection officer approves the provider and its terms and a director signs (the Word question *“Which AI provider reads client documents, and on what terms?”*), `international_transfer_approved` on the `CLAUDE_DOCUMENTS` row of `src/connectors/sources.ts` becomes true, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` goes in the key file, and `DOCUMENT_READING` becomes `true`. **`SCANNED_PAGES` is a separate decision and a bigger one**: a picture of a page is sent *before* anything has been stripped from it |

## Not a stub, but a limitation a reader will meet and should be told about

| What | Where | Why it is not in the table above |
|---|---|---|
| **The document classifier trusts the declared type**, checked against the handled set | `src/web/uploads.ts:134` (`declaredTypeClassifier`) | **It refuses; it does not guess.** An unclassifiable upload is `E_UNRECOGNISED_DOC_TYPE`, the quarantine object is deleted and nothing is promoted. A real classifier would use more inputs; this one does not *pretend* to have used them. **Since CRM 4 of 10 the declared type is not the client's word**: it is the receipt policy of the document type they chose |
| **Fourteen of the twenty-five document types cannot be received** — the CRM specification's list, from the engagement letter to *other* | `document_types.handler_type` NULL (migration 013); `NO_RECEIPT_POLICY` in `src/web/documents.ts` | **Switched off, not stubbed.** The ingestion module refuses to store a type with no receipt policy, and writing one is the data protection officer's decision — *"Which receipt strip should each new document type carry before anyone may upload one?"* When one is written, one migration binds the type and nothing else changes. **A recommended strip for each, with its reason, is recorded and not approved** — `src/web/receipt-recommendations.ts:76` (`RECEIPT_RECOMMENDATIONS`) — and binding one also needs a policy entry of its own in the ingestion module and a rebuild of the type table |
| **Company names kept and individuals stripped on the two conveyancing uploads** — built and switched off | `src/web/conveyancing-strip.ts:64` (`CONVEYANCING_STRIP`); the capture at `:148` (`captureParties`) | **Switched off, not stubbed.** Off, the parties read off a sale contract or the pre-contract enquiry replies are captured exactly as today: every one a role token. On, a company or LLP party to the transaction keeps its name and registered number, and every individual is stripped. No screen calls it yet. It waits on *"May company names stay on conveyancing uploads if we strip the individuals?"* |
| **The per-disclosure log** — built and switched off | `src/web/disclosure-log.ts:61` (`DISCLOSURE_LOG`); recording at `:153` (`recordDisclosure`), reading at `:262` (`disclosuresFor`); the table is migration 021 | **Switched off, not stubbed.** Off, nothing is recorded and a client's reading says NOT KEPT, never none. Its recipients are those of "Who we pass client personal data on to", word for word. It has no screen; it is switched on once that list is signed off and a first real disclosure is near |
| **A data protection officer role** — defined, scoped to the compliance registers, and switched off | `src/authz.ts:1195` (`DPO_ROLE_SWITCH`); its rules at `:1200` (`DPO_RULES`), decided at `:1223` (`canAsDpo`); `src/roles.ts:88` (`DPO_ROLE`) | **Switched off, not stubbed.** It is not one of the six roles, cannot be assigned, and the database has no row for it. Whether it is switched on was Lakhbir's: *"Do we need a compliance role separate from Admin?"* **Decided 15 September 2026 (Phase 132c; Word question 44, "Do we need a separate login for data protection work?", closed): not yet** — compliance stays with Admin and the role stays off until the data protection officer is appointed and has a login (see §5.3). Switching it on is the switch, a migration adding the role row, and the role joining the six |
| **The defence pack generator** — built and switched off, Phase 132 | `src/web/defence-pack.ts:43` (`DEFENCE_PACK_GENERATOR`); built at `:115` (`generateDefencePack`); the action `defence_pack.generate` (ADMIN, SALES) in `src/authz.ts`, with no route | **Switched off, not stubbed.** Off, a request is refused before anything is read or written. On, it gathers the case stamp, the surveyor's commitment, the sources of the facts the gates read, the reconciliation, the tax adviser's sign-off, the s.203 position, the anchor log and the issued deliverables' hashes into **one manifest hashed with SHA-256**. Any missing section is named and makes the manifest INCOMPLETE. It writes one `DEFENCE_PACK_GENERATED` audit line and **issues nothing**. A fact recorded as not known and one never recorded give the same missing section (N4). It waits on the chartered tax adviser signing off the Defence Pack Template |
| **The with-and-without comparison for the allowance and profit answers** — built, run empty and switched off, Phase 132 | `src/web/allowance-profit-comparison.ts:82` (`ALLOWANCE_PROFIT_COMPARISON`) | **Switched off, not stubbed.** It runs each claim's estimate as it stands and again without `sufficient_profit` and the allowance headroom answers. It compares the **benefit** readings and the **cash** readings: the route, a gate known to be shut, and the near-term range. **It reports counts and no verdict.** A null on either side is NOT_COMPARABLE, never a zero, and "I don't know" is counted apart from never asked, read from the raw rows. **No route imports it**, and a test scans `src/` for that. On no real clients it says it is empty. It waits on real clients answering those questions |
| **A person reviewing every value from a document type** — built and switched off, Phase 132; **ON FOR FIRE RISK ASSESSMENTS since Phase 146e, 22 September 2026** | `04-phase-work/phase3/caprebate-document-pipeline.ts:544` (`PERSON_REVIEW_BY_DOC_TYPE`) | **On for one type, off for the other eight.** A fire risk assessment can hold residents' health details — who needs help to leave the building, and why — so every value read from one is checked by a person before it stands, however confident the model was (Lakhbir's instruction in the Phase 146e prompt). The other eight types still ship `false`; test M1 fails if any other is on or this one is off. Switched on for a type, a person must review every value extracted from a document of that type. **A type missing from the table handed in reads as review required**: only an explicit `false` switches review off. Which types warrant it is for whoever owns the extraction's error rate |
| **Client documents read by the Claude API, and scanned PDFs read from their page pictures** — built and switched off, Phase 146e, 22 September 2026. **Provider: Anthropic (the Claude API), chosen by Lakhbir on 22 September 2026 — AWAITING THE DATA PROTECTION OFFICER'S APPROVAL** | `src/connectors/claude.ts` — the switches, `CLAUDE_SWITCHES`, at `src/connectors/claude.ts:96`, the model, `CLAUDE_MODEL`, at `src/connectors/claude.ts:85`, one reading, `readPayloadWithClaude`, at `src/connectors/claude.ts:288`, the model for `runExtraction`, `prepareClaudeModel`, at `src/connectors/claude.ts:357`, what is kept of an answer, `toRawExtraction`, at `src/connectors/claude.ts:235`, and the scan reader, `readScannedPages`, at `src/connectors/claude.ts:414`; the SDK's only way out, `fetchThroughCallOutside`, at `src/connectors/http.ts:582` | **Switched off twice, and refused besides.** `DOCUMENT_READING` and `SCANNED_PAGES` are both `false`, and the Claude API row in `src/connectors/sources.ts` is region US with `international_transfer_approved: false`, so `callOutside` refuses every call. When on, it is the real `ModelFn`: one call per document's permitted segments, on the Anthropic SDK (`claude-opus-5`, the server-side fallback on, structured output matching `RawExtraction`), the key only from the key file, and **one `DOCUMENT_READ_BY_MODEL` audit row per call** — document type, segment ids (place, kind, page), the model that answered, tokens in and out — **never the text**. The pipeline's own boundary check runs before anything is sent, so a payload the strip did not pass is refused before the network, and the `ModelFn` answers only the payload it was prepared for. **A failure of any kind leaves every field asked (N4).** The scan path sends each page's JPEG as it is in the file and asks for its text, which then goes through the same strip; **the pictures go whole, before the strip**, which is why it has its own switch. `test/phase146e.test.ts`, all on recorded answers | Turn nothing on until the data protection officer approves Anthropic and its terms and a director signs — the Word question "Which AI provider reads client documents, and on what terms?" — and, for scans, the officer's separate answer on page pictures going before the strip. Then set `international_transfer_approved` on the row, put `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in `~/.caprebate-secrets.env`, flip the switch, and call `prepareClaudeModel` before `runExtraction` (no screen calls it yet). **Do not change any retention setting** on Lakhbir's "data is stored for 7 years" until it is confirmed whose copy that means |
| **Treating two Companies House records as the same individual** — built and switched off, Phase 132 | `04-phase-work/phase3/caprebate-companies-house-connector.ts:122` (`SAME_INDIVIDUAL`); the check, `sameIndividual`, at line 155 | **Switched off, not stubbed.** Off, nothing corroborates and every call goes to a person. The result names which of four findings it is rather than returning a bare `false`. **No live path references it**, and a test scans for that. Switching it on decides that a name plus a birth month and year is enough, and that is a data protection and tax adviser decision |
| **The backup cycle** — a named blank, not a switch, Phase 132 | `04-phase-work/phase3/caprebate-client-doc-ingestion.ts:1943` (`BACKUP_CYCLE`); `ret3DueAt` at line 1982, `recordDeletion` at 2232, `assertRestoreApproved` at 2299; the tables are migration 022 | **NOT SET**, and setting it is **the data protection officer's with the developer**. `recordDeletion` writes the cycle onto each deletion's evidence, and no caller supplies it. While the cycle is not known, backups read as still pending with no cycle and no expiry, and `ret3DueAt` is null rather than computed as though the copies had expired. A restoration is refused without the data protection officer's approval. It waits on *"Can the client-document folders be excluded from the nightly whole-site backup?"* |
| **The analytics wrapper** — built and switched off, Phase 132 | `CB_TRACK`, in the page shell of `Caprebate site.html`; its build-time check is `03-app/caprebate-analytics-allowlist-check.cjs` | **Switched off, not stubbed.** It has no measurement id, no sink and **no call site anywhere**. The check fails on any `gtag`, `dataLayer` or tag manager outside the wrapper, and on any call whose event, parameter or value the Measurement Plan's section 5 does not allow. It also fails on a post-engagement portal view that names it. It waits on the website going live |
| **One display zone, `Europe/London`, as a constant rather than a per-user setting** | `src/web/localtime.ts` (`DISPLAY_ZONE`) | The staff are in Britain and the clients own British property. It is not a stub — the conversion is real and uses the full IANA database through `Intl` — but a business with staff abroad would replace that one constant with a lookup, and no other file names a zone |
| **There is no self-serve account creation** | absent from `src/web/router.ts` | A client account is created by an ADMIN. The old wireframe's optional `JS-S8` was never built, and nothing downstream depends on it — but a real funnel needs it, and it is priced in §5 |
| **DB-W1-T4 is not run** | `test/web.test.ts` declares it | It is a demonstration against a live WordPress site — the Media Library, the REST API and the sitemap — and there is no WordPress in this build. **Declared in the test file and deliberately NOT counted as a pass** |

A stamp that could be switched off would be a record that could go missing for no reason anybody wrote down |

The delivery is the earliest issue of its client report, a named assumption that can only bring a deletion forward. It must also run from the first job, because a control arm cannot be added to jobs already shown a pre-fill. §1 UC-B7 has the rules |

UNKNOWN reads as absent, and an ambiguous window reads UNKNOWN, which the existing REPORTS and INVOICE gates refuse. | **A fixed clock for tests** | `src/server.ts:81` (`ServerOptions`); its optional `now` at `:117`, passed to `buildApp` | Tests pin it so a fixed-date seed reads the same on any day. **Unset, which is production, it behaves exactly as before** |

| **`CB_S33B` in the engine** | a new shared module of `Caprebate site.html`, exported by the package in `09-app/engine/src/generated/shared.js` | It works out s.33B aggregation. Whether it moves any money is `CB_CTA.s33b_aggregation`, one of the twenty-four switches in §6, and that is off |

## Built by Phase 132f — reading what the engine already decides, 16 September 2026

The back end had not read five engine outputs that Phase 132c and Phase 132e built. Each row below says where the
engine or the phase9 module decides the thing, and where the back end reads it. A back-end reference (`src/…`) is
checked by `03-app/caprebate-crm-fold-check.cjs` check C. **When Phase 132f wrote this table, check C read only back-end `src/` references.** The
site file, the engine package and the phase9 modules sat outside it, so their references below were checked by the
line-landing script recorded in the Phase 132f report instead. Every reference was read against the files as they
stood after the site workstream re-extracted the package (manifest `sourceSha256` 9ed2066f…). **Since Phase 132g, on 16 September 2026, they are verified by check C itself: it reads every `file:line` in this section — the back end, `Caprebate site.html`, the engine package, phase9 and phase3, including phase3's "`name` at line" form — by the same rule, and fails when the file is missing, the line is past its end, or the line does not carry the name.**

| What | Where | On, off, or decided — and what it waits on |
|---|---|---|

A discount a person enters is recorded and never applied (the Phase 132e row above) |

| **`CB_FEE.recommendationThreshold`** — the outcome `CB_FEE.display` returns on every call | the site: `Caprebate site.html:7226` (`recommendationThreshold`), exported at `:7300` (`recommendationThreshold`); the package: `09-app/engine/src/generated/shared.js:5870` (`recommendationThreshold`), exported at `:5944` (`recommendationThreshold`); its type in the back end at `src/web/engine.ts:145` (`RecommendationThreshold`); combined across the rate readings at `src/web/finance.ts:251` (`noClaim`); on every fee-against-benefit comparison at `src/web/finance.ts:175` (`recommendation_threshold`); drawn at `src/web/finance-pages.ts:407` (`noClaimBlock`) | **Always on, and it refuses nothing.** It is shown wherever the fee is shown against the benefit (an organisation's invoice and a claim's fee invoice), marked for the tax adviser and printing the figures. It is BELOW only where every reading is determined and fires. An unknown relief (a rate, pool split or entity that is "not sure" or absent) is UNDETERMINED and never fires |

With it off, London refuses with the same code, message and detail as before, and `resolveProperty` now passes the argument rather than omitting it |

The first back-end code to use a site area must go through it with `INTERNAL_CALCULATION` |

The back end quotes no fee to a client: every fee it reads is earned on a signed-off figure. So the gate is a reading there. It never withholds an invoice or moves the VAT forward look. It is right on both sides of 30 September 2026 |

## Built by Phase 132i — unknowns that relieved on routing, and answers read before they are given, 16 September 2026

Always on; no switch. No figure moves: the cash route proof run with `--moves-from "Caprebate site.html.bak-phase132i"`
lists no moved state, and every one of the 442 questions gives the same single-answer session as before.

- **N4** The heating question in supported living. A session that reaches the engine with the heating question never
  answered, or its answer cleared, is read exactly as "not sure": the central plant line and the "heating arrangement
  unconfirmed" flag go on the result, so the outcome is a referral, not an estimate. The engine writes nothing onto the
  session. No engine reads the fact, so no figure moves.
- **N4** The polluter question in private hospitals. The result's `lrr` (the land remediation panel the client sees) is
  true only on a known "yes" to derelict or contaminated land AND a known "no" to the polluter question. "Not sure", a
  cleared answer and no answer leave the panel off. The session's `lrr` still decides whether the polluter question is
  asked. No figure reads `lrr`.
- **A question never reads an answer given after it.** `CB_CTAQ.place(Q, prefix)` runs once per flow: the seller
  question (x51barred) now sits straight before the earlier-owner question (x12b), which asks the chain question
  itself, so the second asking (x51eeo, five questions) is retired; and the grant-share question (x51gtr) follows the
  grant amount. The tribunal question (x14t) is shown on the window as it stood before any tribunal application. Two
  handlers read a later answer (the fixed-value window reads the intervening owner; the event date reads the acquisition
  date): the answer wrapper records what each handler read, and when a later answer or take-back changes one of those
  facts it applies the earlier question's current answer again. One pass forward through a flow now equals two (suite AF3).
- **Back.** The renderer's Back steps over a question a later answer has hidden and is disabled when none is left
  behind. A question hidden only by its own answer (the intervening-owner question on "No") is still asked on every
  pass forward, and Back still returns to it.

## Built by Phase 132j — the polluter question in four more flows, 16 September 2026

- **The polluter question.** Care homes, dental, supported living and GP asked about derelict or contaminated land
  (x10c) and turned `lrr` on for "yes", but never asked who caused it, so the land remediation panel always showed on an
  answer that was never given. Each now asks x23 straight after x10c, built as the private hospital flow's H23: "yes"
  takes the line away and says why, "Not sure" is flagged, and `lrrFromAnswers` in each flow works out `lrr`, the
  polluter marker and the line from both current answers in any order.
- **The result's `lrr` needs a known "no"** to x23 in all five flows (N4). No figure reads `lrr`; the cash route proof
  run `--moves-from` the pre-change site lists no moved state. Suite AG asserts both N4 forms, the order grid, the
  take-back and the flow itself through the renderer.

## Built by Phase 134 and switched off — the company's name in a staff notice's subject, 18 September 2026

- **`NOTICE_SUBJECT_LEADS_WITH_COMPANY` in `src/web/automation.ts` is `true` since Phase 151** (Word answer 71: yes, staff emails name the client company). What follows is how it read while it waited: it was `false`. Lakhbir asked that every email subject
  lead with the company's name; a notice to our own staff was built by CRM 3 of 10 to carry no organisation's name. Both
  readings are built (`noticeSubject`), and `test/phase134.test.ts` proves each and that the switch is off. The question
  is "Should an email to our own staff name the client company in its subject line?" in CAPREBATE QUESTIONS. An email to
  a **client** leads with the company today (`companyFirst` in `src/web/templates.ts`, migration 032) — that was never
  in question, because the client's own name is no news to the client.

its matcher cannot see a total written with a thousands separator, nor a bare sum of
components, so the one line in this document that carries both was invisible to the
one control built to protect it. It was 1,717 before that, when Phase 113d built the

the last time somebody quoted rather than ran, the Developer Brief said 862 in
four places when the truth was over a thousand.

**TWO OF THOSE LINES WERE ALREADY WRONG WHEN PHASE 113a RE-RAN THEM ON THE SAME
DAY THEY WERE WRITTEN**, and that is worth a sentence rather than a silent
correction. `phase9` said 245 and was 314; the back end said 293 and was 310
before this phase touched it and 343 after. Both were true when the tools that
produced them last ran and neither had been re-run since. **The paragraph above
already told you to run them rather than quote them — this is what happens when
somebody does.** Phase 113a added 32: fifteen in `test/localtime.test.ts` and
seventeen in `test/portal-booking.test.ts`.

**Two things about reading that output.**
The node test runner prints its totals behind a multi-byte information glyph, not
a `#`: grep `pass [0-9]` **unanchored**, because `^# pass` and `^. pass` both
return nothing and look like a broken suite. The site harness **prints only
failures and xfails** — a section heading with nothing under it means those
checks passed.

**The two harness modes must report the same totals.** `--package` tests the
extracted engine and the default tests the site file; **the two totals matching
is the acceptance test for the extraction**, and there is deliberately no
cut-over, because a harness that only tested the package could not tell you the
package still matches the file.

**The four expected failures are correct and should stay.** One is a known
s.187A pooling defect; three are the engine consuming the residual cost model,
blocked on the VOA and cost-book licences. **If a fifth appears, you broke
something.**

## Built by Phase 151b — the screens, the wiring and the smaller decisions, 25 September 2026

Phase 151 built the engines, connectors and switches; Phase 151b built what sits on top of them. Every connector is
still switched off unless the row says otherwise; every reference is checked by check C.

The result route `POST /hooks/identity-check` believes a body only when its signature holds; a bad one is refused (401) and audited. Our own copy is the decision's fields and the body's SHA-256 — never a name, a document number or an image (a trigger refuses them). A PASS on an open check fills "How it was verified"; a person still finishes the check |

# 5 · What the developer must still do

**Mapped onto the Build Order's own blocks**, so a price can be quoted against a
structure that has been read. The blocks are A to G. **They are not the same
thing as the seven build phases** — two overlapping schemes, and mapping one onto
the other is how the last three documents went wrong.

| Block | What it is | State |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | The engine as a package | **BUILT.** `09-app/engine`, byte-for-byte, 21 verification checks |
| **B** | The funnel, `Q1`–`Q3` and the figure | **BUILT.** Six screens, the engine's figure, no external call |
| **C** | The close, the letter, the signing | **BUILT except signing itself and self-serve account creation** |
| **D** | Post-gate research, reconciliation, capture | **BUILT as a surface with no connectors behind it.** Both gates real; nothing is contacted |
| **E** | Client documents | **BUILT against the port — and, since CRM 4 of 10, run to the end on the upload route itself, with types, versions, links and requests on top.** The storage adapter for the real host is not written, and fourteen document types wait on a receipt policy — each with a recommended strip recorded, not approved |
| **F** | Staff, surveyor and operations surfaces | **BUILT.** All six roles have a surface (READ_ONLY, added by Phase 132e, reads the back office and is held by nobody) |
| **G** | The public notices page at `/notices/` | **DRAFTED, NOT HOSTED.** `03-app/caprebate-notices-page.html`, the printable complaint form beside it, and a **fourteen-line host checklist that is the actual work package**. Two statutory duties are unperformable until it is live |


## Built by Phase 151f — the online identity check is Didit's, 25 September 2026

Lakhbir switched the identity check from Veriff to Didit: 500 full checks a month free (ID document, passive liveness,
face match), then US$0.33 a check; one POST for a hosted link; a signed result; data in the EU. Everything else stays
as Phase 151b built it, and it is **still off**.

- **The call.** `POST https://verification.didit.me/v3/session/` with `x-api-key`; the body is the workflow id, our
  check's id as `vendor_data`, `language` and the two names as `expected_details` — never an email, an address or a
  company. Didit's `callback` (only where the client's browser goes afterwards) is not sent.
- **The result.** Set the webhook address in Didit's console to `<the site's address>/hooks/identity-check`. A body
  is believed only when `X-Signature-V2` (HMAC-SHA256 of the sorted, compact JSON) or `X-Signature` (of the exact bytes)
  holds with `DIDIT_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, AND `X-Timestamp` is within five minutes. Approved is PASS; Declined FAIL; In Review,
  Resubmitted and Kyc Expired REFER; Expired and Abandoned NOT_COMPLETED; Not Started, In Progress and Awaiting User are
  acknowledged and not kept; anything unknown is REFER, never PASS.
- **The keys.** `DIDIT_API_KEY`, `DIDIT_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, and `DIDIT_WORKFLOW_ID` (the check set up in Didit's console;
  not a secret) in `~/.caprebate-secrets.env`.
- **Migration 053** lets `identity_checks.provider` be `DIDIT` (and keeps `VERIFF` valid), rebuilding the table the way
  migration 007 did, and refuses Didit's names for a person or a document in our copy of a result.
- **Tests:** `test/phase151f.test.ts`, and sections 1 of `test/phase151.test.ts` and `test/phase151b.test.ts`, now in
  Didit's shapes. Run `cd 09-app/backend && node --test test/phase151f.test.ts` to count them.
- **What the developer must still do:** create the Didit account and the workflow, set the webhook address, and put the
  three entries in the live server's key file. Retention in Didit's console is the data protection officer's to set;
  we keep our own copy either way.

## Built by Phase 151e, line 1 — why every page of the test website failed, 25 September 2026

Every page of caprebate-test.vercel.app answered 500 FUNCTION_INVOCATION_FAILED from the deploy after Phase 146e
(22 September 2026). Reproduced on a clean checkout with only the repository root's dependencies installed, which is
all Vercel installs: `Cannot find package '@anthropic-ai/sdk' imported from src/connectors/claude.ts`. Phase 146e had
added the Claude API library to `09-app/backend/package.json` and not to the root `package.json`. Fixed at the root:
the library pinned at the back end's version, and `vercel.json`'s function carrying `node_modules/**` (the library
brings packages of its own). `test/phase151e.test.ts` fails if the application ever imports a package the root does not
install, pin at the same version, or carry. **If you add a dependency to the back end, add it to the root too.**
